Used in contempt of mere show and parade.
512. aṟutali makaṉukku aṅkam ellām cēṭṭi.
The son of a widow has evil gestures throughout his body.
Said of persons irreclimably and naturally vicious.
513. aṟutta kōḻi tuṭikkumāppōla.
Fluttering as a fowl with its throat cut.
514. aṟuttavaḷ āṇpiḷḷi peṟṟa kati.
The story of a widow who gave birth to a son.
515. aṟupatu nāḷikku eḻupatu kanti.
Seventy rags for sixty days.
516. aṟupattu nālu aṭik kampattiṉ mēl āṭiṉālum, kīḻē vantu tiyākam vāṅkavēṇṭum.
Although one may balance himself on a pole sixty-four feet high, he has
to descend to receive gifts.
517. aṟupatteṭṭuk kōrampalam.
Sixty-eight tricks.
Said of the various impediments which one meets with in carrying out a
scheme.
518. aṟuppuk kālattil elikkum aintu peṇcāti.
In harvest time even a rat has five wives.
519. aṟuvāykku vāy peritu, aricikkuk koti peritu.
The mouth of the rice-pot being enlarged by fracture, the rice must be
boiled the longer.
520. aṟiyil āṭi allavō ampalattil āṭa vēṇṭum?
One must appear in the assembly, must he not, after having practised
privately?
521. aṟiyil irukkiṟavarkaḷi ampalattil ēṟṟukiṟa puraṭṭaṉ.
One who leads private persons into a place of justice and perverts
judgment.
522. aṟpa āci kōṭi tavattik keṭukkum.
The least unlawful desire destroys a myriad acts of austerity.
523. aṟpattiṟku aḻaku kulikiṟatā?
Is beauty (self-respect) to be forfeited for a mere trifle ?
524. aṟpat tuṭippamāṉālum akattūciyi aṭakkum.
Although the broom is inferior it will lay the dust in the house.
525. aṟpar cinēkitam pirāṇa kaṇṭitam.
The friendship of the mean will issue in fatal results.
526. aṟpar cinēkam pirāṇa caṅkaṭam.
The friendship of the base is dangerous.
527. aṟpaṉukku aisvariyam vantāl arttarāttiriyil kuṭippiṭippāṉ.
If a low-bred man obtin wealth, he will carry an umbrella at midnight.
528. aṟpaṉ paṇam paṭittāl vikka vaki aṟiyāṉ.
When a mean person acquires wealth he knows not how to take care of it.
529. aṟṟattukku uṟṟa tāy.
Fitted to the opportunity.
530. aṉal kuḷir vetuvetuppu immūṉṟu kālamum āṟu kālattiṟkuḷ aṭakkam.
Three kinds of weather, hot, cold and temperate are included in the six
seasons.
A year is divided into six seasons of two months each, beginning with
August. The names of the seasons are 1. kār,cloudy. 2 kūtir, cold. 3.
muṉ paṉi, evening dew. 4. piṉ paṉi, morning dew. 5. iḷa vēṉil, mild heat.
6. mutuvēṉil, very hot.
531. aṉupōkam tolintāl aṟpa aviḻtamum palikkum.
Even a common medicine may prove effectual after a disease has passed
the crisis.
532. aṉpaṟṟa māmiyārukkuk kāl paṭṭālum kuṟṟam, ki paṭṭālum kuṟṟam.
The touch whether of the foot or the hand is an offence to one's unkind
mother-in-law.
533. aṉpaṟṟa māmiyārukkuk kumpiṭum kuṟṟantāṉ.
Even obeisance is an offence to an unloving mother-in-law.
534. aṉpaṟṟār vācalilē piṉpaṟṟip pōkātē.
Don't enter the portal of the uncharitable.
535. aṉpāṉa cinēkitaṉi āpattilē aṟi.
Know a loving friend in adversity.
536. aṉpillār tamakku ātikkam illi.
They who are destitute of love are void of influence.
537. aṉpuḷḷa kuṇam aliyillā nati.
A loving disposition is a river without a ripple.
538. aṉpu iruntāl ākātum ākum.
Where love reigns the impossible may be attined.
539. aṉpuṭiyāṉik koṭuttu aliyaccē acal vīṭṭukkāraṉ vantu aḻitta kati.
The story of a neighbour who invited a woman in distress at the loss of
her husband.
540. aṉpē piratāṉam atuvē vekumāṉam.
Love is all-important, and it is it's own reward.
541. aṉṟṟa āyiram poṉṉilum, niṉṟaṟa oru kācu peritu.
A single cash saved is greater than thousands of gold spent as soon as
acquired.
542. aṉṟiṟukkalām niṉṟirukkalākātu.
Pay at once, delay is bad.
543. aṉṟu kaṇṭa mēṉikku aḻivu illi.
The body we then saw has suffered no decay.
The word mēṉiis also used for beauty, freshness.
544. aṉṟum illit tiyal, iṉṟum illip pottal.
There was no stitching then, nor is there any hole now.
545. aṉṟu koḷ , niṉṟukoḷ, eṉṟum koḷḷātē.
Buy when you require, be slow to make purchases, do not make dily
purchases.
546. aṉṟu tiṉṟa cōṟu āṟu mātattiṟku āmā ?
Will the boiled rice eaten then suffice for six months ?
547. aṉṟu tiṉṟa ūṇ āṟu mācattup paciyi aṟukkum.
The food which was then eaten will keep one free from hunger for six
months.
548. aṉṟu eḻutiṉavaṉ aḻittu eḻutuvāṉā?
Will he who wrote then, erase and write agin?
Referring to the changeless preordination of God.
549. aṉṟikkuk kiṭikkiṟa āyiram poṉṉilum iṉṟikkuk kiṭitta arikkācu peritu.
The half cash obtined to-day is greater than thousands of gold to be
got hereafter.
550. aṉṟikkut tiṉkiṟa palākkāyiviṭa iṉṟikkut tiṉkiṟakaḷākkāy peritu.
The Kala fruit, carissa diffusa, of to-day is better than the jack fruit
of the future.
551. aṉṉap piṭi vellap piṭi āccutu.
A handful of boiled rice has become as precious as a handful of sugar.
552. aṉṉa mayam pirāṇa mayam.
The property of food is the support of life.
The supporting, nourishing quality of grin is its special property,
without which, as life is now conditioned, it cannot exist.
553. aṉṉa mayam iṉṟip piṉṉi mayam illi.
There is no physical virtue in aught but food.
The same as the preceding.
554. aṉṉam mikak koḷvāṉum āṭi aḻukku āvāṉum patar.
The glutton and the sloven are alike worthless.
555. aṉṉam iṭṭār vīṭṭil kaṉṉam iṭalāmā?
May we break through the wall of the house of those who fed us?
556. aṉṉam oṭuṅkiṉāl añcum oṭuṅkum.
When food fails, the five senses fail.
Referring to the exercise of the senses as dependent on the health of
the body and its dependence on food.
557. aṉṉa naṭi naṭakkap pōyt taṉ naṭiyum keṭṭāṟpōla.
As in attempting to walk like a swan, the crow lost even its natural
git.
558. aṉṉam muṭṭāṉāl ellām muṭṭu.
When food or grin is scarce, all is scarce.
559. aṉṉappālukkuc ciṅkiyaṭittavaḷ āviṉ pālukkuc carkkari tēṭukiṟāḷ.
She who was wandering about for rice water, is seeking sugar to mix in
cow's milk.
560. aṉṉatāṉattiṟkuc cari eṉṉa tāṉam irukkiṟatu?
What gift is there that equals the gift of food ?
561. aṉṉiya mātar avatikku utavār.
Strange women will not avil in adversity.
562. aṉṉikku utavātāṉ ārukku utavuvāṉ?
Whom will he help that does not help his mother?
563. asta cemmāṉam aṭimaḻikku lakṣaṇam.
A crimson sunset betokens abiding rin.
564. astimacakāntaram eṉṟatu pōl irukkiṟatu.
The difference is as great as that between an elephant and a mosquito.
565. ākaṭiyakkāraṉ pōkaṭiyāyp pōvāṉ.
A scoffer will be destroyed.
566. ākāta nāḷiyil piḷḷi piṟantāl, aṇṭi vīṭṭukkāraṉi eṉṉaceyyum?
If a child be born at an inuspicious time what evil will it occasion to
a neighbour ?
567. ākāta pañcāṅkattukku aṟupatu nāḻikiyum tiyācciyam.
In a false almanac, the sixty hours are to be rejected.
In astrological calculations a time of three and three quarters to four
Indian hours (nāḻi - 24 minutes) for the asterism that rules the day is
considered unlucky: it is called tiyācciyam rejected. An imperfect or
uncertin formula should be wholly rejected.
568. ākātavaṉ kuṭiyi aṭuttuk keṭukka vēṇṭum.
The family of the wicked must be destroyed by associating with it.
569. ākātavaṟṟi ēṟṟāl ārāyntu ēṟṟukkoḷ.
If about to undertake a difficult work do so after due inquiry.
570. ākāttiyakkāraṉukkup piramakattikkāraṉ cāṭciyā?
Is a brahmanicide a suitable witness for an abandoned wretch ?
571. ākāyattukku miyam kāṭṭukiṟatu pōla.
Like pointing out the middle of the sky. An impossibility.
572. ākāyattip paruntu eṭuttukkoṇṭu pōkumā?
Can a hawk carry away the sky?
573. ākāyattil paṟakka upatēcippēṉ, eṉṉit tūkki āṟṟukku appāl viṭu eṉkiṟāṉ
kuru.
The spiritual guide observed, I will teach you how to fly through the
air, take me up and convey me to the other side of the river.
574. ākāya valliṭi atira iṭikkum.
A powerful thunder-clap will occasion tremor.
575. ākāyamaṭṭum aḷakkum irupputtūṇic cellarikkumā?
Will white ants destroy an iron pillar that reaches to the clouds ?
The proverb was used by Sita when speaking to Ravana.
576. ākāyam pārkkappōyum iṭumuṭukkā?
Is there not sufficient space for one to go and look at the sky ?
577. ākāyatti vaṭuppaṭak kaṭikkalāmā?
Can the air be bit so as to leave a mark ?
578. ākāyam peṟṟatu, pūmi tāṅkiṉatu.
The sky brought forth, the earth supported.
579. ākiṟa kālattil aviḻtam palikkum.
If favoured by fortune medicine will take effect in due time.
580. ākuṅkālattil aṭiyāḷum peṇ peṟuvāḷ.
In times of prosperity, even a slave woman may bring forth a
female child.
581.ākuṅkāy piñcilē teriyum.
A sound fruit may be known when it begins to set.
582. ākuṅkālam ākum, pōkuṅkālam pōkum.
In auspicious times it succeeds, in inuspicious times it fails.
583. ākkap poṟuttavaṉ āṟap poṟāṉā?
Will he who waited till it was cooked, not wait till it cools?
584. ākkiṟ kuḻippēṉ, ariciyāy iṟakkuvēṉ.
Should I cook I shall spoil the rice, either by under or over doing it.
585. ākkiṉiyum ceṅkōlum aṟṟatu ari nāḻikiyil.
Rule will last but half an hour where discipline and authority do not
exist.
586. ākkukiṟavaḷ calittāl aṭuppup pāḻ, kuttukiṟavaḷ calittāl kuntāṇi pāḻ.
If the cook be weary, the hearth will be useless ; if she who pounds the
rice be weary the mortar will be useless.
587. āṅkārikaḷukku atikāri.
The chief of the proud.
588. āṅkālam ellām avacāri āṭic cāṅkālam caṅkarā caṅkarā eṉkiṟāḷ.
She exclims Sangara, Sangara at death having through life been given up
to lewd behaviour.
589. ācaritta teyvam ellām aṭiyōṭē māṇṭatu.
All the deities that were venerated have entirely perished.
590. ācāram illā acaṭaruṭaṉ kūṭip pācāṅku pēcip pati iḻantu pōṉēṉ.
By being associated with the base and by speaking hypocritically I have
forfeited my dwelling-place.
591. ācīrvātamum cāpamum aṟavōrkku illi.
The virtuous are not affected by blessing and cursing.
592. āci avaḷ mēlē ātaravu pāy mēlē.
His mind is fixed upon her, his body is on the mat.
593. āci aṟupatu nāḷ, mōkam muppatu nāḷ, toṇṇūṟu nāḷum pōṉāl tuṭippakkaṭṭi.
Sixty days of excessive desire, thirty of enjoyment; when the ninety are
over, the remining time is as useless as a worn-out broom.
594. āci aṇṭātākil, aḻukiyum aṇṭātu.
If evil desire spring not sorrow will not approach.
595. āci uṇṭāṉāl pūci uṇṭu.
If there be heartfelt love, there will be deferential regard.
Spoken of a devoted wife who has a cruel husband.
596. āci uḷḷaḷavum aliccalum uṇṭu.
As long as desire continues, there will be anxiety also.
597. āci koṇṭa pērukku rōcam illi.
Persons of inordinate desire have no shame.
598. ācikku aḷavilli.
Desire has no limits.
599. āci colli mōcam ceykiṟatā?
Is it to deceive after using enticing words ?
600. āci nōvukku aviḻtam ētu?
What remedy is there for love-sickness ?
601. āci peruka aliccalum perukum.
As desire increases, anxiety increases.
602, āci peritō , mali peritō?
Which is greater, (human) desire or a mountin?
603. ācippaṭṭatu ūcippōyiṟṟu.
What was desired is now disgusting.
604. āciyellām tīra aṭittāḷ muṟattālē.
She beat him with the winnowing fan to banish all his desires.
605. āci veṭkam aṟiyātu.
Desire knows not shame.
606. āci vittāl nācam.
Cherished desire ends in ruin.
607. ācci ācci mettap paṭittup pēcātē.
O mother, mother, do not speak so much.
ācci in 3 Provincialism for āyi.
608. āṭap pāṭat teriyātu iraṇṭu paṅku uṇṭu.
Neither able to sing nor dance, but he has two shares.
609. āṭamāṭṭāta tēvaṭiyāḷ kūṭam pōtātu eṉṟāḷ.
The temple girl who could not dance sid that the hall was not large
enough.
610. āṭaviṭṭu nāṭakam pārkkiṟatā?
Is it to look at the drama after having encouraged a profligate course?
Spoken ironically.
611. āṭā jāti kūṭājātiyā?
Are those unfit for the drama unfit for every thing.
612 āṭātu ellām āṭi avarikkāyum aṟuttāccutu.
All is done and the avari fruit is cut ?
Indicating decayed circumstances.
613. āṭi aṟaveṭṭi akavili nelvili.
July harvest having failed, the price of dry grin is that of paddy.
614. āṭi amarntatu kūttu oru nāḻikiyil.
The comedy began and ended in an hour.
615. āṭi ōynta pamparam.
A top that spins no longer.
616. āṭik karu aḻintāl maḻi kuṟintu pōm.
If the embryo clouds of July fail rin will be scant.
617. āṭikkaṟakkiṟa māṭṭi āṭik kaṟakkavēṇṭum, pāṭik kaṟakkiṟa māṭṭip pāṭik kaṟakka
vēṇṭum.
A restless cow must be milked by force, and a gentle cow must be milked
with kindness.
Treatment to be regulated by circumstances.
618. āṭikkāṟṟil utirum carukupōla.
As dry leaves falling in the winds of July.
619. āṭikkāṟṟil ecciṟ kallikku vaḻiyā?
Is there any way of escape for a leaf-plate before the wind of July?
620. āṭikkāṟṟilē ilavampañcu paṟantatu pōl.
Like silk-cotton scattered by the wind of July.
621. āṭikku aḻikkāta māmiyārit tēṭi mayirip piṭittuc ceruppāl aṭi.
Seek, seize her by the hir, and slipper a mother-in-law who does not
invite you in July.
The parents of a newly married woman separate her from her husband, that
she may be under their care during the first year of marriage, in the
month of July. This is necessary to prevent conception in that month and
delivery in the following April. The birth of a first child in April if
a son is believed to bring ruin upon a family.
622. āṭimācattil kuttiṉa kuttu āvaṇimācattil uḷivu eṭuttatām.
They say that the cuff was given in July, and the pin felt in August.
623. āṭiya kālum pāṭiya miṭaṟum.
A dancing foot and a singing throat.
624. āṭi viti tēṭippōṭu.
Provide seed-corn and sow in July.
625. āṭu irukka iṭiyaṉi viḻuṅkumā?
While there are sheep will it (the tiger or wolf) swallow the shepherd.
626. āṭu eṭutta kaḷḷaṉip pōlē viḻikkiṟāṉ.
He stares like a thief who has stolen a sheep.
627. āṭu kaṭikkiṟateṉṟu iṭiyaṉ uṟiyēṟip patuṅkukiṟāṉ.
The shepherd is trembling aloft fearing the sheep may bite him.
628. āṭu kāṟpaṇam vāl mukkāṟpaṇam.
The price of the sheep is a quarter of a fanam that of its til three
fourths of a fanam.
629. āṭu kiṭanta iṭattilē mayirtāṉum kiṭiyāmaṟ pōyiṟṟu.
Not even hir is found where sheep were penned.
630. āṭu kiṭanta iṭamum akampaṭiyāṉ irunta iṭamum uruppaṭātu.
A place where sheep were penned and a place once occupied by an
Agambadyan are profitless.
631. āṭukiṭanta iṭattil paḻupputtāṉum kiṭiyātu.
Not a leaf will be found where the sheep lay.
632. āṭu koḻukkiṟatu ellām iṭiyaṉukku lāpam.
The fattening of the sheep is an advantage to the shepherd.
633. āṭu koṇṭavaṉ āṭittirivāṉ, kōḻi koṇṭavaṉ kūvittirivāṉ. He that has
bought a sheep will wander about; he that has boughtfowls will go about
crying them for sale.
634. āṭu koṭāta iṭiyaṉ āvik koṭuppāṉā?
Will the shepherd who refused to give a sheep give a cow ?
635. āṭu kōṉāṉiṉṟit tāṉāyp pōkumā?
Will a sheep lead itself when the shepherd is absent.
636. āṭuṅkālam talikīḻāy viḻuntālum kūṭum pucipputtāṉ kūṭum.
Though he may fall headlong when dancing, he will not thereby
meet with greater success.
637. āṭuṅkālattut talikīḻāka viḻuntāl ōṭum kappariyum uṭiyavaṉ āvāṉ. If one
should fall headlong when he ought to be active, he will be so
impoverished as to possess only broken pots and an alms-dish.
Indolence in youth leads to poverty in old age.
638. āṭutavumā kurukkaḷē eṉṟāl, kompum kuḷampum taviraccamūlamum ākum eṉkiṟār.
If one say, O my teacher, can the sheep be of any use to you, he replies
yes, all except the horns and the hoofs.
639. āṭu taḻi tiṉpatupōlē.
Like a goat cropping leaves.
640. āṭu naṉikiṟateṉṟu kōṉāy aḻukiṟatām.
It is sid that the wolf wept becuse the sheep were wet.
641. āṭu niṉitta iṭattil paṭṭi pōṭukiṟatā?
Will the pen be put up where the sheep may wish ?
642. āṭu piḻittāl mayirtāṉum koṭāṉ.
If the sheep should recover, he will not give even a hir.
Said where benefits are not requited.
643. āṭu vīṭṭilē āṭṭukkuṭṭi kāṭṭilē.
The dam at home, the lamb in the jungle.
644. āṭi illātavaṉ ari maṉitaṉ.
He who is naked is but half a man.
Self-respect essential.
645. āṭiyit tiṉṟāl veṇṇey uṇṭā?
If the cream be consumed, can butter be obtined ?
646. āṭkāṭṭi contakkāraṉiyum tiruṭaṉiyum kāṭṭikkoṭukkum.
The lapwing will by it’s cries betray whether he who approaches be the
owner or a thief.
647. āṭkāṭṭi teriyāmal tiruṭappōkiṟavaṉ keṭṭikkāraṉō, avaṉ aṟiyāmal avaṉ
kālaṭipiṭittup pōkiṟavaṉ ke ṭṭikkāraṉō?
Which is the cleverer, the thief that goes out to steal taking care that
the lapwing does not see him, or he that follows the track of that thief
?
648. āṭṭāḷukku oru mōṭṭāḷ.
A stupid servant to a shepherd.
649. āṭṭil āyiram, māṭṭil āyiram, vīṭṭilē karaṇṭi pāl illi .
Possessed of sheep by the thousand, of cattle by the thousand, he has
not a spoonful of milk at home.
650. āṭṭukku vāl aḷavaṟuttu vittirukkiṟatu.
The til of the sheep is proportioned to its size.
651. āṭṭukkum māṭṭukkum iraṇṭu kompu, aiyampiṭārikku mūṉṟu kompu.
Sheep and oxen have two horns, an Aiyampidari has three.
652. āṭṭukkut tōṟkumā kiḻappu?
Can an old tiger be overcome by a sheep?
653. āṭṭukkum māṭṭukkumuṟiyā, kāṭṭukkum pāṭṭukkum variyā?
Are relationships observed among, sheep. and oxen? have woods and
uncultivated tracts any line of demarkation?
654. āṭṭukkiṭiyilē kōṉāy pukuntatupōla.
As the wolf entered the sheepfold.
655. āṭṭukkut tīrntapaṭi kuṭṭikkum ākiṟatu.
The fate of the lamb is that of its dam.
656. āṭṭuttalikku vaṇṇāṉ paṟakkiṟatu pōla.
As the washerman flies at the sheep's head.
657. āṭṭu veṇṇey āṭṭu mūḷikkum kāṇātu.
The butter the sheep yields is not enough (to stew) the marrow with.
658. āṭṭuvittup pampi koṭṭukiṟāṉ.
He cuses them to dance and beats the drum.
659. āṭṭuk kuṭṭi mēl āyiram poṉṉā?
What, thousands of gold for a young sheep?
660. āṭṭukkuṭṭikku āṉiyik kāvukoṭu.
Sacrifice an elephant for the sake of a kid.
661. āṭṭukkuṭṭiyit tōḷilē vittuk kāṭeṅkum tēṭiṉa kati.
The story of one who wandered through the jungle in search of a lamb
that he had on his shoulder.
662 āṭṭik kāṭṭi vēṅki piṭikkap pārkkiṟāṉ.
He aims to catch the leopard by exposing the sheep.
663. āṭṭit tēṭi ayalāḷ kiyiṟ koṭuppatippārkka, vīṭṭik kaṭṭi neruppu vippatu
nallatu.
It is better to build a house and set fireto it, than to buy sheep and
place them under the care of others.
664. āṭṭikkorumuṟi kāṇac cōṭṭi illiyō?
Have you no desire to see us at least once a year ?
665. āṇamum kaṟiyum aṭukkōṭē vēṇṭum.
Broth and curry ought to be prepared with all their ingredients.
666. āṇavattāl aḻiyātē.
Do not destroy yourself by pride.
667.āṇikku iṇaṅkiṉa poṉṉum māmikku iṇaṅkiṉa peṇṇum arumi. Standard gold,and
a woman at one with her mother-in-law are rare.
668. āṇukku avakēṭu ceytālum peṇṇukkup piḻikēṭu ceyyalākātu.
Although one may injurer a man; a woman may not be injuriously treated.
669.āṇi aṭittu vaḷar, peṇṇip pōṟṟi vaḷar.
Trin a boy strictly, but a girl kindly.
670. āṇiyum vēṇṭām cattiyamum vēṇṭām tuṇiyippōṭṭut tāṇṭu. Neither swearing
nor oathsare required, put down the cloth and stride over it.
671. āṇṭa poruḷi aṟiyātār ceyta tavam māṇṭa marattukku aṇitta maṇ.
The austerity of those who are ignorant of the Supreme is as profitless
as soil at the foot of a dead tree.
672. āṇṭārukkuk koṭukkiṟiyō curikkuṭukkikkuk koṭukkiṟiyō?
Do you give to mendicants, or to the hard shell of the bottle-gourd ?
673. āṇṭār irukkumaṭṭum āṭṭum kūttum.
While the head of a family lives, prosperity may be enjoyed.
674. āṇṭikaḷ kūṭi maṭam kaṭṭiṉāṟpōla.
Like mendicants combining to build a choultry.
675. āṇṭi kiṭappāṉ maṭattilē cōḷikiṭakkum teruvilē.
The mendicant lies in the choultry and his bag in the street.
676. āṇṭikku iṭac coṉṉāl tātaṉukku iṭaccollukiṟāṉ.
When told to give rice to the Saiva mendicant, he says give it to the
Vaishnava mendicant.
677. āṇṭikku vāyppēccu pārppāṉukku atuvum illi.
To the religious mendicant a word, to the brahman not even that.
678. āṇṭicci peṟṟa añcum avam.
The five children borne by the mendicant's wife are all weakling.
679. āṇṭi makaṉ āṇṭiyāṉāl nēram aṟintu caṅku ūtuvāṉ
When the son of a mendicant assumes a mendicant's profession, he will
blow his conch at the proper time.
680. āṇṭiyi aṭittāṉām kuṭuviyi uṭittāṉām.
It is sid that he beat the mendicants and broke his alms-dish.
681.āṇṭiyum tātaṉum tōṇṭiyum kayiṟum.
The Saivite and the Vaishnavite mendicants are sa a water-pot and its
cord.
United or separate.
682. āṇṭiyik kaṇṭāl liṅkaṉ eṉkiṟāṉ, tātaṉik kaṇṭāl
raṅkaṉ eṉkiṟāṉ.
When he meets a Saiva mendicant be is a Lingan, and when he meets a
Vaishnava mendicant he is a Rangan.
683. āṇṭiyum āṇṭiyum kaṭṭikkoṇṭāl cāmpalum cāmpalum oṭṭikkoḷḷum.
When two mendicants embrace each other, the ashes of the one cleave to
the other.
684. āṇṭi vēṣam pōṭṭum aliccal tīravilli.
Though he assumed the guise of a mendicant, his troubles did not cease.
685. āṇṭi kuṇṭiyit taṭṭiṉāl paṟappatu cāmpal.
If a mendicant be struck on his posteriors, ashes fly.
686. āṇṭu māṟiṉ kārum aṉṟu aṟutta campāvum āḷaṉ kaṇṇukku aritu.
Kar rice a year old, and newly reaped Samba (superior rice) are rare to
the eye of a cultivator.
687. āṇṭu maṟuttāl tōṭṭiyum kumpiṭāṉ.
If the year withhold her increase, even tho menial servants willneglect
to do obeisance.
688. āṇṭimēṟ kōpam kaṭāviṉ mēl āṟiṉāṉ.
He wreaked his anger aginst his master on the male buffalo.
689. āṇpiḷḷikaḷ āyiram ottiruppārkaḷ, akkāḷ taṅkicci ottirārkaḷ.
A thousand men may live together in harmony, whereas two women are
unable to do so although they be sisters.
690. āṇmiyaṟṟa vīraṉ āyutattiṉ mēṟ kuṟi colluvāṉ.
The coward blames his weapon.
691. ātaravaṟṟa vārttiyum āṇikiṭāvāta kimaramum palaṉ ceyyātu.
A comfortless word, and a palmira rafter without a bolt are of no use.
692. ātāyamillāta ceṭṭi āṟṟōṭē pōvāṉā?
Will a chetty (a merchant,) take his departure by a river without gin ?
693. ātāyamillāta ceṭṭi āṟṟik kaṭṭi iṟippāṉā?
Will a chetty dam up a river and drin out its waters for nothing?
694. ātīṉakkāraṉukkuc cātaṉam vēṇṭumā?
Is a man possessed of landed property without a legal instrument?
695. āttirakkāraṉukkup putti maṭṭu.
The over-hasty is wanting in sense.
696. āttirakkāraṉ kōttiram aṟivāṉā?
Will the over-hasty be nice about family pedigrees ?
697. āttirappaṭṭavaṉukku appōtu iṉpam.
The hasty is gratified at the instant.
698. ātti paṭukiṟa pāṭṭukkuḷḷē makaṉ mōrukku aḻukiṟāṉ.
While his mother is in extremity, her grown up son is crying for
butter-milk.
699. āstiyillātavaṉ arimaṉitaṉ.
Ho who possesses no property is but half a man.
700. āstiyuḷḷavaṉ āstikku aṭimi.
A man of wealth is the slave of his possessions.
701. āstiyuḷḷavaṉukku nācam illi.
The wealthy are not exposed to immediate ruin.
702. ānti ciṟitu kīccup peritu.
The owl is small, its screech is loud.
703. āpattiṟ kāttavaṉ āṇṭavaṉ āvāṉ.
He who helps another in his misfortune becomes his master.
704. āpattum campattum ārukkum uṇṭu.
Prosperity and adversity are common to all.
705. āpattukkup pāvam illi.
A thing done through necessity is no crime.
706. āpattukku utaviṉavaṉē pantu.
He is a friend who aids in adversity.
707. āppāttāḷ kalyāṇam pōyppārttāl teriyum.
The condition of one's elder sister may be ascertined by attending her
wedding.
708. āppip piṭuṅkiṉa kuraṅku nācam aṭintatupōla.
As the monkey perished by drawing out a wedge.
709. āmaṇakku vitittāl āccā muḷikkumā?
If castor seed (palma christi) be sown will achcha (ebony) spring up?
710. āmaṇakku muttu āṇimuttāmā?
Are the nuts of the castor plant pearls ?
711. āmaṇakkum paruttiyum aṭara vitippāṉā?
Will he sow Castor and cotton seeds in close proximity ?
712. āmi kiṇaṟṟilē aṇil kompilē.
The tortoise or a turtle is in the well, the squirrel is on a branch.
Said of two things or persons not found together.
713. āmi piṭippār mallāttuvār nām atu coṉṉāṟ pāvam.
They will catch the turtle, they will turn it on its back, for me to say
so would be a crime. Indicating that it is more dangerous to speak of
the crime of another than to commit it.
714. āmuṭiyāṉik koṉṟa aṟa nīli.
A most abandoned woman who murdered her own husband.
715. āmuṭiyāṉ cettapiṉpu aṟutalikkup putti vantatu.
The woman became wise after the death of her husband.
716. āmuṭiyāṉukku aḻutavaḷukku antukkaṇṇaṉ vantu vāyttāṉ.
A blear-eyed man became the husband of a woman who had beencrying for
one.
717. āmuṭiyāṉ aṭittataṟku aḻavilli, cakkaḷatti cirippāḷēṉṟu aḻukiṟēṉ.I do not
weep becuse my husband has beaten me, but becuse my rival will lugh
at me.
718. āmuṭiyāṉ cettu avatippaṭukiṟapōtu, aṇṭi vīṭṭuk kāraṉ vantu akkuḷil
kuttiṉāṉām.
When in great extremity she was mourning the death of her husband, a
neighbour came and attempted to tickle her under the arm.
719. āmuṭiyāṉ kōppillāk kūttum, kuruvillā ñāṉamum pōl irukkiṟāṉ. The
husband resembles a merry, making without food and knowledge without a
competent teacher.
720. āmuṭiyāṉ vaṭṭamāy ōṭiṉālum vācalāl varavēṇṭum.
No matter what the circumambulation of a husband may be, he entershis
house by the door.
721. āmuṭiyāṉukkup poy coṉṉālum aṭuppukkup poy collalāmā?
True, you may utter a falsehood to your husband, but can you deal
falsely with the hearth?
722. āmuṭiyāṉi vittukkoṇṭallō avacāriyāṭavēṇṭum?
It is while she has a husband, is it not, that a woman, should play the
harlot?
723. āmuṭiyāṉ cettavaḷukku maruttuvicci tayavēṉ ?
Of what use is the favor of a midwife to her who has lost her husband?
724. āmuṭiyāṉ palam iruntāl kuppi ēṟic caṇṭi ceyyalām.
If your husband be a powerful man, you may ascend the dunghill to fight.
725. āmi tiṭaril ēṟiṉāṟpōla.
As if a tortoise or turtle crept up a hillock.
726. āmiyuṭaṉē muyal muṭṭiyiṭappōyk kaṇpituṅkic cettatām.
It is sid that when the hare went with the tortoise to lay eggs it
strined its eyes out and died.
727. āya upāyam aṟintavaṉ aritalla velvatu.
It is not difficult for one to win who understands the tricks of dice.
728. āyakkāraṉukkup piramakattikkāraṉ cāṭci.
The brahmanicide is the witness of the custom officer.
729. āyakkāraṉ añcu paṇam kēṭpāṉ atāveṭṭukkāraṉ aimpatu paṇam kēṭpāṉ.
The under-hand extortioner demands fifty fanams whilst the custom
officer demands five.
730. āyattilum niyāyam vēṇṭum.
Even in gin justice is required.
731. āyattukkuk kutiri kīyattukkuk kuṭṭi.
Hire for the horse, nothing for its colt.
732. āyattukkup payantu āṟṟil nīntiṉatu pōla.
Like swimming the river to avoid the tax.
733. āyattuṟiyil aniyāyam ceyyātē.
Do not commit injustice at the custom-house.
734. āyirakkalam nellukku ōr antuppūcci pōtum.
One grin destroying insect will consume a thousand grins of rice.
735 āyiram kākkikkuḷ oru aṉṉam akappaṭṭatu pōla.
As a swan in the midst of a thousand crows.
736. āyiram kākkikku oru kalluppōla.
Like a single stone thrown at a thousand crows.
737. āyiram kaṭṭu aṇṭattit tāṅkumā ?
Can a thousand props support the universe ?
738. āyiram kaṭṭu āṉippalam.
A thousand bonds may equal the strength of an elephant.
739. āyiram coṉṉālum avacāri camucāriyākāḷ.
Though a thousand-times admonished, afaithless woman will not become a
faithful wife.
740. āyirattilē piṟantu aiññūṟṟilē kāl nīṭṭiṉatu pōla.
Like one born with a thousand stretched out this legs with five hundred.
741. āyirattil oruvaṉē alaṅkārapuruṣaṉ.
Beauty is found only in one of a thousand.
742. āyiram naṭcattiram kūṭiṉālum oru cantiraṉ ākātu.
The combined light of a thousand stars is not equal to a single moon.
743. āyiram mākāṇi aṟupattiraṇṭari .
A thousand sixteenths make sixty-two and a half.
744. āyiram uṭiyār amarntiruppār tuṇipoṟukki tontōm tontōm eṉṟu
kūttāṭuvāṉ.
He whose fortune amounts to thousands is quiet, while the rag- gatherer
leaps for joy.
Dignity is characteristic of the worthy, and levity of the worthless.
745. āyiram vantālum āyattoḻil ākātu.
Though it may bring in thousands, the occupation of a tax gatherer is
bad
746. āyiram varuṣam ceṉṟu cettālum anītaccā ākātu.
Although one may live a thousand years an unnatural death is bad.
747. āyiram vittikaḷ kaṟṟālum ulakattil āṭamparaṅkaḷ vēṇṭum.
Although one has acquired a thousand arts, he will still feel the
necessity of earthly show.
748. āyiram peyarik koṉṟavaṉ arivittiyaṉ.
He who has killed a thousand persons is half a doctor.
749. āyiram vantālum āttiram ākātu.
Though a thousand may thereby be obtined, impetuosity is not good.
750. āyiram peyar kūṭiṉālum oru antuppūcciyik kollakkūṭātu.
Though a thousand persons unite, they cannot kill a single grin insect.
751.āyiram poṉ pōṭṭu yāṉi vāṅki arippaṇattu aṅku cattukkup pāl māṟukiṟatā?
After laying out thousands for an elephant why hesitate to give half a
fanam for a goad?
752. āyiram arikkāṟpaṇam.
A thousand eighths of a fanam.
753. āyiram collukku ari eḻuttu.
Half a letter is equal to a thousand words.
754. āyiram pacukkaḷil oru pacu utittukkoṇṭāl naṣṭamā?
Will it prove a loss if one of a thousand cows kicks?
755.āyiram paṉiyuḷḷa appaṉukkup piṟantum palluk kutta oru īrkkum illi. Although
born of a father possessed of a thousand palmira trees, he has not a
fibre with which to pick his teeth.
756. āyiram pāmpiṉuḷ oru tēri akappaṭṭāṟpōla.
Like a toad among a thousand serpents.
757. āyiram poṉ peṟṟa kutirikku arippaṇattuc cavukku.
Half a fanam to buy a whip for a horse worth thousands of gold.
758. āyiram naṟkuṇam oru lōpa kuṇattāl keṭum.
A single avaricious desire will destroy a thousand good qualities
759. āyiram poṉ peṟṟa kutirikkum cavukkaṭi vēṇṭum.
Even a horse worth thousands of gold may require a whip.
760. āyiram poy collik kōyilik kaṭṭu.
Tell lies by the thousand and build a temple.
761. āyutaparīkṣi aṟintavaṉ nūṟṟil oruvaṉ.
One of a hundred makes a skilful swords-man.
762. āyutam illāri aṭikkiṟatā?
What ! strike the unarmed?
763. āyōtaṉa mukattil āyutam tēṭukiṟatu pōla.
Like seeking a weapon in the face of battle.
764. āyccalāyccalāy maḻi peykiṟatu.
It rins in successive torrents.
765. āyntu pārātāṉ kāriyam tāṉ cāntuyaram tarum.
An ill-considered undertaking may occasion the agony of death.
766. ārakkaḻutti araṇmaṉikku ākātu.
A female with an inuspicious mark on the neck will prove an evil in a
king's palace.
767. āraṭā viṭṭatu māṉiyam nāṉē viṭṭukkoṇṭēṉ.
Sirrah! who invested you with a free tenure, I did it myself.
A long continued favour is regarded as a right.
768. ārākilum paṭiyaḷantu viṭṭatā?
Has any one measured out my dily allowance ?
769. ārārukku āḷāvēṉ ākāta uṭampiyum puṇṇiyum koṇṭu.
How many shall I serve with this debilitated and ulcerated body?
770. ārāṟ keṭṭēṉ nārāṟ keṭṭēṉ.
By whom was I ruined, by twisted hemp.
771.āriyak kūttāṭiṉālum kāriyattiṟ kaṇ vēṇṭum.
Although engaged in a mere puppet-show, one ought to keep the eye on the
min chance.
772. ārukkum añcāṉ ārpaṭikkum tōlāṉ.
He is fearless and shrinks not in the presence of any foe.
773. ārukku ār catam?
Who is really true to another ?
774. ārukkup piṟantu mōrukku aḻukiṟāy?
To whom were you born? you cry for butter-milk.
775. ārukkākilum turōkam ceytāl aintāṟu nāṭpoṟuttuk kēṭkum, āttumatturōkam
ceytāl appōtē kēṭkum.
Treachery aginst any man will be slowly requited, but treachery aginst
one's self will meet with immediate retribution.
776. ārumaṟṟatē tāram ūril oruvaṉē tōḻaṉ.
A wife who has no connections is to be preferred and so the friend ship
of one.
777. ārum ārum uṟavu? tāyum piḷḷiyum uṟavu.
Who are related to each other? the mother and her child.
778. ārum illāta ūrilē acuvamēta yākam ceytāṉām.
He is sid to have performed Ashwamedha, (the sacrifice of a horse) in
an uninhabited country.
779. āri nampit tōḻā kārukku ēṟṟam pōṭṭāy?
My friend, on whom did you depend for assistance when you putup a
picotta to irrigate the kar rice ?
An efficient picotta is worked by two, frequently by three men.
780. ārō cettāṉ evaṉō aḻutāṉ.
Some one died : someone cried.
781.ār āttāḷ cettatum poḻutu viṭintāl teriyum.
In the morning it will be known whose mother is dead.
782. ār kaṭaṉ vittālum mārikaṭaṉ vikkakkūṭātu.
Whomsoever you delay to pay, the debt of Mari must be at once
discharged.
Mari is the Goddess who presides over pestilence,&c.
783. ār kuṭiyik keṭukka āṇṭivēṣam pōṭṭāy?
Whose family did you intend to ruin when you assumed the guise of a
religious mendicant ?
784. ār kuttiṉālum ariciyāṉāl cari.
No matter who pounds it if we obtin the rice.
785. ālakāla viṣam pōṉṟavaṉ antaramāvāṉ.
He who is as dangerous to society as the poison of a serpent will be
ruined.
786. ālaciyam amirtam viṣam.
Indolence changes nectar into poison.
787. ālamaram paḻuttateṉṟu paṟavikku ār cīṭṭaṉuppiṉār?
Who informed the birds that the banyan tree was in fruit ?
788. āliṉmēṟ pulluruvi.
A parasite on a banyan tree.
789.ālum vēlum pallukku uṟuti nālum iraṇṭum collukku uṟuti.
As the banyan and the acacia strengthen the teeth, so Naladiyar and
Kural give force to speech.
Naladiyar and Kural two celebrated poetie works on moral subjects.
790. ālik karumpum vēlit turumpumāṉēṉ.
I am become a bruised reed tossed on the waves.
791. āliyam aṟiyātu ōtiya vētam.
The temple understands not the hidden meaning of the Vedas.
792. āliyillā ūrilē iluppippūc carkkari.
The flowers of the olive, Bassia, are regarded as sugar in a place
without a Sugar cane mill.
793. āli viḻutu tāṅkukiṟāppōlē.
As the pendulous roots of its branches support the banyan tree.
794.āl paḻuttāl aṅkē aracu paḻuttāl iṅkē.
If the banyan be in fruit, thither, if the arasu, hither, the birds
flock.
795. āl pōl viḻuntuviṭṭu, aṟukupōl vērōṭi mūṅkil pōṟ cuṟṟam muciyāmal
vāḻntirukka.
Day you prosper as the banyan with its dughter-stems, take root like
the wide-spreading arugu grass, and as the bamboo amid unfailing
friends.
A congratulatory expression to a newly married couple.
796. āvaṇi mātam aḻukal tūṟṟal.
The month of August is attended with incessant drizzling.
797. āvatti aṭarāṉ pāvatti toṭarāṉ.
He is not subject to calamity who pursues not a sinful course.
798. āval māttiram iruntāl eṉṉa, aṉṉam iṟaṅkiṉāl allavōpiḻippāṉ?
What avils mere desire, he will live, will be not, if he swallows rice?
799. āvāri iliyum āpattukkutavum.
Even the leaves of the avári may be useful in misfortune.
800. āveṉṟu pōṉa piṟaku aḷḷi iṭukiṟatu ār ?
After his last gasp who will distribute our food ?
801. āvēṟu niṟamāṉālum, pālvēṟu niṟamāmō?
Will the colour of the skin of a cow affect that of its milk?
802. āḻamaṟiyum ōṅkil mēḷam aṟiyum aravam.
The onkil fish is acquainted with depth, and the snake knows the sound
of a drum.
This reference to the instincts of animals serves to indicate the
aptitude &c., of individuals.
803. āḻam aṟiyāmal kāli yiṭātē.
Do not step down unless you know the depth.
804. āḻākku arici mūḻākkuppāṉi mutaliyār varukiṟa vīṟāppip pārum.
With an àlak of rice in hand and a pot that will suffice to boil only
three fourths of it look at the ostentation the Mudaliar displays.
805. āḻiyellām vayalāṉāl eṉṉa avaṉi yellām aṉṉamayamāṉāl eṉṉa?
What if the great deep were an extended field and all the world standing
corn?
Even then each could enjoy only what he might consume.
806. āḷamāṭṭātavaṉukkup peṇṭēṉ ?
Of what use is a wife to him who cannot govern her?
807. āḷaṉillāt tukkam aḻutālum tīrātu.
The grief occasioned by the death of a husband will not be removed by a
flood of tears.
808. āḷaṉ illātavaḷ āṟṟu maṇalukkuc cari.
She who has no husband is like sand in the bed of a river.
Uncertin as to position.
809. āḷāṉa āṭkaḷukku aviḻ akappaṭāk kālattilē kākā picācu kañcikku aḻukiṟatu.
The raven demon crying for kanji when the well-to-do cannot obtin a
grin of boiled rice.
810. āḷukku oru kuṭṭuk kuṭṭiṉālum avaṉukkup putti varātu.
Although each of you may cuff the fellow, he will not thereby become
wiser.
811. āḷukku oru kuṭṭuk kuṭṭa aṭiyēṉ tali miṭāppōla.
Being buffeted by every one, my head has become as large as a water-pot.
812. āḷukkuḷḷē āḷāyiruppāṉ.
He may escape recognition in a crowd.
813. āḷi aṟintutāṉ aṟukkiṟāṉ.
After ascertining the character of the person he undermines his
interests.
814. āḷi āḷ aṟiyavēṇṭum mīṉip puḷiyaṅkāy aṟiyavēṇṭum.
Man must be tested by man and fish by tamarind acid.
815. āḷi āḷ kuttum āḷ miṭukkup pattup pērik kuttum.
Personal valor enables one to encounter an enemy, but outward show will
scare away ten.
816. āḷic cuṟṟippārāmal aḷakkiṟatā?
What, do you take your measure of a person without eyeing him all around
?
817. āḷip pār mukattip pār.
Look at the person, look at his face.
Spoken in derision of a boaster.
818. āḷip pārttu vāyāl ēyttāṉ.
He eyed the man and deceived him by his talk.
819. āḷ āḷik kuttum, pakaṭam pattup pērik kuttum.
One man may stab one, threatening may stab ten.
820. āḷ illāmal āyutam veṭṭumā?
Will a sword cut unwielded ?
821. āḷ irukkak koli cāyumā ?
Is he who is yet alive, murdered ?
822. āḷ illāp paṭi ampalam.
Mere weapons are ineffective.
823. āḷ iḷantali kaṇṭāl tōṇi mitakkap pāyum.
When a dhony finds her steersman unskilful she pitches.
Spoken of something going wrong from want of skill.
824. āḷ ēṟa nīr ēṟum.
As men embark, the surrounding water rises.
825. āḷ koñcamākilum āyutam miṭukku.
Though feeble in person, his weapon is powerful.
826. āḷ mettak kūṭiṉāl mīṉ mettap piṭikkalām.
When numerous fishermen combine together, multitudes of fish may be
cught.
827. āṟalla nūṟalla ākiṟatu ākaṭṭum.
Neither six nor a hundred are required, let that be which will be.
828. āṟām pēṟu peṇṇāyp piṟantāl āṟāṉa kuṭittaṉam nīṟākaviṭum.
If the sixth-born be a female, a family of overflowing wealth will be
reduced to powder.
829. āṟilum cāvu nūṟilum cāvu.
Death may occur at six, or at a hundred years of age.
830. āṟiṉa kañci paḻaṅkañci.
Cold kanji is old kanji.
831. āṟiṉa puṇṇilum acaṭu niṟkum.
There will be a scab even after the wound is healed.
lnterrupted amity though restored will occasion suspicion.
832. āṟiṉāl accilē vār āṟāviṭṭāl miṭāvilē vār.
If cool pour it into the mould, if not pour it into the large earthen
pot.
833. āṟu kaṭakkumaḷavum aṇṇaṉ tampiyeṉal, āṟu kaṭantāl nīyā nāṉār.
To avow fraternal relationship until the river is crossed; and to ignore
it on arriving at the opposite bank.
834. āṟu kaliyāṇam mūṉṟu peṇkaḷ mārōṭu mār taḷḷukiṟatu.
Where six marriages are being celebrated three women are pressing and
pushing.
835. āṟu kātam eṉkiṟapōtē kōvaṇatti aviḻppāṉēṉ?
Why loose your waist-cloth on mentioning that the river is yet ten miles
off?
836.āṟu keṭa nāṇal iṭu ūr keṭa nūli iṭu.
Plant reeds to destroy a river, to destroy a country introduce yarn.
Weavers who neglect tillage, and foul the water by dying processes &c
are here referred to.
837. āṟu koṇṭatu pāti tūṟu koṇṭatu pāti.
The river has destroyed one half and the jungle the other.
838. āṟu niṟiyac calam pōṉālum pāykiṟatu koñcam cāykiṟatu koñcam. Though the
river is in full flood part of it flows on in its course, and part
diverges.
839. āṟu niṟiyap pōṉālum nāykku nakkut taṇṇīr.
Though the river is full to overflowing, a dog laps.
Amid the greatest abundance one can only enjoy what is required
840. āṟu nīntiṉa eṉakkuk kuḷam nīntuvatu aritō?
Having swum a river, will it be difficult for me to swim over in tank?
841. āṟu nīntiṉavaṉukku vāykkāl evvaḷavu?
What is a mere channel to him who has swum a river ?
842. āṟu nērāyp pōkātu.
A river never flows stright.
843. āṟu nērāṉa ūr nili nillātu.
A town in the course of a river will not endure.
844. āṟu pārkkap pōka āyccikkup piṭittatu caluppu.
The old dame cught a cold by going to look at the river.
845. āṟu pōvatē pōkku aracaṉ colvatē tīrppu.
The flow of a river is its course, the utterance of a ruler is his
decision.
846. āṟu mācap payaṇam añci naṭantāl muṭiyumā?
Can a six months journey be accomplished if the party walk hesitatingly?
An arduous enterprize requires unremitted exertion.
847. āṟu mātam kāṭṭilē, āṟu mātam vīṭṭilē.
Six months in the jungle and six at home.
848. āṟu mātattukkuc caṉiyaṉ piṭittāṟpōla.
As Saturn seized one for six months together.
849. āṟum kaṭaṉ nūṟum kaṭaṉ perukkac cuṭeṭā paṇikāratti.
Debt in debt whether it amounts to six or a hundred; you fool(?) bake
the cakes large.
850. āṟu vaṭivilēyum karipput teḷivilēyum varuttam.
Their severity is most felt, when a flood subsides, and a famine
terminates.
851. āṟellām kaṇṇīr aṭi ellām ceṅkuruti.
All the way is bedewed with tears, and the footprints are red with
blood.
852. āṟṟit tūṟṟi ampalattil vikkap pārkkiṟāṉ.
He aims to expose me, after comforting and abusing me.
853. āṟṟilē pōkiṟa taṇṇīri appā kuṭi , aiyā kuṭi.
Drink sir, and drink the water that is flowing in the river.
Spoken of apparent generosity which in reality costs nothing.
854. āṟṟilē oru kālum cēṟṟilē oru kālumāy irukkiṟāṉ.
He has one foot in the river and one in mud.
Said of one involved in inextricable difficulty.
855. āṟṟilē pōṭṭālum aḷantu pōṭa vēṇṭum.
Although you are throwing it into the river, measure it first.
856. āṟṟilē kaṇukkāl taṇṇīrilum añci naṭappatu uṇṭu.
Though water in a river is up to one's ankle only, some will wade with
hesitation.
857. āṟṟilē ūrukiṟatu maṇalilē cuvaṟukiṟatu.
Creeping along a river and sinking in the sand.
858. āṟṟilē viṭṭa toppi pōl tavikkiṟēṉ.
I am tossed about like a tuft of sacred grass that has been thrown into
a river.
859. āṟṟil karitta puḷiyum aṅkāṭikkiṭṭa patarumāyiṟṟu.
It is the pulp of the tamarind fruit dissolved in a river, and chaff
sent to the bazaar.
Said of things useless and unaviling.
860. āṟṟiṟ peruveḷḷam nāykkeṉṉa caḷapput taṇṇīr.
The river is in flood, but what of that to a dog? it is only a fordable
stream.
861. āṟṟukkup pārppāṉ tuṇiyā, cōṟṟukkup payaṟṟaṅkāy kaṟiyā?
Will a Brahman be of any use when one crosses a river, or pulse in pod
serve for a curry?
862. āṟṟukkum payam kāṟṟukkum payam.
Both the river and weather are dreaded.
863. āṟṟukkup pōṉatum illi ceruppuk kaḻaṟṟiṉatum illi.
I neither went to the river, nor put off my shoes.
864. āṟṟupperukkum aracum ari nāḻi.
The flood of a river and the reign of a king last but half an hour.
865. āṟṟu maṇali aḷaviṭakkūṭātu.
The sands of a river cannot be counted.
866. āṟṟu nīri nāy nakkik kuṭikkumō eṭuttuk kuṭikkumō?
How does a dog drink the water of a river, by lapping or by lading?
867. āṟṟik kaṭantallō akkari ēṟa vēṇṭum?
Before ascending its opposite bank it is necessary first, is it not to
cross the river?
868. āṟṟik kaṭantāl ōṭakkāraṉukku oru coṭṭu.
After crossing the river the boatman gets a cuff.
869. āṟṟik kaṭattiviṭu ākācattiṟ paṟakkak kuḷiki tarukiṟēṉ
eṉkiṟāṉ.
He says, carry me over the river and I will give you a pill that
willenable you to fly through the air.
870. āṉatallāmal āvataṟivārō?
Can one comprehend the future as well as the past?
871. āṉa teyvatti āṟu koṇṭupōkiṟatu aṉumantarāyaṉukkut teppattiruviḻāvā?
After the river has carried away every imaginable deity, do you stay to
celebrate an aquatic festival in honour of Hanuman?
Said of one seeking the lesser aid when the greater has failed.
872. āṉamaṭṭum ātāḷiyaṭittuppōṭṭu, ānti pōl viḻikkiṟāṉ.
Having spent all he stares like an owl.
873. āṉa mutali aḻittavaṉ māṉam iḻappatu aritalla.
Having lost his capital, it will not be difficult for him to lose his
reputation.
874. āṉavaṉ ākātavaṉ ellāttilum uṇṭu.
The good and the bad exist among all.
875. āṉāl accilē vārkkiṟēṉ ākāviṭṭāl miṭāvilē vārkkiṟēṉ.
If I succeed I will pour it into a mould, if not, into a large pot.
Accommodating one's conduct to circumstances.
876. āṉi aṭiyiṭātē kūṉi kuṭipōkātē.
Do not begin to build in June; nor set out to occupy a house in March.
877. āṉi ari āṟu, āvaṇi muḻu āṟu.
In June half a river, in August a full river.
878.āṉi āṉi vālotta karumpu.
In June sugar-cane is like an elephant's til.
879. āṉi acintu tiṉṉum vīṭu aciyāmal tiṉṉum.
An elephant moves when eating, a house eats without moving.
880. āṉi ācāravācalik kākkum, pūṉi puḻutta mīṉik kākkum.
An elephant waits before the outer hall of a king’scourt; a cat watches
the putrid fish.
881. āṉi āyiram peṟṟāl aṭiyum āyiram peṟumā?
If an elephant fetch a thousand, will the mark of his footstep be worth
so much?
882. āṉi iruntu aracāṇṭa iṭattilē pūṉi iruntu pulampi aḻukiṟatu.
A cat sits and mews in the place once occupied by the royal elephant.
883. āṉi ilikkaṟi pūṉi porikkaṟi.
An ordinary curry for an elephant and a superior curry for a cat.
884. āṉi uṇṭa viḷāṅkaṉi pōla.
Like a blighted wood-apple swallowed by an elephant.
885. āṉi ēṟiyum cantu vaḻi nuḻivāṉēṉ?
Why enter by a corner when mounted on an elephant ?
886. āṉi ēṟit tiṭṭivāyil nuḻivārā?
Will one mounted on an elephant enter through a wicket?
887. āṉi oru kuṭṭipōṭṭum palaṉ, paṉṟi pala kuṭṭi pōṭṭum payaṉ illi.
It is of value though an elephant brings forth a single young one but
the many young ones of a pig are of no value.
One good thing is better than ten bad ones.
888. āṉi kaṭṭat tūr vāṉamuṭṭap pōr.
The stalks of the grin are so strong that an elephant may be tied to
them, and the stacks are as high as the heavens.
Said in exaggeration of an abundant harvest.
889. āṉi kaṇṭa piṟavikkuruṭar aṭittukkoḷḷukiṟatu pōl.
As the blind quarrelled about an elephant they had examined.
890. āṉi kaṟuttiruntum āyiram poṉ peṟum.
Though black, an elephant will fetch a thousand gold pieces.
891. āṉi kāṇāmaṟpōṉāṟ kuṇṭuc caṭṭiyil tēṭiṉāl akappaṭumā ?
If an elephant be lost, is it to be sought in an earthen pot ?
892. āṉi kuṇṭuc caṭṭiyiṉum kuḻiciyiṉum uṇṭō ?
Is the elephant in the rice-pot or in the Water-pot ?
893. āṉi kēṭṭa vāyāl, āṭṭuk kuṭṭi kēṭkiṟatā ?
What, does the mouth that asked for an elephant ask for a lamb ?
894. āṉi koṭuttum aṅkucattiṟkup piṇakkā ?
Having given the elephant is there a dispute about the goad ?
895. āṉi koṭiṟṟil aṭakkukiṟatu pōla enta maṭṭum aṭakkukiṟatu ?
How long shall I conceal it as an elephant incloses a thing in his jaws
?
896. āṉik kavaṭum pūṉit tiruṭum.
The deceit of an elephant and the thievishness of a cat.
897. āṉikku aṟupatu muḻam aṟakkuḷḷaṉukku eḻupatu muḻam.
Keep away from an elephant sixty cubits, and from a dwarf seventy
cubits.
898. āṉikkum pāṉikkum cari.
The same reason is applicable alike to elephants and earthen pots.
899.āṉikkum pulikkum neruppik kaṇṭāl payam.
The elephant and tiger are afrid of fire.
900. āṉikkum aṭi caṟukkum.
Even an elephant may slip.
901. āṉikkut tīṉiyiṭum vīṭṭil, āṭṭukkuṭṭikkup pañcamā?
Will a lamb be famished in a place where elephants are fed ?
902. āṉik kūṭṭattiṟ ciṅkam pukuntatupōla.
As if a lion had entered a herd of elephants.
903. āṉikkut tēri ūṇā?
Are toads fit for elephant's food ?
904. āṉikkut tuṟaṭum aṉṉattukku miḷakāyum vēṇṭum.
An elephant requires a goad, and boiled rice a chilli (condiment.)
905. āṉikku illi kāṉalum maḻiyum.
An elephant is not affected by sunshine or rin.
906. āṉikku oru kālam pūṉikku oru kālam.
The elephant has its time, the cat also has its time.
907. āṉi cettālum āyiram poṉ.
Although dead, an elephant will fetch a thousand gold pieces.
908. āṉi taḻuviya kiyālē āṭu taḻuvukiṟatō?
What! does the hand that has caressed an elephant caress a sheep?
909. āṉi taṉ taliyil tāṉē maṇṇip pōṭṭukkoḷḷum.
The elephant will put sand on its own head.
To see this done by wild elephants when travelling through a jungle is
very interesting.
910. āṉi taṉṉik kaṭṭac caṅkiliyit tāṉē koṭuttatu pōla.
As an elephant gives the chin to tether himself.
Said of one who, whether consciously or not, supplies means to his own
disadvantage.
911. āṉi turattivantālum ālayattil nuḻiyalākātu.
Though chased by an elephant, it is not right to enter a temple.
912. āṉi niḻalip pārkkat tavaḷi kalaṅkiṉāṟpōlē.
As a frog trembled at the shadow of an elephant.
913. āṉi paṭuttāl āḷmaṭṭam.
When an elephant lies down its height will equal the stature of a man.
914. āṉi paṭuttāl āṭṭukkuṭṭikkut tāḻumā?
When an elephant lies down, will he be lower than a lamb ?
915. āṉi pārkka veḷḷeḻuttā?
Are your eyes too dim to see an elephant ?
You must be blind indeed.
916. āṉi pōṉa vīti
The street by which an elephant passed.
917. āṉi mitikkap piḻippārkaḷā?
Has any survived after being trodden under foot by an elephant ?
918. āṉi muṭṭat tāḷ vāṉamuṭṭap pōr.
Stubble as high as an elephant, stacks towering to the skies.
919. āṉi mēyum kāṭṭil āṭṭukkuṭṭi mēya iṭam illiyā?
Is there not room for a lamb to crop its food in a jungle where
elephants feed?
920. āṉi mēlē pōkiṟavaṉic cuṇṇāmpu kēṭṭāṟpōla.
Like asking chunam (lime) of one who is going along on an elephant.
921. āṉimēl iṭṭa pāram pūṉimēl iṭṭāṟpōla.
As if an elephant's load was transferred to a cat.
922. āṉiyum aṟukampulliṉāl taṭipaṭum.
Even an elephant may be impeded by arugu, Agrostis linearis, grass.
This grass is sacred to Ganesa.
923. āṉiyum āṉiyum muṭṭumpōtu iṭiyil akappaṭṭa kocukupōla.
As a gnat between two elephants that are brought into collision.
924. āṉiyi vittuvāṉukkum pūṉiyik kuṟavaṉukkum koṭu.
Give an elephant to a pundit, and a cat to a mountineer.
925. āṉiyik koṉṟavaṉ pūṉiyi vellamāṭṭāṉā?
Is he who slew an elephant unable to overcome a cat ?
926. āṉiyi āyiram poṉṉukku vāṅki iruppu aṅkucattiṟku ēmāntu niṟpāṉēṉ?
After having pid a thousand gold pieces for an elephant, why hesitate
to buy an iron goad?
927. āṉiyik kuttic cuḷakālē mūṭuvāḷ.
She will stab the elephant and cover it with a sieve.
928. āṉiyik kaṭṭic cuḷakālē maṟippāḷ.
Having tied the elephant she will cover it with a winnowing fan.
929. āṉiyittēṭak kuṭattukkuḷ ki vittatu pōla.
Like putting one's hand into a water-pot in search of a missing
elephant.
930. āṉiyi viṟṟut tuṟaṭṭikku maṉṟāṭukiṟāṉ.
Having sold the elephant he begs for the goad.
931. āṉiyit taṇṇīril iḻukkiṟa mutali, pūṉiyit tariyil iḻukkumā?
Can an alligator which can draw an elephant in water, drag a cat on dry
ground ?
932. āṉi varum piṉṉē maṇi ōci varum muṉṉē.
The sound of the bell is heard before the elephant makes its appearance.
Spoken of events which cast their shadows before.
933. āṉi vāl piṭittuk kariyēṟalām āṭṭukkuṭṭiyiṉ vāl piṭittuk kariyēṟalāmā?
One can cross a river by holding the til of an elephant, can one do so
by holding the til of a sheep?
934. āṉi viḻuntāl kompu, puli viḻuntāl tōl.
On the death of an elephant the tusk remins, on the death of a tiger
the skin.
935. āṉi viḻuṅkiya ammiyārukkup pūṉi cuṇṭāṅki.
A cat will be a small thing to an old dame who swallowed an elephant.
936. āṉi viḻuntāṟ kutiri maṭṭam.
When down, an elephant is as high as a horse.
937. āṉi vēkam aṭaṅkum tuṟaṭṭiyāl.
The force of an elephant is subdued by the goad.
938. ikaḻcciyuṭiyōṉ pukaḻcciyaṭiyāṉ.
The contemptible will not be prised.
939. ikkariyil pākalip pōṭṭu akkariyil koḻukompu naṭukiṟāṉ.
He puts down the seed pagal (a creeper) on this side of the river and
erects a supporting pole on that.
940. iṅku irunta pāṇṭam pōla.
Like a vessel that contined assafoetida.
941. iccitta kāriyam irakaciyam allavē.
A thing that is greatly desired is not secret.
942. iñci tiṉṟa kuraṅkupōla.
Like a monkey that has eaten green ginger.
943.iṭakkaṉukku vaḻi eṅkē? kiṭakkiṟavaṉ talimēlē.
Where is the way of the perverse ? on the head of the man that is lying
down.
944. iṭaṅkoṭuttāl maṭam piṭuṅkuvāṉ.
If permitted he will pull down the choultry.
Intimating what persons of violent tendencies must be at once repressed.
945. iṭatukikku valatuki tuṇi valatukikku iṭatuki tuṇi.
The right hand helps the left and the left the right.
946. iṭam valam teriyātavaṉōṭu iṇakkam paṇṇakkūṭātu.
One may not make friendship with him who does not know the right side
from the left.
Intimating that one must have nothing to do with fools.
947. iṭi ōci kēṭṭa pāmpu pōla.
Like a snake that has heard thunder.
948. iṭikkuk kuṭi piṭikkalāmā?
Will an umbrella be of any use in a thunder-storm ?
949. iṭukiṟa teyvam eṅkum iṭum.
A liberal deity will give every-where.
950. iṭuppu vitta iṭamellām aṭuppu vittāṉ.
In every place on which he reclined he constructed a hearth.
Spoken of one who takes care of himself.
951. iṭuppu oṭinta kōḻikku uraṟ kuḻiyē kati.
The mortar hole is the asylum of the fowl with a broken back.
952.iṭumpu ceyvārkku irāp pakal nittiri illi.
Oppressors sleepnot day or night
953. iṭuvatu picci peṟuvatu mōkṣam.
Alms-giving secures heavenly bliss.
954. iṭuvār picciyik keṭuvār keṭuppār.
The evil-disposed will destroy the good deeds of the beneficent.
955.iṭuvāḷ iṭuvāḷeṉṟu ēkkaṟṟu iruntāḷām, nāḻi nelluk koṭuttu nālāciyum
tīrttāḷām.
It is sid that she waited patiently for her mistress assured she would
be rewarded; and at length she got ameasure of rice, and her four
desires were met.
956. iṭikkaṇakkaṉ cettāṉ, iṉip piḻippāṉ nāṭṭāṉ.
The curnam of the shepherd caste is dead, henceforth the peasant may
prosper.
957. iṭi cāynta kuṭam kiṭi.
The water-pot remins aslant as when put down.
958. iṭitteruvil kōlam varumpōtu kucatteru eṅkē eṉkiṟāḷ?
When the marriage procession comes along the shepherd's street she asks
where the potter's street is.
959. iṭippiṟappum kaṭippiṟappum ākātu.
Middle and lower ranks in life are not good.
960. iṭip putti piṭariyilē.
The sense of the shepherd is in his neck.
961. iṭiyaṉ keṭuttatu pāti maṭiyaṉ keṭuttatu pāti.
The shepherd destroyed half, and the simpleton destroyed half.
962. iṭiyaṉ kaliyāṇam viṭiyum poḻutu.
A shepherd's marriage takes place at day-break.
963. iṭiyāṇṭiyum kucattātaṉum illi.
Among shepherds there are no Saiva mendicants, among potters there are
no Vaishnava mendicants.
964. iṭiyūṟu ceytōṉ maṉiyil irukkalātu pēy mutalāy ?
Even a demon will not hunt the dwelling of him, that defeats the hopes
of another.
965. iṭṭa eḻuttiṟku ēṟa ācip paṭṭāl kiṭikkumā?
If one's desires are in excess of destiny, will they be obtined?
966. iṭṭataṉ mēl ēṟācippaṭukiṟatā?
Is it fit to wish for more than what is already given?
967. iṭṭavaṉ iṭāviṭṭāl veṭṭuppaki.
If the giver cease to give, mortal hatred will ensue.
968. iṭṭārkku iṭu, cettārkku aḻu.
Give to those who have given, weep for the dead.
969. iṭṭu vittāl tiṉṉavum eṭuttu vittāl cumakkavum teriyum.
He knows how to eat if food be set before him, and he knows how to carry
if the burden be lid upon him.
970. iṇaṅkārōṭu iṇaṅkuvatu ikaḻcci.
To associate with the obstinate will bring disgrace.
971. itu eṉṉa veḷḷarikkāy viṟṟa paṭṭaṇamā ?
What! is this a town in which water-melons were sold ?
972. ittaṉi periyavaṉ kiyip piṭittāl eppaṭi māṭṭōm eṉkiṟatu ?
When so great a personage takes one by the hand, how can one say nay ?
973. ittaṉi attaṉi āṉāl attaṉi ettaṉi ākum?
If this amounts to that how much will that come to ?
974. inta aṭikku enta nāyum cākum.
Such a blow would kill any dog.
975. inta elumpik kaṭippāṉēṉ contappallup pōvāṉēṉ?
Why gnaw this bone, and why lose one's own teeth?
976. intak kūḻukkō ittaṉi tirunāmam?
Is it for this gruel that so many sacred namams are used?
977. intak kūḻukkā irupatteṭṭu nāmam ?
What, is it for this gruel that I have made twenty-eight námam sectarial
marks.
978. intakkarippiṟ cettāl, iṉṉam oru karippu mayirip piṭuṅkumā?
If one die during this famine will another famine pull off his hir?
979. intap pūṉiyum pāl kuṭikkumā eṉṟāṟpōl irukkiṟāṉ?
He is as if one sid of a cat, will this also drink milk?
980. intap perumiyum pantal aḻakum pārttiyā paṇṇikkārā ?
O, headman, hast thou noticed this greatness, and the beauty of the
Pandal?
981. inta nāyi ēṉ ippaṭic ceykiṟāy?
Why do you treat this dog in this way?
982. intiraṉic cantiraṉi iliyālē maṟippāḷ, ematarumarājaṉik kiyālē maṟippāḷ.
She will conceal Jupiter and the moon with a leaf, and Yama, the regent
of the dead, with her hand,
983. imik kuṟṟam kaṇṇukkut teriyātu.
The defects in the eye-lashes are not apparent to the eye.
984. iyamaṉ oruvaṉik kolvāṉ, ēṟṟam mūvarik kollum.
Yama the regent of the dead, kills one at a time, a picotta may kill
three at once.
985. iyaṟki vācaṉiyō ceyaṟki vācaṉiyō?
Is the habit natural or acquired?
986. irakkam illātāṉ neñcam irumpilum kaṭitu.
The heart of one void of compassion is harder than iron.
987. irakkap pōṉālum ciṟakkap pōkavēṇṭum.
Though yougobegging, go decently attired.
988. iraṅkātavar uṇṭā ? peṇ eṉṟāl pēyum iraṅkum.
Are there those who have no pity ? If the word woman be uttered even a
demon will be moved with compassion.
989. iraṇṭu āṭṭilē ūṭṭa viṭṭa kuṭṭipōla.
Likea lamb allowed to suck two sheep.
Spoken of a youth full of strength and activity.
990. iraṇṭu paṭṭa ūril kuraṅkum kuṭi irātu.
Even a monkey will not stay in a village divided aginst itself.
991. iraṇṭu tōṇiyil kāl vikkiṟatā ?
Is it to plant your feet on two dhonies?
992. iraṇṭu peṇṭāṭṭikkāraṉ vīṭṭil neruppēṉ?
Why fire in the house of a man that has two wives?
993. iraṇṭu vīṭṭilum kaliyāṇam iṭiyilē cettatām nāykkuṭṭi.
It is sid that the pup died between two houses where marriage
festivities were going on.
994. iraṇṭu nāykku oru elumpu pōṭṭatu pōla.
As a bone thrown to two dogs.
995. irantum parattukku iṭu.
Though you have to beg them, make your offerings to God.
996.irantōrkku īvatu uṭiyār kaṭaṉ.
It is the duty of the richto give alms to beggars.
997.irappavaṉukku veṇcōṟu pañcamā?
Is it difficult for a beggar to get fine rice?
998. irappavaṉukku eṅkum pañcam illi.
A beggarnowhere suffers from famine.
999. iraval uṭimi icivāy irukkiṟatu, eṉ piḷḷi āṇi nāṉ koṭukkamāṭṭēṉ.
The thing borrowed suits; I swear by my child that I will not return it.
1000. iraval catamā , tiruṭaṉ uṟavā?
Is a thing borrowed a durable possession, is a thief a friend?
1001. iravaṟ cōṟu pañcam tāṅkumā?
Will borrowed rice prove a security aginst famine?
1002. iravaṟ cīliyi nampi iṭuppuk kantiyi eṟintāḷām.
It is sid, that relying on a borrowed cloth, she throw away her
tattered cloth.
1003. irāku ticiyil vāḻntavaṉum illi, rāja ticiyiṟ keṭṭavaṉum illi. None ever
prospered under Rahu, nor was any one ever ruined under Jupiter.
Rahuis the name of a monstrous serpent ordragon, the ascendingnode, and
regarded as one of the nine planets, whichwith Kethu the descending
node, is sid to grasp the sun and moon periodically, and thus to cuse
eclipses. When the supernals assembled at a feast to drink the water of
immortality obtined from the churning of the milky ocean Rahu and
Kethu, two Asuras, clandestinely got admission. The sun and moon saw
them and at once informed Vishnu of their presence. Vishnu dispatched
his marvellous disc and decapitated them just as the coveted nectar was
about to enter their throats. They to avenge themselves resolved
periodically to swallow the sun and moon.
1004. irāk kaṇṭa caṉi miṭāppōla vīṅkiṉa kati.
The story of one who in a dream of the night saw Saturn swelling as a
large pot.
1005. irācaṉ ēṟiya kutiriyippōl.
Like a horse ridden by a king.
1006.irācamukattukku elumiccam paḻam pōla.
As a lemon offered to a king.
A gift to procure his favour: a hindu when offering a lemon or fruit of
any kind invariably does so with the right hand.
1007. irāca kīriyik kāvukiṟatupōla.
Like carrying a royal mongoose.
1008. irācaṉ āṉālum taṉ tāykku makaṉē.
Though a king, he is the son of his mother.
1009. irācaṉ ceṅkōl taṉ nāṭuvariyil.
A king's sceptre extends to the limit of his territory.
1010. irācā makaḷ āṉālum koṇṭavaṉukkup peṇṭutāṉ.
Though a princess, she is the wife of the man who has taken her in
marriage.
1011. irācāḷiyik kaṇṭa kokkuppōla.
Like a heron which saw a falcon.
1012. irācā kaṭaṉpaṭap puḷukki kāṭittaṇṇīr kuṭittatupōla.
As a slave lived on gruel becuse the king was in debt.
1013. irāppaṭṭiṉi kiṭantavaṉ uritta vāḻippaḻam viṟkiṟatā eṉṟu vicārittāṉām.
It is sid that he who starved the whole night inquired if peeled
plantins could be obtined.
1014. irāppaṭṭiṉi kiṭappavaṉ akavili kēṭpāṉō?
Does be who lies down at night fasting, enquire after the price of
grin?
1015. irāppaṭṭiṉi pāyōṭē.
The sense of hunger ends on the mat.
1016. irāppakal kaṇṇilē.
In the eye night and day.
1017. irāmar irunta iṭam ayōtti.
Ayodhya was the city of Rama.
1018. irāmaṉ maṅkiyōṭu iṇaṅkiyatu avam.
Acquiescence with the wishes of his wife was fatal to Rama.
1019. irāmaṉippōla rājā iruntāl aṉumāṉip pōlac cēvakaṉum iruppāṉ.
If the king be equal to Rama, his servant may be equal to Hanuman.
Hanuman was the monkey chief, ally of Rama in his expedition aginst
Lanka.
1020. irā muḻutum rāmāyaṇam kēṭṭuc cītikku rāmaṉ eṉṉa eṉṟa kati.
The story of one who having listened to the reading of the Ramayana a
whole night, asked what relationship existed between Sita and Rama.
1021. irāmēcurattukkup pōyum caṉīcuraṉ toliyavilli.
Though he went on a pilgrimage to Rameswaram, Saturn has not
left him.
1022. irāvaṇaṉ kuṭikku makōtaraṉ pōlum, cuyōtaṉaṉ kuṭikkuc cakuṉipōlum. He is
what Mahodharan was, to the family of Ravana and what
Sakuni was to Suyodhana.
Said of one who under professions of friendship secretly plotsone's
ruin.
1023. irāvaṇa canniyācipōl irukkiṟāṉ.
He is as Ravana in the guise of a hermit.
1024. irāvuttaṉit taḷḷiṉatum allāmal kuḻiyum paṟittatām kutiri.
It is sid that the horse not only threw his rider, but dug his
gravealso.
1025. irukkiṟa aṉṟikku erumimāṭu tiṉṟāṟpōla.
Like a buffalo he consumes the day's supply.
1026. irukkiṟavaṉ cevviyāy iruntāl cirikkiṟavaṉ cevviyāyc cirippāṉ.
If he who is being shaved sits properly, the barber will shave well.
1027. irukka vēṇṭum eṉṟāl irumpit tiṉ.
If you wish to live long, eat iron.
1028. irukka irukka ellām icivākum.
In time all will be in harmony.
1029. irukkum iṭam ēvumā ?
Will the place on which we sit, incite ?
1030. iruci uṭimi irāttaṅkal ākātu.
Oblations made to a demoness must not be kept till the morning.
1031. iru cuḻi, iruntu uṇṭālum uṇṇum, irantu uṇṭālum uṇṇum.
He who has a double twirl of hir, may live either on his own resources
or by begging.
1032. iruṭṭu vēliyō kuruṭṭu vēliyō?
Was the work done in the dark or by the blind ?
1033. iruṭṭu vīṭṭukkuḷ pōṉāl tiruṭṭukki niṟkumā?
Will his thievish hand be restrined when he enters a dark room?
1034. iruṭṭuk kuṭivāḻkkit tiruṭṭukku aṭiyāḷam.
It is a sign of thievishness for a family to live in obscurity.
1035. irutali maṇiyaṉ pāmpippōla.
Like a snake that has a head at both ends.
1036. irutalikkoḷḷi eṟumpu pōl āṉēṉ.
I have become like an apt on a fire-brand lighted at both ends.
Spoken when in danger from opposite quarters.
1037. iruntallō paṭukkavēṇṭum?
Must one not sit before lying down?
1038. irunta nāḷ ellām iruntuviṭṭu ūrppaṟiyaṉukkut tārivārtta kati. The story
of one who after leading an unexceptionable course of life gave his
dughter in marriage to the village Pariah.
1039. iruntavaṉ talimēlē iṭi viḻuntatu pōla.
As the thunder-bolt fell on the head of the one who remined.
1040. irunta kāl mūtēvi naṭanta kāl cītēvi.
The sluggish foot is Mudevi the goddess of adversity, the active footis
Shridevi the goddess of prosperity.
These two personages were produced when the milky ocean was churned to
obtin ambrosia. They play an important part in the economy of the
Hindus.
1041. iruntavaṉukkup pōṉavaṉ kuṇam.
He that went away was better than he who stayed.
1042. iruntavaṉ eḻuntirukkiṟataṟkuḷḷē niṉṟavaṉ oru kātam pōvāṉ.
Whilst he who was seated was rising, the man who was standing had gone
ten miles.
1043. iruntāl pūṉi, pāyntāl puli.
While squatting, a cat, when springing, a tiger.
1044. iruntāl iruppīr, eḻuntiruntāl niṟpīr.
If sitting you sit, if you rise you stand.
1045. iruntu paṇam koṭuttu naṭantu vāṅkavēṇṭiyatāy irukkiṟatu.
One must walk a long way to get back the money lent when seated.
1046. irupattoru maḻiyum eṇṇi ūṟṟiyatu.
The twenty-one kinds of rin were enumerated and poured out.
1047. iruppuk katavu iṭittut taviṭṭuk koḻukkaṭṭi eṭukkiṟatā?
What! knocking down an iron door to obtin a bran cake?
1048. iruppuc calākiyi viḻuṅkippōṭṭu iñciccāṟu kuṭittāl tīrumā?
If one has swallowed a bar of iron, will its effects be removed by
drinking a decoction of ginger?
1049. iruppuk kōṭṭiyum kaṟkatavum pōla.
Like an iron fort and a stone door.
1050. iru maṉatu maṅkiyōṭu iṇaṅkuvatu avam.
To associate with a double minded woman is fatal.
1051. irumpu cempāṉāl turumpum tūṇām.
If iron becomes copper, a straw may become a pillar.
One impossibility is as like as another.
1052. irumputtūṇic cel arikkumā?
Can white-ants eat an iron pillar ?
1053. irumpu cempāṉāl tirumpip poṉ ākum.
If iron can be converted into copper, Copper may be reconverted into
gold.
1054. irumpukkaṭṭiyik kāṟṟaṭikkiṟapōtu ilavam pañcu eṉakku eṉṉa putti eṉkiṟatām.
It is sid that when the wind was driving along a piece of iron, the
silk-cotton asked what was best for him to do.
1055. irumpukkaṭṭi paṟakkiṟapōtu ilavampañcukku iruppiṭameṅkē ?
When a piece of iron is flying away, where will silk-cotton find a
resting place ?
1056. irumpu aṭikkiṟa iṭattil nāykku eṉṉa vēli?
What has a dog to do in a smith's shop?
1057. irumpu piṭitta kiyum ciraṅku piṭitta kiyum cummā irātu.
The hand that has taken up an iron tool and the hand affected by the
itch will not be still.
1058. irumpu aṭikkiṟa iṭattil nāykku eṉṉa kiṭikkum?
What will a dog get in a smith's shop?
1059. irumputtūṇi eṟumpu arittāṟpōla.
As if an ant had gnawed an iron pillar.
1060. irumpu tuṟaṭṭukku aciyāta puḷiyaṅkāy tiruppāṭṭukku aciyumā?
Will the tamarind fruit that has resisted an iron hook shake at a sacred
song.
In illustration of this proverb the following may not be considered
irrelevant. In the year 1826 when I went to my first Missionary station,
Trincomalee on the Eastern coast of Ceylon, I called on the chief
Mudaliar to pay my respects and to urge the clims of Christianity. The
old gentleman who remembered the coercive system adopted by the Dutch
for the propagation of Christianity, and well knowing too that
Missionaries under the wild sway of Great Britin can use no weapon but
the Gospel, uttered with great animation the above proverb. In view of
the youthful character of his visitor he seemed to feel like Goliath in
the presence of David.
1061. irumpi eṟumpu arikkumā ?
Can an ant gnaw iron ?
1062. irumpi eli tiṉṉumā?
Can a rat eat iron ?
1063.irumpi eli kavviṟṟu eṉkiṟāṉ paṭukkāḷi.
The infamous liar says that the rat took the iron in its mouth.
1064. irumpik kaṟiyāṉ arittāl piḷḷiyip paruntu koṇṭu pōkātā?
If the white-ants can eat iron, why cannot a hawk carry off a child ?
1065. iruvarum ottāl iṇakkuvār ēṉ?
If the two agree what need of arbitrators?
1065. iruvarālē ākāta kāriyam oruvarālē ākumā?
Can an affair be effected by one that could not be accomplished by two?
1067. iruvar naṭpukku oruvar poṟumi.
The friendship of two depends on the forbearance of one.
1068. iruḷaṉ piḷḷikku elikkuñcu pañcamā?
Does the son of an Irulan, a wild tribe, suffer from a scarcity of young
rats?
1069. iruḷaṉ rājaviḻi viḻippāṉā?
Can a savage put on the look of a king ?
1070. iruḷum oru kālam nilavum oru kālam.
A time of darkness, a time of moonshine.
1071. ireṭṭiyārē ireṭṭiyārē eṉṟāl, kalappiyit tiṭṭeṉṟu pōṭṭa kati.
The story of one who dropped his plough when accosted O Reddy, O Reddy.
1072. ilakkaṇam kaṟṟavaṉ kalakkam aṟa maṉṉar capi kāṇpāṉ.
The learned will appear undismayed in a royal assembly.
1073. ilakṣaṇam avalakṣaṇam mukattilē.
Beauty and ugliness are in the face.
1074. ilaṅkaṇam parama oḷaṣatam.
Abstinence is the best medicine.
1075. ilaṅkiyil piṟantavaṉ ellām irāvaṇaṉ āvāṉā?
Are all that are born in Ceylon, Ravanas?
1076. ilaṅkiyic cuṭṭa kuraṅku.
The monkey that set fire to Lanka.
1077. ilavu kātta kiḷi āṉēṉ.
I am like the parrot that waited for the silk-cotton pod.
Spoken of one who has been sadly disappointed in his expectations.
The proverb is sid to refer to a parrot that on seeing a green pod on a
silk-cotton tree believed it would ripen into fruit, whereas it
eventually burst and the cotton was scattered to the winds.
1078. iluppippūvit tiruppiṉāl irupuṟamum pottal.
If the flower of ilupi (Bassia Longifolia) be turned, it will be found
hollow on both sides.
1079. ili tiṉṉi kāy aṟiyāṉ.
He who feeds on leaves knows not the flavor of fruit.
1080. iliyum paḻuppum eṅkum uṇṭu.
Leaves green and sear are found every where.
1081. ilukīkam vitīkam iraṇṭum vēṇṭum.
The secular and the sacred are both necessary.
1082. illatu vārātu uḷḷatu pōkātu.
That which is not will not come, that which is, will not go.
1083. illaṟam peritu tuṟavaṟam ciṟitu.
Domestic life is better than that of the ascetic.
1084. illaṟam peritu tuṟavaṟam paḻippu.
Domestic life is honourable, that of the ascetic is disgraceful.
1085. illātavaṉ pollātavaṉ.
He that is destitute is wicked.
1086. illātatu piṟavātu aḷḷātatu kuṟiyātu.
That which is not, will not come out; that from which nothing is taken;
will not lessen.
1087.illātu colli allaṟ paṭutal.
Uttering a falsehood and suffering the evil thereof.
1088. illāḷi viṭṭu vallāṇmi pēcukiṟatā?
What! is it to speak manfully after deserting thy wife?
1089. illātum illaṉ iruppatum illaṉ.
He lacks nothing, nor does he possess any thing.
1090. illātavaṉ peṇcāti ellārukkum tōḻiyā?
Is the wife of the destitute the friend or companion of all ?
1091. illi eṉṟa vīṭṭil palliyum cērātu.
Even a lizard will not enter a house occupied by the niggardly.
1092. illōr irappatu iyalpu.
It is natural for the destitute to beg.
1093. ivaḷ vili mōril veṇṇey eṭuttut talimakaṉukkuk kaliyāṇam paṇṇuvāḷ.
She will make butter out of the butter milk-given for sale, and make
provision for the marriage of her first-born son.
1094. ivaṉukkum avaṉukkum ēḻām poruttam.
There are seven points of agreement between this person and that.
1095. ivaṉukkuc collum putti kaṭaliṟ peruṅkāyam karittatu pōl irukkiṟatu.
The advice given to him is like assafoetida dissolved in the sea.
1096. ivaṉ ūrārukkup piḷḷi.
He is the child of the whole village.
Said of a self-willed wandering youth.
1097. ivaṉ makā periya kaḷḷaṉ, kālālē muṭintatik kiyālē aviḻkkiṟatu aritu.
He is a clever rogue, what he ties with his feet it is difficult to
untie with the hand.
1098. ivaṉ kallātu kaṟṟavaṉ, uḷḷaṅkiyil vikuṇṭam kāṭṭuvāṉ.
He has attined the unattinable; he can show Vaikundam Paradise in the
palm of his hand.
1099. ivaṉ putti ulakkik koḻuntu.
His wit is as sharp as a wooden pestle.
1100. iḻavukku vantavaḷ tāli aṟuppāḷā?
Will she who attends a funeral remove her marriage symbol?
1101. iḻukkuṭiya pāṭṭiṟku ici naṉṟu.
Harmony sets off a faulty stanza.
1102. iḻuvi kaṇṭāl aṭi pārppāṉēṉ?
When the tril marks are before you, why look for footprints ?
1103. iḻi āyiram poṉ peṟṟa intiravarṇappaṭṭu.
A scarlet cloth each thread of which is worth a thousand gold pieces.
1104. iḻiyat tīṭṭik kuḻiya vaṭittal.
Thoroughly pounded and properly boiled.
1105. iḷakiṉa irumpik kaṇṭāṟ kollaṉ kuṇṭiyit tūkki aṭippāṉ.
When the smith perceives that the iron is soft, he will rise himself to
the stroke.
The Indian smith squats at the forge, and rises to his feet only when he
uses a two-handed hammer.
1106. iḷaṅkaṉṟu payam aṟiyumā?
Does a young calf know fear?
1107. iḷamiyiṟ cōmpal mutumiyiṉ miṭimi.
Indolence, in youth will bring poverty in old age.
1108. iḷamiyil muyaṟci mutumiyiṟ kākkum.
Industry in youth will support one in old age.
1109. iḷamiyiṟ paḻakkam eppōtum maṟavātu.
The habits of early life will never be forgotten.
1110. iḷamiyum mutumiyum cariyāṉa vayatalla.
Youth and senility do not properly belong to a man's age.
1111. iḷaveyil kāyāta nīyā tī pāyappōkiṟāy?
Do you who cannot endure the early beams of the sun presume to walk over
fire?
1112. iḷiñaṉ āṉālum ākavāṉ mūppu.
Although young he will play the man.
1113. iḷittavaḷ taliyil īrum pēṉum.
In the tresses of a poor woman are found nits and lice.
1114. iḷittavaṉ ciṉēkitaṉic cēr.
Associate with the friend of the poor.
1115. iḷittavaṉē eḷḷi viti.
O you weak fellow, sow the sessamum seed.
1116. iḷiyāḷ ili tiṉṉi mūttāḷ kāyaṟivāḷ.
The younger sister feeds on leaves, the elder is accustomed to fruit.
1117. iḷiyāḷē vāṭi maliyāḷam pōvōm, mūttāḷē vāṭi muṭṭikkoṇṭu cāvōm.
Come my younger sister, we will proceed to Malayalam; come my elder
sister, we will strike our heads together and die.
1118. iṟaku muṟṟip paṟavi āṉāl, ellām taṉ vayiṟṟit tāṉ pārkkum.
When mature and on the wing, all birds will look after their own food.
1119. iṟakkum kālam vantāl piṟakkum īcalukkuc ciṟaku.
When death approaches, white-ants take wing.
1120. iṟakkum kālam varāmal piṟakkumā īcalukkuc ciṟaku?
Will white-ants take wing except on the approach of death?
1121. iṟaṅkum tuṟiyilē nīttāṉāl, inta āṟṟi eppaṭik kaṭakkiṟatu?
If on stepping into the riverone is obliged to swim, how will I get
across ?
1122. iṟaṅku poḻutilē maruntu kuṭi.
Take medicine at sun-set.
1123. iṟantavaṉ iruppavaṉukku vaḻikāṭṭi.
The dead is the pilotof the living.
1124. iṟantavaṉ piḷḷi iruntavaṉ aṭikkalam.
The child of the dead is the ward of the living.
1125. iṟantavaṉukku eḷḷum taṇṇīrum.
Sessamum and water offered to the dead.
1126. iṟutta kuṭikku aṉarttam illi.
A family out of debt is out of danger.
1127. iṟuppāṉukkup paṇamum kiṭiyātu, uḻippāṉukkup peṇṇum kiṭiyātu.
He who pays his debts cannot obtin money, nor can the labourer obtin a
wife.
1128. iṟicci tiṉkiṟavar kaṭuppukku maruntu aṟivār.
They who live on flesh are acquainted with the medicine for tooth ache.
1129. iṟicci tiṉṟālum elumpik kōttup pōṭṭukkoḷḷalāmā?
Is one to wear a neck-lace of bones becuse he eats flesh ?
This and the preceding proverb are somewhat singular but not unnatural
among a vegetarian people.
1130. iṟitta kiṇaṟu ūṟum, iṟiyāta kiṇaṟu nāṟum.
The water of a well always drawn, is fresh, that of a well not drawn is
fetid.
1131. iṟitta kiṇaṟu curakkum.
The water of a well always drawn springs up a-fresh.
1132.iṉam pirinta māṉ pōla.
Like a deer separated from its herd.
1133. iṉimēl oru teyvattik kiyeṭukkiṟatā?
Hereafter is it to lift up the hands to a god?
1134. iṉimēl emalōkapariyantam cātikkalām.
Hereafter he may effect his purpose as far as the realms of Yama.
1135. iṉpamum tuṉpamum poṟumiyilē.
Joy and grief must be regulated by moderation.
1136. iṉpattil āci evarkkum uṇṭu.
Happiness is desired by all.
1137. iṉpamum tuṉpamum eṭutta uṭalukku.
The body it is that is affected by pleasure and pin.
1138. iṉṟu iruppār nāḷikku illi.
Those who are alive to-day may not be on the morrow.
1139. iṉṟikku ili aṟuttavaṉ nāḷikkuk kuli aṟuppāṉ.
He who cut off the leaves to-day may possibly cut off the bunch
tomorrow.
1140. iṉṟikku ākiṟatu nāḷikku ākaṭṭum.
That which is practicable to-day may be so on the morrow.
1141. iṉṟikkuc cettāl nāḷikku iraṇṭu nāḷ.
If one die to-day, to-morrow will be the second day.
1142. iṉṟi eṉpatum nāḷi eṉpatum illi eṉpataṟku aṭiyāḷam.
To say today; tomorrow, indicates refusal.
1143. ī kaliyāmal tēṉ eṭuppārkaḷ, eṭukkāmaṟ piṭippārkaḷ.
They will take the honey without dispersing the bees, they will take it
without rising it up.
1144. īki uṭiyavaṉ ekkaḷippu aṭivāṉ.
The liberal giver will be happy.
1145. īkku viṭam taliyil, tēḷukkuk koṭukkil.
In a fly, the head is the seat of poison, in a scorpion, the til.
1146. īcalukku ellām paki.
All are aginst the winged white-ants.
1147. īcal iṟaku ellāvaṟṟilum mirutu.
The wing of a white-ant is the softest of all things.
1148. īcal maṭintāṟpōl māṇṭatē cēṉi.
The army perished like winged white-ants.
1149. īcaṟ perumpōkkil tavaḷi tatti viḻuṅkukiṟatu.
The frog leaps and swallows the swarming winged white-ants.
1150. īcaṉukku oppu iṅku oṉṟum illi.
Nothing here is equal to God.
1151. īccaṅkaḷ etilum kuḷircci .
Date-palm toddy is the coolest of beverages.
1152. īccam kāṭṭil erumi kuṭiyirunta kati.
The story of a buffalo that lived in a date-palm grove.
1153. īṭaṉ pāṭañcāṉ kūḻiyerutu nuḷampañcātu.
A strong man fears not hardship, nor does a bullock with a stunted til
fear gnats.
1154. īṭākātavaṉi etirākkātē.
Do not oppose your inferior.
1155. īṭuḷḷa kuṭikkuk kēṭu illi.
A wealthy family is not exposed to danger.
1156. īṭṭiya poruḷiṉum eḻuttē uṭimi.
Learning is more substantial than accumulated riches.
1157. īṭṭi eṭṭiyamaṭṭum kuttum, paṇam patiṉkātamum kuttum.
A spear wounds as far as it reaches; the effect of money reaches ten
kathams
(100 miles).
1158. īṭṭukku īṭum cōṭṭukkuc cōṭumāy iruntāl vāci.
Like for like, and equality in a pir are desirable.
1159. īṭṭukkum pāṭṭukkum iruntāḷ iṭukumari.
The miden who was given in marriage was equal to all theexigencies of
life.
1160. īntu pārttāl immi veḷi ākum.
The smallest fraction will come out by division, immi = 1,075,200th part
of a unit.
1161. īppiciṉi irappatukūṭak kakkicam.
A miser will find even begging difficult.
1162. īyattik kāyccalām irumpik kāyccalāmā ?
Load may be melted, can iron ?
1163. īyam piṭittavaṉ etu colliṉum kēḷāṉ.
He who has taken up lead will not listen, no matter what is it to him.
1164. īyāta pullaṉi evviṭattilum kāṇōm.
A niggardly, savage is nowhere found.
1165. īyār tēṭṭit tīyār koḷvār.
The wicked obtin by rapacity what the miserly accumulate.
1166. īyār poruḷukkut tīyār.
Thewicked enjoy the property of the miser.
1167. īyum eṟumpum eṅkum uṇṭu.
Flies and ants abound everywhere.
1168. īyip piṭittāl ki vēṟu kāl vēṟu.
If you catch a fly, its limbs will be separated.
1169. īyip pōlē cuttamum eṟumpippōlē palamum.
Clean as a fly, andstrong as an ant.
1170. īraccīliyip pōṭṭuk kaḻuttu aṟuppāṉ.
He will cut the throat after tying a wet cloth round it.
1171. īra nāviṟku elumpu illi.
There is no bone ina slanderous tongue.
1172. īram aṟṟa iṭattilē ī moykkumā, īram uḷḷa iṭatitlē ī moykkumā? Do insects
swarm in a dry place or in a wet place ?
1173. īraveṅkāyattiṟku irupatteṭṭup puri.
In an onion there are twenty-eight coats.
1174. īri niṉippāṉ pēri maṟakkāṉ.
He who thinks of a nit will not forget its name.
1175. īrip pēṉākkip pēṉip perumāḷ ākkiṉēṉ.
I magnified the nit into a louse, and the louse into Perumal.
1176. īr uruvap pēṉ akappaṭumā?
Will lice be obtined when drawing nits between the finger and the
thumb?
1177. ī viṣṭittatum nāy tiruṭit tiṉṟatum.
That which a bee ejects and that which a dog has stolen
1178. ī viḻunta cātam eṭuttāloḻiyap pōmā ?
Will a fly that has fallen into the boiled rice.go unless it be taken
out?
1179. īvōṉukku oru pōcaṉam, irappōṉukku ērāḷam.
To the giver a meal, to the beggar plenty.
1180. īḻattiṟ cekkāṭa iṅkē patam pārkka.
The pressis turned in Lanka, and the material under pressure is examined
here.
1181. īṉavum teriyātu nakkavum teriyātu.
It knows not how to bring forth nor how to lick.
1182. īṉṟavaḷ tāy pāṭṭi, ivaḷ tāyiṉ tāy pīṭṭi.
The mother of one's mother is his grandmother, her mother is his great
grand mother.
1183.īṉṟōr nañciṟ cāṉṟōr illi.
Indulgence in the parent is not good for the child.
1184. ukamuṭiya maḻi peytālum ōṭṭāṅkiḷiñcil payir ākumā?
Though it should rin to the end of the world, would broken oyster
shells vegetate?
1185. ukirccuṟṟiṉ mēl ammi viḻuntatu pōl.
As a grind-stone fell on the whitlow.
1186. uṅkaḷ uṟavilē vēkiṟatippārkkilum oru kaṭṭu viṟakilē vēkiṟatu nallatu.
It is better to be consumed by a faggot than by your friendship.
1187. uṅkaḷ peṇṭukaḷ koṇṭal aṭittāl kaṇkaḷ koḷḷātu.
The eyes will be over-powered by the dancing of your women.
1188. uṅkaḷik kaṭalilē kikaḻuviṉēṉ.
I have washed my hands of you in the sea.
1189. ucu piṭi eṉṟāl nī piṭi eṉkiṟatām nāy.
It is sid that when he urged his dog to seize, it replied, you do so.
1190. uccantaliyiṟ ceruppāl aṭittālum ucci kuḷirumā?
If slippered on the head, will the crown of the head become cool?
1191. uṭampu ellām puḻuttavaṉ ammaṉ kōvilik keṭutta kati.
The story of a man who defiled Amman covil by his ulcerated body.
1192. uṭampuḷinta kaḻuti uppukaḷattiṟkup pōṉatu pōla.
As the wearied ass went to the salt-pit.
1193. uṭampu eṭuttavaṉ ellām ōṭu eṭuttāṉ.
All who have taken a body have taken an alms-dish.
1194. uṭampu tēṟṟikkoṇṭu allavā yōkattil pōkavēṇṭum?
Must one not invigorate the body and then proceed to meditation?
1195. uṭampu eṅkum cuṭukiṟa taḻali maṭiyilē kaṭṭukiṟāy.
Thou huggest up in thy bosom the embers that warm every part of the
body.
1196. uṭampu muḻutum naṉintavarukkuk kūtal eṉṉa ?
Of what consequence is cold to him whose whole body is drenched ?
1197. uṭalukkō pāl vārttu uṇpatu, ūrukkō pāl vārttu uṇpatu?
Do we feed on milk for the benefit of the body, or that our neighbours
may know it?
1198. uṭal iraṇṭu uyir oṉṟu.
Divided as regards body, one in soul.
1199. uṭal oruvaṉukkup piṟantatu nā palarukkup piṟantatu.
The body is born for one, the tongue for many.
1200. uṭal uḷḷavarikkum kaṭal koḷḷāta kavali.
Anxieties which the ocean cannot contin attendthe body as long as it
exists.
1201. uṭaṉpiṟappu illā uṭampu pāḻ.
The body of one who has no kindred is exposed to desolation.
1202. uṭāppuṭivi pūccikku iri.
A garment not worn is a prey to moths.
1203. uṭutta puṭivitāṉē pāmpāyk kaṭikkiṟatu.
The cloth one has put on bites as a snake.
1204. uṭumpu pōṉālum pōkiṟatu kiyiviṭṭāl pōtum.
The guana may go, it is enough if the hand be free.
1205. uṭumpukku iraṇṭu nākku maṉitaṉukku oru nāḷukku.
The guana has two tongues, man only one.
1206. uṭumpukku iraṇṭu nākku uṇṭu, maṉitaṉukku iraṇṭu nākku uṇṭā ?
The guana has a double tongue, has man the same?
1207. uṭi muḷḷukku etirē utikkalāmā?
Can you kick aginst the thorns of the Acacia?
1208. uṭimi eṉpatu kalviyuṭimi.
Learning is real wealth.
1209. uṭimiyum vaṟumiyum oru vaḻi nillā.
Wealth and poverty are not abiding.
1210. uṭimiyum koṭuttu arumiyum kulikiṟatā?
What! give one's property and sacrifice one's reputation?
1211. uṭiyavaṉ pārāp payir uruppaṭumā?
Will a crop not looked after by the owner flourish ?
1212. uṭiyavaṉ coṟpaṭi uralic cuṟṟik kaḷipaṟi.
