Pluck up the weeds about the mortar as your master bids you.
1213. uṭiyavaṉiṟ kippaṟṟiṉavaṉ miṭukkaṉ.
An embezzler is more obstinate than the owner.
1214. uṭiyavaṉ coṟpaṭi kamukaṭi kaḷipaṟi.
Weed around the areca tree at thy master's biding.
1215. uṭiyavaṉ illāc cēli oru muḻam kaṭṭi.
The cloth whose owner is absent is a cubit too short.
1216. uṭiyār vīṭṭu mōrukku akappik kaṇakku eṉṉa?
What necessity is there for estimating the quantity of buttermilk by the
ladle in the homestead of the chief?
1217. uṭkāntu allavō paṭukka vēṇṭum?
You must sit before lying down, must you not?
1218. uṭkāntavaṉik kaṭṭa māṭṭātavaṉ ōṭukiṟavaṉik kaṭṭuvāṉā?
Can he tie a man that is running who cannot tie one that is sitting?
1219. uṭcuvar irukkap puṟaccuvar pūcalāmā?
What! beautify the outside of a wall while the inside is neglected?
1220. uṭcuvar tīṟṟip puṟaccuvar tīṟṟu.
Garnish the inside of the wall and then the outside.
1221. uṭpuṟattukku veḷippuṟam kaṇṇāṭi.
A mirror reflects whatever is before it.
1222. uṇarvillāk karuviyum uppillāc cōṟum cari.
A mere instrument void of feeling and food without salt are alike.
1223. uṇkiṟa cōṟṟilē nañcik kalakkiṟatā ?
What, is it to mix poison with the rice one is eating?
1224. uṇṭa uṭampiṟku uṟuti, uḻuta pulattil nellu.
There is strength in a body well fed, and grin in the ploughed field.
1225. uṇṭa piḷḷi urañ ceyyum.
A child that eats well thrives.
1226. uṇṭa vayiṟṟukkuc cōṟum moṭṭittalikku eṇṇeyumā?
Is rice required for a full stomach, or oil for a baldhead?
1227. uṇṭatu tāṉ ēppam varum.
That which has been eaten will be evidenced in the belching.
1228. uṇṭa vīṭṭukku iraṇṭakam paṇṇalāmā?
May hospitality be requited by treachery?
1229. uṇṭa uṭampu urukum tiṉṟa pākkuc civakkum.
The body nourished by another's bounty will melt, arecanut when chewed
turns red.
1230. uṇṭa cuṟṟam urukum.
Friendship cemented by food will compassionate.
1231. uṇṭār mēṉi kaṇṭāl teriyum.
The body shows who is well fed.
1232. uṇṭāl tīrum paci, kaṇṭāl tīrumā?
Hunger is appeased by eating, will it be so by looking at food?
1233. uṇṭāl tiṉṟāl uṟavu, koṇṭāl koṭuttāl uṟavu.
Friendship is promoted and strengthned by hospitality and also by
intermarriage.
1234. uṇṭālum uṟutippaṭa uṇṇavēṇṭum.
When you eat, do so to keep up your strength.
1235. uṇṭāṟ kollum viṣam.
Poison destroys when taken.
1236. uṇṭi curuṅkutal peṇṭirkku aḻaku.
Moderation in food becomes women.
1237. uṇṭu tiṉṟu uḷḷē iruveṉṟāl uyara eḻumpi ēṉ kutikkiṟāy?
When you are told to eat heartily and remin in-doors, why do you rise
up and jump?
1238. uṇṭu uṟiyil iru eṉṟāl uruṇṭu tariyil viḻukiṟāy.
If I say eat and sit on the hanging-shelf, you fall to the ground.
1239. uṇṭu koḻuttāl naṇṭu vaḷiyil irātu.
If a crab gets fat, it will not stay in its hole.
1240. uṇṭu tiṉṟu uyaram āṉāl ūrilē kāriyam eṉṉa ?
What business has he in the village seeing that he is grown up?
1241. uṇṇak ki calittu irukkiṟāṉ.
His hand is weary with eating.
1242. uṇṇa vā eṉṟāl kutta varukiṟāy.
When I call you to eat, you come to strike me.
1243. uṇṇākki aṟuttuc cuṇṇāmpukkuṟi iṭuvēṉ.
I will cut your uvula and smear it with chunam-lime.
1244. uṇṇāc cottu maṇṇāyp pōkum.
Property not enjoyed comes to nought.
1245. uṇṇāmal ūr ellām tiriyalām uṭukkāmal oru vīṭṭukkum pōkalākātu.
One may wander over the whole country without eating, but one
may not go to a single house without dressing.
1246. uṇṇāmal oṉpatu vīṭu pōkalām, uṭukkāmal oru vīṭum pōkal ākātu.
One may go to nine houses without eating, but not to one without
garments.
1247. uṇṇum kīriyilē naṇṇum pulluruvi.
Mistleloe attached to edible greens.
1248. uṇpatu irukka oru karumam ceyyēl.
Do not go to work without eating.
1249. uṇpāṉ tiṉpāṉ civappirāmaṇaṉ, kuttukku niṟpāṉ vīra muṣṭi.
The Saiva Brahman will freely eat, the ferocious mendicant will show
fight.
1250. uṇpārip pārttālum uḻuvārip pārkkal ākātu.
Though you look at those who are eating, you may not look at those who
are ploughing.
1251. uṇmi collik keṭṭārum poy colli vāḻntārum illi.
None ever perishes by speaking the truth, none flourishes by uttering
falsehood.
1252. utaṭu maṉṟāṭap pōy uḷḷirunta pallum pōṉāṟpōla.
As if the teeth also went out when the lips had gone to beg.
1253. utaṭṭilē uḷḷa vāḻippaḻatti uḷḷē taḷḷuvār uṇṭō ?
Are there any who push into the mouth the plantin that is already
between the lips?
1254. utaṭṭukku miñciṉa pallum tiruṭṭukku miñciṉa kiyum ākātu.
Protruding teeth and a hand given to thieving are bad.
1255. utaṭṭilē puṇ āṉāl pāl kaṟakkātā ?
Will the cow not yield its milk becuse it has sore lips?
1256. utaṭṭut turumpu ūtap pōkātu ?
A small particle adhering to the lip cannot be removed by blowing.
1257. utara nimittam veku kirtavēṣam.
Divers disguises for the sake of the belly.
1258. utavāp paḻaṅkalamē ōci illā veṇkalamē!
Thou art a useless vessel, a piece of brass, without sound!
1259. utavāta ceṭṭikkuc cīṭṭu eḻutiṉatupōla.
Like writing a chit to a thrifty merchant.
1260. utavi ceyvārkku iṭiyūṟu ētu?
What, accident can harm those who help others?
1261. utaṟukāli vantāḷ uḷḷatum keṭuttāḷ.
A woman with a bustling git came and destroyed what there was.
1262. utārikkup poṉṉum turumpu.
Even gold is a thing of nought to the generous.
1263. utirattukku allō urukkam irukkum ?
Sympathy arises, does it not, from consanguinity?
1264. utitta kāl puḻukkiṟapōtu allavō puḻukkum?
Worms will breed in due course, will they not, in a foot accustomed to
kick?
1265. utitta kāl puḻukkiṟataṟku muṉṉē aṭivayiṟu cīḻkaṭṭukiṟatu.
Before his kicking feet were eaten of worms his abdomen has ulcerated.
1266. utippāṉukku veḷuppāṉ cātivaṇṇāṉ.
He that washes for him who kicks him is a first-rate washerman.
1267. uttamaṉukkum tappilikkum uṭaṉpaṭikki vēṇṭām.
Contracts are needless as regards honest men and cheats.
The one needs no binding, and the other admits no obligations.
1268. uttamaccēvakaṉ peṟṟa tāykku atikam.
A faithful servant is more than a mother.
1269. uttamaṉukku ettālum kēṭu illi.
The good are exposed to no danger.
1270. uttiyōkam puruṣa lakṣaṇam.
Exertion is a manly quality.
1271. uttiyōkattukkut takka cukam.
Facilities suited to one's office.
1272. upakārattukku apakāram varuvatu turatiṣṭam.
It is unfortunate when evil is returned for good.
1273. upacariyāta vīṭṭilē uṇṇātiruppatē oru kōṭi taṉam.
It is a crore of wealth to abstin from entertinment in a house void of
civility.
1274. upacāram ceytavarkku apacāram paṇṇukiṟatā?
What! are the civil to be requited by incivility!
1275. upacāra vārtti kācākumā, uṇṭāloḻiyap paci tīrumā?
Will mere ceremonypass for money, will hunger be appeased otherwise than
by eating?
1276. upāyattāl ākiṟatu parākkiramattāl ākumā?
Can that which may be realized by cunning be obtined by force ?
1277. uppu irunta pāṇṭamum upāyam irunta neñcamum taṭṭi uṭiyāmal tāṉē uṭiyum.
A salt-cup and a deceitful heart will of themselves come to destruction.
1278. uppu irunta pāṇṭamum uṇmi illā neñcum taṭṭi uṭiyāmal tāṉē uṭiyum.
A vessel impregnated with salt, and a mind without truth need none to
destroy them, they destroy themselves.
1279. uppu illāp paṇṭam kuppiyilē.
Unsalted food is fit only for the rubbish heap.
1280. uppu iṭṭavarkaḷi uḷḷaḷavum niṉi.
Remember through life those who have given you salt.
1281. uppu mutal karppūram varikkum vēṇṭum.
Every thing from salt to camphor is required.
Said of things domestic and sacred.
1282. upput tiṉṟavaṉ taṇṇīrik kuṭippāṉ.
He who has eaten salt will drink water.
1283. uppukkaṇṭam paṟikoṭutta pārppātti pōla viḻikkiṟāy.
Thou starest as the Brahmanee who had lost her salted mutton.
In her bewilderment she had no remedy becuse she dared not make known
the loss of a thing she ought not to have had in her possession.
1284. uppu vāṇikaṉ aṟivāṉā karppūra vili ?
Does the salt merchant know the price of camphor?
1285. uppuk kaṭṭiṉāl lōkam kaṭṭum.
If there be a supply of salt the world will be sustined.
1286. uppum karppūramum oṉṟāy vaḻaṅkumā?
Are salt and camphor used together?
1287. uppu nīr mēkam uṇṭāl ulakiṟ piravākam.
If the ocean were to become clouds, the world would be flooded.
1288. upput taṇṇīrukku ilāmiccam vēr pōṭavēṇṭumā ?
Must the root of cusa grass be added to sea-water to make it fragrant?
1289.uppu miñciṉāl taṇṇīr, taṇṇīr miñciṉāl uppu.
Excess of salt calls for water, excess of water requires salt.
1290. uppic cintiṉiyō tuppic cintiṉiyō?
Did you spill the salt or the food ?
1291. uppittoṭṭu uppit tiṉṉātē.
Do not use salt both as food and condiment.
1292. uppit toṭṭukkoṇṭu urali viḻuṅkalām.
Using salt as a condiment you may swallow a mortar.
1293. uppōṭu oṉpatum paruppōṭu pattum vēṇṭum.
Nine things are required with salt, ten with pulse.
1294. umakku eṉṉa, vayatukku narittatō mayirukku narittatō ?
Have you grown grey by age, or is your hir prematurely grey?
1295. umiyik kuṟṟik ki calittatu pōla.
As the hand was wearied by beating husks in a mortar.
1296. uyarap paṟantālum ūrkkuruvi karuṭaṉ ākumā?
Though it may fly high, will a common bird become a hawk ?
1297. uyirum uṭalum pōla.
Like soul and body.
1298. uyiri vittirukkiṟatilum cettāṟ kuṇam.
It is more natural to die than to continue in life.
1299. uyirōṭu tirumpip pārātavar cettāl muttam koṭuppārā?
Will he who would not turn to look at me when alive kiss me when dead?
1300. uyirōṭu oru muttam tarātavaḷ cettāl uṭaṉkaṭṭi
ēṟuvāḷā?
Will she who would not kiss me when alive ascend my funeral pyre when I
am dead?
1301. uyir iruntāl uppumāṟit tiṉṉalām.
If one is alive, he may subsist by bartering salt.
1302. uralil akappaṭṭatu ulakkikkut tappumā?
Will that which is in the mortar escape the pestle?
1303. uralilē taliyi māṭṭikkoṇṭu ulakkikkup payappaṭṭāl tīrumā?
Will your fear of the pestle avil aught after your head has become fast
in the mortar?
1304. uralukku oru pakkam iṭi mattaḷattukku iraṇṭu pakkamum iṭi.
The mortar is beaten at one end the drum at both.
1305. ural pañcam aṟiyumā?
Does the rice mortar know what a famine is?
1306. ural pōy mattaḷattōṭē muṟiyiṭṭatu pōla.
As the mortar went to the tomtom with its complints.
Used when one complins of his misfortunes to another who is in greater
distress. The mortar is beaten at one end only, whereas thetomtom is
beaten at both ends.
1307. uru ēṟat tiru ēṟum.
Supernatural power increases according to the intensity of the
incantations.
1308. urukkam uḷḷa ciṟṟātti otukkil vāṭi kaṭṭi aḻa.
O my loving aunt, come with me apart that we may embraceeach other and
weep.
1309. uruci kaṇṭa pūṉi uṟiyit tāvum.
The cats that has tasted nice things will leap on the swinging-tray.
1310. uruṭṭap puraṭṭa uḷḷatum uḷḷukku vāṅkum.
When one deals frudulently, even that which he has diminishes.
1311. uruṭṭum puraṭṭum oṭukkum ciṟappi.
Fruds and trickswill destroy reputation.
1312. uruṭṭum puraṭṭum miraṭṭum collum.
Tricks and fruds and threats.
1313. uruttirākṣap pūṉi oṉṟum teriyātu pōl irukkum.
Like a beaded cat be appears as if he knew nothing.
1314. uruttirākṣap pūṉi upatēcam paṇṇiṉatupōla.
As a beaded cat gave divine instruction.
1315. uruppaṭat tiruppaṭum.
As the figure is formed, beauty will become manjfest.
1316. uruvatti alla kuṇattippār, paṇattiyalla caṉattippār.
Look to the temper, not beauty, to the connections not at money, when
choosing a wife.
1317. uruvattiṉāl alla, iṉpap pēcciṉāl kiḷi naṉkumatikkappaṭum.
It is not for its form, but for its Sweet prattling that a parrot is
esteemed.
1318. uruviya vāḷi uṟiyil iṭāta vīraṉ.
A hero who does not sheath his sword.
1319: ulakattukku ñāṉam pēy, ñāṉattukku ulakam pēy.
To the world wisdom is folly, to wisdom the world is folly.
1320. ulakam palavitam.
The world has many forms.
1321. ulakilē kaḷḷaṉukku ūrār yāvarum paki.
The whole country is at enmity with the thief who dwells there.
1322. ulakkippūcikku aciyāttu tiruppāṭṭukku aciyumā?
Will that which has resisted the strokes of the pestle shake at asacred
chant?
1323. ulakki tēyntu uḷippiṭi āyiṟṟu.
The pestle by wearing away bas became the bundle of a chisel.
1324. ulakki peruttu uttiram āyiṟṟu.
The pestle grew and became a beam.
1325. uluttaṉ viruntiṟku oppāṉatu oṉṟum illi.
Nothing can be compared to the banquet of a miser.
1326. ulivāyi mūṭiṉālum ūr vāyi mūṭalāmā?
You way cover the mouth of a rice-pot, but can you cover the mouth of
the country?
1327. ulōpikku iru celavu.
The avaricious are subject to double expense.
1328. ullācanaṭi melukkukkuk kēṭu miṉukku eṇṇey talikkuk kēṭu.
An affected git is injurious to graceful movement, and varnish is bad
for the head.
1329. uḻak kuḷir aṭittāl nāṟṟup piṭuṅkappaṭātā?
If it be too cold to plough, why not put out the young plants?
1330. uḻakku uṟṟārkkup patakkup paratēcikku.
A quarter of a measure to one's friends, and sixteen measures to &
religious mendicant.
1331. uḻakkilē kiḻakku mēṟkā?
Is the distinction of east and west observable in an ullak a small
measure of capacity?
1332. uḻakkilē vaḻakku.
A dispute about an ullak.
See the preceding.
1333. uḻakku miḷaku koṭuppāṉ ēṉ, oḷittiruntu miḷaku nīrkuṭippāṉ ēṉ?
Why give a measure of pepper, why drink the pepper water in a corner?
1334. uḻaviṟku ēṟṟa koḻu.
A plough share adapted to the tillage.
1335.uḻaviṉ mikutta ūtiyam illi.
Nothing more advantageous than tillage.
1336. uḻavu oḻinta māṭu paṭṭippuṟattilē.
Cattle unfit for the plough are kept outside.
1337. uḻavu māṭu āṉāl ūrukkuḷḷē vilippaṭātā?
If the ox be fit for the plough, will it not fetch a price in its own
village?
1338. uḻavukku oru cuṟṟum varātu, ūṇukku muṉṉē varum.
At the plough it will not go a single round, at foddering time it comes
first.
1339. uḻavukkup piṇittuviṭukiṟa māṭum, kūṭṭukkup piṭittuviṭukiṟa āḷum
utavātu.
The bullock yoked to the plough by force, and a man acting as a
subsitute are alike useless.
1340. uḻukiṟati viṭṭu naḻuvukiṟavaṉ teyvam āṭiṉāṟpōla.
Like one who, to avoid ploughing, feigned to be demonized.
1341. uḻukiṟa nāḷiyil ūrukkup pōy, aṟukkiṟa nāḷiyil arivāḷ koṇṭuvantāṟpōla.
As if one retired into the interior in the ploughing season, and
returned with a sickle at the approach of harvest.
1342. uḻukiṟamāṭu paratēcam pōṉāl, aṅku oruvaṉ kaṭṭi uḻuvāṉ iṅku oruvaṉ kaṭṭi
uḻuvāṉ.
If a ploughing bullock go astray, it will be yoked wherever found.
1343. uḻukiṟa māṭṭi nukattāl aṭittāṟpōla.
Like beating the ploughing ox with the yoke.
1344. uḻukiṟa māṭṭi erutu nakkiṉatupōla.
As an ox licked a yoke of oxen when ploughing.
1345. uḻukiṟavaṉ iḷappamāṉāl erutu macciṉamuṟi koṇṭāṭum.
The ox treats the unskilful ploughman as his brother-in-law.
1346. uḻutavaṉukku ūrkkaṇakkuppaṇṇat teriyātu.
A ploughmanis unable to keep village accounts.
1347. uḻutavaṉ kaṇakkup pārttāl uḻakkēṉum miñcātu.
When a ploughman balanceshis accounts, not even an ullak grin remins.
1348. uḷavaṉ illāmal ūr aḻiyumā?
Can a.country be destroyed if there be no spy?
1349.uḷavu illāmal kaḷavu illi.
NoSpy, no theft.
1350. uḷivaḻiyum, aṭimaḻiyum, poti erutum, oruvaṉumāy alikiṟāṉ.
He wanders alone after a pack-bull, through deepmudand incessantrin.
1351. uḷvīṭṭuk kaṭaṉum, uḷḷaṅkic ciraṅkum keṭṭatu.
To be in debt to an inmate and to have the itch in the palm of the hand
are intolerable evils.
1352. uḷḷa piḷḷi urali nakkikkoṇṭirukka, maṟṟoru piḷḷikkuttiruppatikku
naṭakkiṟāḷ.
While her child licks the mortar, she goes on apilgrimage to Tirupati in
expectation of another.
1353. uḷḷaṅkip pāṟcōṟṟi viṭṭup puṟaṅki nakkiyatu pōl.
Like putting rice-milk in the palm and licking the back of the hand.
1354. uḷḷatik koṇṭu illatip pārāṭṭalām.
Withwhat we have we may pretend to have that which we have not.
1355. uḷḷatic coṉṉāl uṭampellām puṇṇām.
It is sid that the whole body breaks out into ulcers when the truth is
told.
1356. uḷḷati viṟṟu nallatik koḷḷu.
Sell what you have and buy what is really good.
1357. uḷḷaṉum kaḷḷaṉumāy irukkiṟāṉ.
He is both an inmate and a thief.
1358. uḷḷaṅkiyil oṉpatu koṇṭi muṭippēṉ.
I will tie nine knots of hir in the palm of the hand.
1359. uḷḷaṅkiyil nellikkaṉipōla.
Like the Nelli berry (Phyllanthus simplex) in the palm of the hand.
Said of something unmistakably evident.
1360.uḷḷaṅkiyil urōmamuḷitta tāyiṉ, aṟivilāṉ aṭaṅkuvāṉ.
If it be that hir has sprung up in the palm of the hand, a fool may
yield to discipline.
1361.uḷḷaṉum kaḷḷaṉum kūṭiṉāl viṭikiṟa maṭṭum tiruṭalām.
When an inmate of a house joins the thief, stealing may be carriedon
till day- break.
1362. uḷḷatic coṉṉāl noḷḷikkaṇṇikku nōppāḷam.
If the truth be told, the blind woman will be greatly afflicted.
1363. uḷḷati ellām koṭuttu noḷḷikkaṇṇiyik koṇṭāṉām.
It is sid that he took to wife a blind girl having given away all he
had.
1364.uḷḷavaṉ piḷḷi uppōṭu uṇṇum, illātavaṉ piḷḷi carkkariyōṭu uṇṇum.
The child of the wealthy takes salt with his food, the child of this
poor, sugar.
1365. uḷḷakkaruttu vaḷḷalukkut teriyum.
Our inmost thoughts are known to God.
1366.uḷḷatic coṉṉāl ellārukkum paki.
When the truth is told, every one takesit ill.
1367. uḷḷatiyum keṭuttāṉ koḷḷik kaṇṇaṉ.
The evil-eyed destroyed even what there was.
1368. uḷḷatic coṉṉavaṉ ūrukkup pollātavaṉ.
He who speaks the truth is regarded as a wicked person by the country.
1369. uḷḷāḷum kaḷḷāḷum kūṭṭamā?
Is association between an inmate and a thief to be tolerated?
1370. uḷḷiruntārkkut teriyum uḷvaruttam.
Internal distress is known to the inmates.
1371. uḷḷūr marumakaṉum uḻukiṟa kaṭāvum cari.
A son-in-law of the same village and a ploughing buffalo are over
wrought.
1372. uḷḷē koṭṭiṉa tēḷē oru mantiram collukiṟēṉ kēḷē.
O thou scorpion thou hast thrust thy sting into me, listen, I will utter
an incantation.
1373. uḷḷē pakiyum utaṭṭilē uṟavumā?
What! is itenmity at heart and friendship on the lips?
1374. uḷḷē vayiṟeriya utaṭu paḻam coriya.
Bowels burning within, lips shedding ripe fruit.
1375. uṟavilē nañcu kalakkiṟatā ?
What! is poison to be mixed with friendship?
1376. uṟavupōla iruntu kuḷavipōlak koṭṭukiṟatā?
What ! to feign friendship and to sting as a wasp?
1377. uṟavu uṟavutāṉ paṟiyilē ki vikkātē.
No doubt about friendship, but do not put your hand into my basket.
1378. uṟavukkum pakikkum poruḷē tuṇi.
Wealth may aid both in friendship and enmity.
1379. uṟavukku oṉpatu paṭi ūrukkup pattup paṭi.
Nine measures for your friends and ten for the country.
1380. uṟi aṟa mūḷi nāykku vēṭṭi
The snapping of the cord of the swinging-tray is like a hunting
excursion to the cropped dog.
1381. uṟiyilē tayir irukka ūr eṅkum pōṉāṟpōl.
As if one sought curds throughout the village when a supply was on his
own tray.
1382. uṟiyilē veṇṇey irukka neykku alivāṉēṉ?
Why go about in quest of ghee when you have butter on your own tray?
1383. uṟiyiṟ paṇam pōyt teruviṟ caṇṭiyi iḻukkiṟatu.
The money on the swinging-tray goes out and picks a quarrel in the
public road.
Money is not infrequently put on a swinging tray in a cap or small
basket.
1384. uṟutiyāṉa kāriyam orupōtum keṭātu.
An affair firmly settled will never fail.
1385. uṟutīṅkukku utavātavaṉ uṟṟavaṉā?
Is he a friend who helps not in adversity?
1386. uṟcākam ceytāl maccit tāṇṭuvāṉ.
If encouraged, he will jump over the house top.
1387. uṟṟa cinēkitaṉ uyirkku amirtam.
A true friend is the nectar of life.
1388. uṟṟatu coṉṉāl aṟṟatu poruntum.
When the truth is told, a breach between friends may be healed.
1389. uṟṟatu colla ūrum alla, nallatu colla nāṭum alla.
This is not the village in which a man may speak the truth, nor the
country in which, to give advice.
1390. uṟṟār tiṉṟāl puṟṟāy viḷiyum, ūrār tiṉṟāl pērāy viḷiyum.
If friends consume one's grin an ant hole, if strangers consume it,
fame.
1391. uṟṟārukku oṉṟum koṭāṉ, namaṉārukku nālum koṭuppāṉ.
He will give nothing to friends, but to Yama the regent of the dead he
will give even four i. e., all.
1392. uṟṟār utavuvārō aṉṉiyar utavuvārō?
Will friends help or will strangers ?
1393. uṉakkum pēpē uṉ appaṉukkum pēpē .
Pe, pe to you, and pe pe to your father also.
The following story is told about this proverb:-a debtor had two
creditors. One of them sid if you will pay me I will suggest an
expedient for getting rid of the other creditor. He then instructed him
when agin applied to for payment, to feign madness, and to say Pé, Pé-a
demon, a demon. The creditor not satisfied had him summoned before a
Magistrate where he did as before. The Magistrate thinking the debtor
mad, decided that he was not amenable to a court of justice. The other
creditor then went to the debtor expecting to be pid according to
promise. To his surprise the man sid Pe,Pe, whereupon he sid, do you
deal with me also in this way, when, the debtor
replied as the proverb stands.
1394. uṉakku yāṉ apayam eṉakku nī upayam.
I am under your protection, you are my patron.
1395. uṉ cāmarttilē kuṇṭu pāyātu.
Bullets will not fly by your strength.
1396. uṉ collilē uppum illi puḷiyum illi.
In your words there is neither salt nor acid.
1397. uṉ muṟukkut tirukku ellām eṉ uṭuppukkuḷḷē.
Your petulance and frud are all in my dress.
1398. uṉṉi vañcittavaṉi orupōtum nampātē.
Never trust one who has deceived you.
1399. uṉṉik koṭuppēṉō ceṉṉik kūṉi, nī cumi cumantallō kūṉippōṉāy.
O thou hunch-backed woman of Madras, how can I give thee up, hast thou
not become crooked by bearing burdens ?
1400. ūcal āṭiyum taṉ niliyil niṟkum.
Though the swing oscillates, it will resume its proper centre.
1401. ūci oru muḻattuṇiyiyāvatu koṭukkum uṟṟār eṉṉa koṭuppār?
A needle will give a cubit of cloth, what will your friends give ?
1402. ūci koḷḷap pōyt tulām kaṇakkup pārkkiṟatā?
When going to buy a needle, is the weight to be regarded?
1403. ūci kōkkiṟataṟku ūril tuḷāvāram ēṉ?
Why such a stir in the village about threading a needle ?
1404. ūcikkuk kaḷḷaṉ uṭaṉē iruppāṉ.
Where there is a needle there will be a thief.
1405. ūcikku ūci etirēṟip pāyumā?
Can one needle penetrate another when brought point to point?
1406. ūci poṉṉāṉāl eṉṉa peṟum?
Though made of gold what will a needle fetch?
1407. ūci pōkiṟatu kaṇakkup pārppāṉ pūcaṉikkāy pōkiṟatu teriyātu.
He notes the loss of needles, but not that of pumpkins.
1408. ūcipōla miṭaṟum tāḻipōla vayiṟum.
A throat like a needle, a belly like a caldron.
1409. ūci maliveṉṟu cīmikkup pōkalāmā?
May one go to a foreign country to buy needles becuse they are there
cheap?
1410. ūci muṉiyil tavam ceytālum uḷḷatutāṉ kiṭikkum.
Though penance be performed by standing on the point of a needle, that
which is predestined only will be obtined.
1411. ūciyiṉ kātil oṭṭakam nuḻiyumā ?
Can a camel pass through the eye of a needle ?
1412. ūciyi ūcikkāntam iḻukkum, uttamaṉic ciṉēkam iḻukkum.
A magnet attracts a needle, friendship, the good.
1413. ūṇaṉ karumam iḻantāṉ, uluttaṉ peyar iḻantāṉ.
The glutton failed in his object, and the miser lost his fame.
1414. ūṇiṉāl uṟavu pūṇiṉāl aḻaku.
Friendship is promoted by entertinments, and beauty is set off by
ornaments.
1415. ūṇiṉāl putti pūṇiṉāl cāti.
Good sense is indicated by food, and rank by ornaments.
1416. ūṇukku muntavēṇṭum kōḷukkup pintavēṇṭum.
Be first at a feast, and the last to slender.
1417. ūṇum illi uṟakkamum illi.
Neither food nor sleep.
1418. ūṇ aṟṟapōtē uṟavu aṟṟuppōm.
When entertinment is discontinued, friendship ceases.
1419. ūṇpākku oḻiya vīṇpākku ākātu.
Betel is not good except after food.
1420. ūta aṟintavaṉ vāti uppu aṟintavaṉ yōki.
He is an alchemist who knows how to infuse metals, and he is a Yogi who
knows the quality of salt.
1421. ūtārikkup poṉṉum turumpu.
Even gold is a mere rush to the spendthrift.
1422. ūtiṉāṟ pōm uṟiñciṉāl varum.
If blown it will fly off, if sucked it will go in.
1423. ūtukiṟa caṅku ūtiṉāl viṭikiṟapōtu viṭikiṟatu.
If you blow the conch, the day will break as it is wont.
1424. ūttivāyaṉ tēṭa nāṟṟavāyaṉ tiṉṉa.
Acquired by the filthy mouthed and consumed by the fetid mouthed.
1425. ūtti tiraṇṭu accāṇi ākumā?
Will the dirt on one's body when collected form a linch-pin?
1426. ūttivāyaṉ tēṭak karppūravāyaṉ tiṉṉa.
Accumulated by the foul mouthed and consumed by a mouth fragrant with
camphor.
1427. ūtti pōkak kuḷittavaṉum illi pacipōkat tiṉṟavaṉum illi.
No one performs ablutions so as not to repeat them, nor does any one eat
so as not, agin to suffer hunger.
1428. ūmi ūrik keṭukkum āmi kiṇaṟṟik keṭukkum.
The dumb destroys a village, a tortoise or turtle destroys a well.
1429. ūmi ūrik keṭukkum peruccāḷi vīṭṭik keṭukkum.
A community is injured by the dumb, and a house by bandycootes.
1430. ūmi kaṇṭa kaṉāppōl irukkiṟatu.
It is like the dream of the dumb.
He is unable to relate his dream and therefore cannot obtin its
interpretation.
1431. ūmikku uḷaṟuvāyaṉ uṟpātapiṇṭam.
The dumb regards a babbler as a wonder.
1432. ūmiyaṉ pēccu paḻakiṉa pērukkut teriyum.
The speech-gesticulation of the dumb is known to those accustomed to it.
1433. ūmiyāyiruntāl ceviṭum uṇṭu.
If one is dumb he is deaf also.
1434. ūmiyum ūmiyum mūkkic coṟintāṟpōla.
As the dumb on meeting the dumb scratch their noses.
If a person on meeting a dumb man scratches his own nose the dumb man
becomes very angry.
1435. ūmiyum alla ceviṭum alla.
Neither dumb nor deaf.
1436. ūrārip pakittu uyirōṭu iruntavar illi.
No one at enmity with the whole community ever preserved his life.
1437. ūrār kaṇakku uṭiyavaṉ piṭariyilē.
The liabilities of a village are on the neck of its chief.
1438. ūrār uṭimikkup pēyāyp paṟakkiṟāṉ.
He flies on the property of others like a demon.
1439. ūrār erumi pāl kaṟakkiṟatu nīyum ūṭṭu nāṉum uṇṇukiṟēṉ.
The buffalo of the village is in milk, you suck and I will also suck.
1440. ūrilē kalyāṇam mārpilē cantaṉam.
Marriage ceremonies in the village and sandal paste on the breast.
1441. ūril oruvaṉē tōḻaṉ ārum aṟṟatē tāram.
Have one only of the community as a friend, and a woman without ties as
a wife.
1442. ūril eḷiyāri vaṇṇāṉ aṟivāṉ cātippoṉ pūṇṭārit taṭṭāṉ aṟivāṉ.
The washerman knows the poor of a village, the goldsmith knows whose
ornaments are made of fine gold.
1443. ūrukkellām oru vaḻi uṉakku oru vaḻiyā ?
While all the people of the village have one road in common, do you
expect one for yourself ?
1444. ūrukkup pāl vārttu uṇṇukiṟāyā uṭampukkup pālvārttu uṇṇukiṟāyā?
Do you take milk on account of the community or to promote your own
health?
1445. ūrukkellām cāstiram collukiṟa palli kūḻppāṉiyil viḻuntāṟpōla.
As if a lizard, the oracle of the whole village, should fall into a pot
of gruel.
Spoken reproachfully, when a person remarkable either by position or
profession, has done something injurious to his reputation.
The lizard-palli a newt-here referred to, is seen on the walls of houses
in India and Ceylon, and its chirp may be frequently heard. Its voice is
never disregarded since it may bear a message of great consequence to
the family or individual concerned. A small book called the Shaster of
the Lizard contins all needed information regarding its chirping. On
hearing it the facts to be observed are the point of the compass, the
hour of the day &c. These being known the party has recourse to his
formula and ascertins whether the omen is auspicious or not. The
premonitions of this little domestic monitor derive their significance
from the character of the preternatural regent who may at the time
preside over the region indicated by the chirp. Thus it appears that the
shaster of the domestic lizard is based on the dogmas of Astrology.
1446. ūrukku ākātavaṉ vīṭṭukkum ākāṉ.
He who is useless abroad is useless at home.
1447. ūrukku ākāta piḷḷi tāykku ākumā?
Is a child injurious to the community, of any use to its mother?
1448. ūrukku ōmal vīṭṭukku vayiṟṟericcal.
Fame abroad and famine at home.
1449. ūrukku iḷittavaṉ piḷḷiyārkōyil āṇṭi.
He who was too feeble for the service of the community became a devotee
in the temple of Genesha.
In Sanskrit a similar proverb exists : “The dancing girl being old has
become a devotee”. It is a case not of breaking away from sinful habits,
but the power of sinning is gone.
1450. ūruṭaṉ pakikkil vēruṭaṉ keṭum.
If one is hated by the whole community he will be rooted out.
1451. ūreṅkum pēr vīṭu paṭṭiṉi.
Fame throughout the country, at home starvation.
1452. ūrellām vāḻkiṟateṉṟu vīṭellām puraṇṭu aḻutāl varumā?
Can you gin anything by wallowing and weeping becuse the village is
prosperous?
1453. ūrellām cuṟṟi eṉakku eṉṉa putti eṉkiṟāṉ.
Having wandered all over the country he says, what advice do you give.
1454. ūrellām uṟṟār antipaṭṭāl poti cantiyilē.
He has friends in every part of the village, yet his bundle is left in
the public road after sun-set.
1455. ūreṉṟu iruntāl paṟiccēriyum irukkum.
Wherever there is a village there is a Paracheri.
A Parachery is a hamlet of Pariahs—non caste Natives. Pariahs are
employed in the lowest offices, as beaters of the tomtom or Native drum
on festive and mournful occasions. They are employed to publish
proclamations &c, as a bell-man in Europe.
1456. ūri uḻakkāl aḷakkiṟāḷ nāṭṭi nāḻiyāl aḷakkiṟāḷ.
She measures the village with an ulak and the province with a nali.
The uḻakku is a quarter of a paṭi. uḻakkāḻākku three eighth of a measure. A
nāḻi is a measure, of which there are eight in amarakkāl , marcal.
1457. ūrip pārkkac coṉṉāl paṟiccēriyip pārkkiṟāṉ.
When told to visit the village, he goes to the Pariah quarter.
1458. ūrip piṭitta caṉi piḷḷiyāriyum piṭittatu.
Saturn that seized the village seized also Ganesha. One thing happens to
all.
1459. ūri vaḷittālum uṟṟa tuṇi illi, nāṭṭi vaḷittālum nalla tuṇi illi.
Though one goes round the village he finds no help, though he goes round
the country no real aid can be got.
1460. ūrōṭa okka ōṭu nāṭōṭa naṭu ōṭu.
If the people of the village flee, join them, if the whole country flee,
flee in their midst.
1461. ūrōccam vīṭu paṭṭiṉi.
Distinguished abroad, starving at home.
1462. ūr āḷukiṟavaṉ peṇṭu piṭittāl āruṭaṉ colli muṟiyiṭukiṟatu?
When the ruler of a village forcibly takes away another man's wife, to
whom is be to make his complint ?
1463. ūr irukkiṟatu ōṭu irukkiṟatu.
The village still exists and also the alms-dishes.
1464. ūr iraṇṭu paṭṭāl kūttāṭikkuk koṇṭāṭṭam.
If the village be divided into two parties, it will be advantageous to
the commedians.
1465. ūr iḷappam vaṇṇāṉukkut teriyum.
That which is bad or exceptionable in a village is known to its
washerman.
The clothes of all pass through his hands, he has therefore the
opportunity of inferring much from their condition.
1466. ūr uṇṭu piccikku, kuḷam uṇṭu taṇṇīrukku.
There is a village for alms, and a tank for water.
1467. ūr ōrattiṟ kolli uḻutavaṉukkup payir illi.
He that cultivates a field on the outskirts of a village loses its
produce.
1468. ūr kūṭic cekkut taḷḷiṉāṟpōla.
As if a whole village united to put an oil-press in motion.
1469. ūrkkuruvimēl rāmapāṇam toṭukkiṟatā?
What, discharge Rama's arrow at a sparrow ?
Why lavish means so preciouson objectsso mean?
1470. ūrkkōḻiyum nāṭṭukkōḻiyum kūṭi uraliṟ puḻuṅkal aricikkuc cētam. When
the poultry of the village and the poultry of the country at large come
together, the rice in the mortar is spoiled.
1471. ūr naṣṭam ūrilē tēr naṣṭam teruvilē.
Loss occurring in a village affects the community, injury occasioned by
a temple car is apparent in the street.
1472. ūr nallatō vāy nallatō?
Is the village good or your description?
1473. ūrppiḷḷiyi muttamiṭṭāl, utaṭṭukkuk kēṭu.
To kiss the child of another is bad for the lips.
1474. ūr vāyip paṭaliṭṭu mūṭalāmā?
Is it possible to shut the mouth of a village by a hurdle?
1475. ūr vāḻntāl ōṭṭuppiccikkum vaḻi irukkum.
If the village be prosperous—there will be an opening for alms-dishes.
1476. ūḻikkāyccal atikam āṉāl cūṉiyakkāraṉ koḷḷi.
When epidemic fever previls, sorcerers abound.
1477. ūḻi pēriṉum ūkkamatu kiviṭēl.
Though the world come to an end do not remit your efforts.
1478. ūḻiṟ peruvali oṉṟu uṇṭō ?
Is there any power greater than destiny?
1479.ūḻum uṟcākamum ottukkoḷḷavēṇṭum.
Destiny and exertion must go together.
1480. ūṉṟak koṭutta taṭi ucciyi uṭikkiṟatu.
The staff I gave for his support breaks the crown of my head.
1481. eṅkaḷāl oṉṟum illi ellām uṅkaḷ karmam.
Nothing lies in our might, all must be done by you.
1482.eṅkum maṭamāy irukkiṟatu irukkattāṉ iṭam illi.
Choultries everywhere, but no place of accommodation.
1483.eṅkē aṭittālum nāy kālit tūkkum.
No matter where hit, adog when struck, lifts up his leg.
1484. eṅkē puki uṇṭō aṅkē neruppu uṇṭu.
Wherever there is smoke there is fire.
1485. eccilit tiṉṟu ēppam iṭṭāṟpōla.
Like belching after eating leavings.
1486. eccilit tiṉṟu paci tīrumā ?
Can hunger be appeased by eating leavings?
1487. ecciṟkallikku maṇṇāṅkaṭṭiātaravu maṇṇāṅkaṭṭikku
ecciṟkalli ātaravu.
A clod is the support of the leaf-plate, and the leaf-plate is the
support of the clod.
1488.eccil irakka aṭikkum, paṟṟu paṟakka aṭikkum.
Spitting about will drive one to beggary, and ceremonial impurity will
scatter a family.
1489. ecciṟkiyāl kākki ōṭṭamāṭṭāṉ.
He will not scare away a crow with an unwashen hand.
1490. eccil eṭukkac coṉṉārkaḷā ettaṉipēr cāppiṭṭateṉṟu kaṇakkup pārkkac
coṉṉārkaḷā?
Did they order the leavings to be removed, or an account to be taken of
the number who had eaten?
1491. eccil tiṉṟālum vayiṟu niṟiyat tiṉṉa vēṇṭum, ēccukkēṭṭālum poḻutu
viṭikiṟa maṭṭum kēṭkavēṇṭum.
If you eat offal eat heartily, if you listen to abusive language, listen
till day-break.
1492. eccililikku nāy aṭittukkoṇṭu niṟkiṟatu pōla.
As dogs impatiently wait for leaf-plates.
1493. eccilik kaḻuvi uṉ cuttattilē vār.
Wash off the refuse and pour the water on your clean person.
Spoken in derision of affected cleanliness.
1494. eṭukka muṉṉam kaḻuti iṭuppu oṭintu viḻuntatām.
It is sid that the ass fell down with a broken hip as soon as it was
loaded.
1495. eṭukkap pōṉa cīmāṭṭi iṭuppu oṭintu viḻuntāḷām.
It is sid that the wealthy matron, whom one had gone to take in
marriage, fell down, her waist having broken.
1496. eṭuttālum paṅkāruppeṭṭiyi eṭukkavēṇṭum iruntālumciṅkārakkaḻuvil
irukkavēṇṭum.
If you steal, take away a golden casket;if you are impaled, endure the
punishment on an ornamented stake.
1497. eṭutta cumi cumantallō iṟakka vēṇṭum?
A burden must be first carried, must it not, before it is put down?
1498. eṭuttu āḷāta poruḷ utavātu.
Things unused, spoil.
1499. eṭutta aṭi maṭaṅkumā?
Will the lifted foot be drawn back?
1500. eṭuttuviṭṭa erutu ettaṉi nāḷ uḻum ?
How long can an ox plough that requires to be lifted up?
1501. eṭuppatu picci ēṟuvatu pallakku.
His occupation is begging, his conveyance a palanquin.
1502. eṭuppārum piṭippārum uṇṭāṉāl iḷippum tavippum uṇṭu.
Where many are in attendance to lift up and support, there will be
weariness and fainting.
1503. eṭuppār kik kuḻanti.
A baby in the arms.
1504. eṭuppār maḻuvit taṭuppār puliyik koṭuppār arumi.
There are who take up the battle axe, and there are who stop tigers, but
givers are scarce.
1505. eṭuppuṇṭa kalappi iruntu uḻumā?
Will a worn out plough last long?
1506. eṭṭāp paḻattip pārttuk koṭṭāvi viṭṭatu pōla.
As one gazed and gaped at a fruit beyond his reach.
1507. eṭṭāp pū tēvarkku eṭṭum pūtaṅkaḷukku.
Flowers beyond reach are sacred to God, but those within reach are for
themselves.
1508. eṭṭik kuṭṭi iṟaṅkik kālip piṭittukkoḷḷukiṟatā?
What! is it to reach up and cuff, and cower and cling to the feet ?
1509. eṭṭi paḻuttu eṉṉa , īyātār vāḻntu eṉṉa ?
What if the fruit of the etti tree (strychnos nux vomica) ripens ; of
what use is the prosperity of the niggardly?
1510. eṭṭimaramāṉālum pacceṉṟu irukkavēṇṭum.
Though a poisonous tree, it should be green.
1511. eṭṭi maramāṉālum vitta maratti veṭṭātē.
Cut not down the tree you planted though it is the (strychnosnux
vomica.)
I have observed among many natives a remarkable disinclination, to cut
down trees: though not rational creatures they are sid to have one
sense.aṟivu.
1512. eṭṭikkup pāl vārttu vaḷarttālum tittippu uṇṭākumā ?
Although you may nourish an etti tree by pouring milk at its root, will
it become sweet?
1513. eṭṭiyilē kaṭṭumāmpaḻam uṇṭāmō?
Will an etti tree bear graft-mangoes?
1514. eṭṭiyuṭaṉē cērnta ilavum tīppaṭum.
Even the silk-cotton tree growing by the etti will also be consumed by
fire.
Evil association brings destruction.
1515. eṭṭiṉāl, kuṭumiyip piṭikkiṟatu. eṭṭāviṭṭāl kālip piṭikkiṟatu.
If it can be reached, seizing by the kudumi knot of hir, if not,
clinging to the feet.
1516.eṭṭi eṭṭip pārttuk kuṭṭiccuvarilē muṭṭikkoḷḷalāmā ?
Can you strike your head aginst a dwarf wall when peeping on tiptoe?
1517.eṭṭi eṭṭip pārppārum ēṇi vittup pārppārum kuṭṭiccuvarālē
kuṉintu niṉṟu pārppārum uṇṭu.
There are who peep on tiptoe, there arewho peep on a ladder, there are
those who peep over a dwarf wall crouching.
1518. eṭṭimaramāṉālum vittavarkkup pakṣam.
Though an etti tree, he who planted it will like it.
1519. eṭṭippaḻatti iccikkiṟatā?
What, is an etti fruit to be desired?
1520. eṭṭuk kiḻavarum oru moṭṭik kiḻaviyik kaṭṭikkoṇṭārkaḷ.
Eight old men conjointly took to wife a bald-headed old woman.
1521. eṭṭuk kuñcu aṭittālum caṭṭikkaṟi ākātu.
Eight chickens are not sufficient for a chatty of curry.
1522. eṭṭu varuṣattu erumikkaṭā ērikkup pōka vaḻi tēṭukiṉṟatu.
A buffalo eight years old is at a loss to find its way to the tank.
1523. eṇcāṇ uṭampum oru cāṇāṉēṉ.
My eight-span body is reduced to one span.
1524. eṇcāṇ uṭampilē eḷḷattaṉi nāṇam illi.
There is not a grin of modesty in his eight-span body.
1525. eṇcāṇ uṭampiṟkum ciracē piratāṉam.
The head is the chief part of the eight-span body.
1526. eṇṇam ellām poy emaṉ ōli mey.
All our thoughts are vin, the ola of Yama is real.
Ola is the name of the Palmyra palm leaf. On this material, books,
accounts, correspondence &c., are written with an iron pen. Several of
the Tamil books I first read were written on ola. Yama, the regent of
the dead, is graphically represented as stretching out his hand to
deliver the order for departure.
1527. eṇṇa m ellām poy ēḷitam mey.
Expectations are all unreal, a despicable condition is a reality.
1528. eṇṇaṟak kaṟṟu eḻuttaṟa vācittālum peṇ putti piṉ puttiyē.
No matter how skilled a woman may be in numbers and letters, her
judgment will be second rate.
Many modern Hindus of the day(1873, though themselves earnest about
University honors, evince but very little interest regarding the
intellectual and moral culture of their dughters. The Rev R. M.
Banerjee, a learned brahman of Calcutta, very recently gave a sad
account of the disabilities to which native ladies in that city are
subject.
1529. eṇṇappaṭṭa kutiri ellām maṇṇip pōṭṭukkoḷḷa,
taṭṭuvāṇikkutiri vantu koḷḷukku aḻukiṟatām.
Whilst horses of the first blood liek the ground, the miserable tattoo
is neighing for gram.
1530. eṇṇam aṟṟa irācaṉ paṉṟivēṭṭi āṭiṉāṟpōl.
As an incutions king went to hunt wild hog.
1531. eṇṇam ellām poy eḻutiya eḻuttu mey.
All imaginations may be false, that which is written is true, certin.
1532. eṇ illātavar kaṇ illātavar, eḻuttu illātavar kaḻuttu illātavar.
Those who are ignorant of figures have no eyes, those who are ignorant
of letters have no head.
1533. eṇṇic ceykiṟavaṉ ceṭṭi eṇṇāmaṟ ceykiṟavaṉ maṭṭi.
He who acts after due consideration is a chitty a superior man, he who
acts without consideration is a matti a fool.
1534.eṇṇic ceyvatu ceṭṭu eṇṇāmaṟ ceyvatu vēḷāṇmi.
Trade requires forethought, agriculture requires none.
1535. eṇṇiya eṇṇam eṉṉaṭi aṇṇā eṉṟu aḻitta muṟi eṉṉaṭi?
What was our expectation, and what led you to call me elder brother?
1536. eṇṇit tuṇivatu karumam, tuṇintapiṉ eṇṇuvatu iḻukku.
An action undertaken after due consideration may prove successful,
consideration after action may end in disgrace.
1537. eṇṇiya kuṭikku oru miṉṉiya muṭi.
A brilliant crowd for an illustrious family.
1538. eṇṇey muntukiṟatō tiri muntukiṟatō?
It is questionable which will be first consumed, the oil or the wick.
1539. eṇṇey pōka muḻukiṉālum eḻuttup pōkat tēyppār uṇṭā ?
Although oil may be washed off, can the writing of Brahma be rubbed off?
1540. eṇṇeyc celavoḻiya piḷḷi piḻitta pāṭu illi.
As regards the child's recovery, nothing has resulted but an expenditure
of oil.
1541. eṇṇeyk kuṭattic cuṟṟum eṟumpu pōla.
Like ants round a pot of oil.
1542. eṇṇeyp piḷḷiyō vaṇṇap piḷḷiyō ?
In the beauty attributable to oil, or is it real ?
1543. eṇ mikuttavaṉē tiṇ mikuttavaṉē.
He who excels in figures excels in strength.
1544. etākutala pēciṉāl akappic cūṉiyam vippēṉ.
If you speak a word, I will place before you an empty ladle.
1545. etārttavāti veku caṉa virōti.
He who is truthful may be the enemy of many.
1546. etiri iḷippamāṉāl kōpam caṇṭappiracaṇṭam.
If the opposite party be inferior in position, the anger of his enemy
will rage like a tempest.
1547. etirikku iḷakkāramāyc collukiṟatā?
What, is it to yield to an opponent ?
1548. etirtta vīṭu ēkāli vīṭu, aṭutta vīṭu ampaṭṭaṉ vīṭu.
The house opposite is the washerman's, and my next door neighbour is a
barber.
1549. etirvīṭu ēkāli vīṭu, pakkattu vīṭu paṇi ceypavaṉ vīṭu, aṭutta vīṭu ampaṭṭaṉ
vīṭu.
The opposite house is the washerman's, the adjoining house is the
goldsmith's, and the next to mine is that of the barber.
1550. etirttavaṉ ēḻi eṉṟāl kōpam caṇṭāḷam.
When the opposed is poor, the anger of his opponent ends in cruelty.
1551. etu eppaṭip pōṉālum taṉ kāriyam taṉakku .
No matter how any thing may go, each should mind his own business.
1552. ettaṉitāṉ tulakkiṉālum pittaḷi nāṟṟam pōkumā?
No matter how frequently it may be polished, the bad odour of brass will
not leave it.
1553. ettaṉi puṭam iṭṭālum irumpu pacumpoṉ ākātu.
Though iron may be heated never so much, it will not become gold.
1554. ettaṉi ēḻi āṉālum elumiccaṅkāy attaṉi poṉ illāmaṟpōmā?
However poor one may be, will he not possess gold, at least of the value
of alemon ?
1555. ettāṟ piḻikkalām ottāṟ piḻikkalām.
How may we subsist ? if united we may subsist.
1556. ettilē piḷḷi peṟṟu iravalilē tālāṭṭukiṟatu.
Bringing forth a child without cost, and rocking it in a borrowed
cradle.
1557. ettēca kālamum vaṟṟāp peruñ camuttiram.
An ocean that knows no ebb anywhere at any time.
1558. enta āyutamum tīṭṭattīṭṭak kūrum.
The more an instrument is whetted, the sharper it becomes.
1559. enta ili utirntālum īccam ili utirātu.
Though the leaves of other trees may fall off, those of the date-palm
will not.
1560. entap puṟṟilē entap pāmpu irukkiṟatō teriyātu.
It is not known what species of snake may be found in any particular
hole.
1561. enta vākkup poyttālum cantavākkup poyyātu.
Whatever else may fail the words, marks, of low breeding will not fail.
1562. ennēramum avaḷ pēril kaṇṇāy irukkiṟāṉ.
His eye is always upon her.
1563. emaṉukku vaḻikāṭṭuvāṉ.
He can pilot Yama, the regent of the dead.
1564. emaṉ piḷḷiyip pēy piṭikkumā ?
Will a demon seize the child of Yama, the regent of the dead?
1565. eykiṟavaṉ eytāl ampu eṉṉa ceyyum?
What can the arrow do if discharged by a skilful archer ?
1566. eytavaṉ irukka ampi nōvāṉēṉ?
Why blame the arrow, letting the archer go free?
1567. erikiṟa koḷḷiyi ēṟat taḷḷiṉatupōla.
As a burning firebrand was pushed into the hearth.
1568. erikiṟa koḷḷiyil entak koḷḷi nalla koḷḷi?
Which of the burning firebrands is the best ?
1569. erikiṟatip piṭuṅkiṉāl kotikkiṟatu aṭaṅkum.
If the burning fuel be removed, the bubbling will cease.
1570. erikiṟa neruppi eṇṇey viṭṭu avikkalāmā?
May a burning fire be extinguished by pouring oil on it ?
1571. erikiṟa neruppilē ney viṭṭatu pōla.
As if ghee were poured on a flaming fire.
1572. erikiṟa vīṭṭi avikkak kiṇaṟu veṭṭa nāḷ pārttatu pōla.
Like seeking an auspicious day, to dig a well in order to quench the
flames of a house on fire.
1573. erikiṟa vīṭṭiṟ piṭuṅkukiṟatu lāpam.
Whatever is abstracted from a burning house is a gin.
1574. erutiṉ puṇṇiṟkuc cāmpal maruntu.
Ashes are medicine for the sores of a bull.
1575. erutukkum taṉ puṇ aḻaṟci kākkikkum taṉ paci aḻaṟci.
The buffalo suffers from the smarting of its sores, and the crow that
picks them suffers from the smarting of hunger.
1576. erutu koḻuttāl toḻuvattil irātu, paṟiyaṉ koḻuttāl pāyil irāṉ.
If an-ox grow fat, it will not remin in a stall; if a Pariah becomes
rich, he will not sit on a mat.
1577. erutu nōyi niṉikkum, kākki paciyi niṉikkum.
The ox feels the pin, the crow its hunger.
1578. erutu nōy kākkikkut teriyumā?
Is a crow aware what pin it occasions when picking the sore of an ox?
1579. erutu īṉṟatu eṉṟāl tōḻattilē kaṭṭu eṉkiṟatu.
If it be reported, that the bull has a calf, he will say, O, tie it up
in the stall.
1580. erutu keṭṭārkkum eṭṭē kaṭukkāy, iḷampiḷḷittāyccikkum eṭṭē kaṭukkāy.
Giving eight gall nuts to him who has lost a ball, and eight to a woman
in the first stage of pregnancy.
1581. erutu vāṅkumuṉṉē puṉceykku uḻu eṉkiṟāy.
You bid me plough the dry land before the ox is bought.
1582. erutu niṉitta iṭattilē tōḻam kaṭṭukiṟatā ?
Is its shed put up where the bull may think fit ?
1583. erutu kōpaṅkoṇṭu paratēcam pōṉatu pōla.
As a bull went away in a rage to a foreign country.
1584. erutu maṟiviṟ pullut tiṉkiṟāyā?
Dost thou eat grass screened by a bull ?
1585. erutukku nōy vantāl koṭṭāyic cuṭukiṟatā?
Is its shed to be burnt down becuse the ox is sick ?
1586. erutum vaṇṭiyum ottāl mēṭu paḷḷam ētu?
What matters the ruggedness of the road if the bullocks and bandy a
country cart-hold together?
1587. erumikkaṭā eṉṟālum kuḻantikkup pāl oru pīr illiyā eṉkiṟāy. Though it
is a male buffalo, you ask me if there be not a drop of milk for the
child.
1588. erumi vāṅkumuṉ ney vili kūṟukiṟāy.
You publish the price of ghee before buying a buffalo.
1589. erumik kompu naṉikiṟataṟkumuṉṉē eḻupatu taram maḻi varukiṟatu.
It will rin seventy times before a buffalo's horns will be wet.
1590. erumik kōmayam ekkiyattiṟku ākumā?
Will the dung of the buffalo serve for sacrificial purposes ?
1591. erumi māṭṭiṉ mēl maḻi peytatupōla.
As it rined on a buffalo.
1592. erumi māṭṭiṉ mēl ettaṉi cūṭu iruntālum teriyātu, pacumāṭṭil oru cūṭu
iruntālum teriyum.
Brands on a buffalo however numerous will scarcely appear, but a single
brand on a cow will be visible.
1593. erumi iruntallō pāl kaṟakkavēṇṭum?
We may milk may we not, provided there be a buffalo ?
1594. erumiyilum veḷḷāṭu ēṟakkaṟakkumā ?
Will a goat yield more milk than a buffalo ?
1595. eruvukkup pōṉavaṉ iḷiyāḷik kippiṭittāṟpōla.
As he who went in search of manure, seized the hand of a miden.
1596. eruvukkup pōṉavaṉ elumiccampaḻam eṭuttatu pōla.
As he who went to pick up cow-dung, gathered lemons.
1597. eli ammaṇattōṭē pōkiṉṟatu eṉkiṟāṉ.
He says that the rat goes naked.
1598. eli aḻutāl pūṉi viṭumā?
Will the cat leave its hold on the crying of the rat ?
1599. eli aḻutu pulampiṉālum pūṉi piṭittatu viṭātu.
Although the rat may cry and lament, the cat will not relinquish its
hold.
1600. eli aṟukkum tūkkamāṭṭātu.
The rat nibbles the grin, but it does not carry off the basket.
1601. eli irukkiṟa iṭattil pāmpu irukkum.
Where there is a rat, there will also be a snake.
1602. elikkup payantu vīṭṭic cuṭṭatu pōla.
Like burning down the house for fear of rats.
1603. elikkup pirāṇāvasti pūṉikkuk koṇṭāṭṭam.
The death-struggle of a rat is the sport of a cat.
1604. elikku aṉukūlam pāmpu piṭāraṉukku añcutal, eḷiyārkku aṉukūlam valiyār
irācaṉukku añcutal.
The snake's fear of him who catches it, is favourable to the rat, when
the strong fear the king, the weak are benefited.
1605. elikku maṇiyam cuvari aṟukkiṟatu tāṉ.
The business of the rat is to burrow in the wall.
1606. eṉ taliyilē kōṭāli viḻuntatu pōla.
As an axe fell on the head of the rat.
1607. eli taliyil kōpuram iṭintu viḻuntāṟpōla.
As if the tower of the temple should fall on the head of a rat.
1608. eli pūṉiyi vellumā?
Can a rat overcome a cat?
1609. eli pūṉikkuc calām paṇṇuvatu pōla.
As a rat makes salaam to a cat.
1610. elippiḻukki iṟappil iruntu eṉṉa, varappil iruntu eṉṉa ?
What matters it whether the droppings of a rat are in the inside of a
sloping roof, or on the ridge of a cornfield ?
1611. eliyum pūṉiyum pōla irukkiṟatu.
Living like a rat and a cat.
1612. eliyum pūṉiyum iṇintu viḷiyāṭiṉatupōla.
As the rat and the cat united in sport.
1613. eliyik kaṇṭu pūṉi ēkkam aṭiyumā ?
Will the cat be alarmed at the sight of a rat ?
1614. eliyik kaṇṭu pūṉi ēṅki ēṅkik kiṭakkumō?
Will a cat be greatly alarmed at the sight of a rat?
1615. elivaḷi āṉālum taṉivaḷi vēṇṭum.
A separate hole is to be preferred though it be a rat-hole.
One's own cot is desirable however humble.
1616. eli vīṭu kaṭṭap pāmpu kuṭikoḷḷum.
A rat makes the hole, a snake inhabits it.
1617. elivēṭṭikkut tavil aṭippārā?
Do they beat a tomtom when hunting rats?
1618. elumiccaṅkāykkup puḷi ēṟṟukiṟatu pōla.
Like infusing acidity into a lemon.
1619. elumiccañ ceṭikku erup pōṭṭāṟpōla.
Like manuring a lemon tree.
1620. elumpu kaṭikkiṟa nāykkup paruppuc cōṟu ēṉ?
Why give pulse to a dog that gnaws bones ?
1621. elumpu kaṭikkiṟa nāy irumpu kaṭikkumā?
Can a dog that gnaws bones bite iron?
1622. elumpu illā nā ellām pēcum.
A boneless tongue may say any thing.
1623. ellām aṟintavaṉum illi, oṉṟum aṟiyātavaṉum illi.
No one knows everything, nor is any one ignorant of everything.
1624. ellām irukkiṟatu peṭṭiyilē ilikkaṟi kaṭiyac caṭṭi illi.
Everything wanted is in the box, but there is no chatty in which to
macerate the vegetable curry.
1625. ellāt taliyilum eṭṭu eḻuttu, eṉ taliyilē pattu eḻuttu.
On all heads there are eight letters, on mine there are ten.
1626. ellārukkum collumām palli, tāṉ pōyk kāṭippāṉiyil viḻumām.
It is sid thut the lizard which uttered prognostications, will go and
fall into the refuse pan.
1627. ellā macakamum cāyantarattiṟṟāṉ varukiṟatu.
It is in the evening that all the mosquitos come.
1628. ellārukkum caṉi turumpu pōla, eṉakkuc caṉi malipōla.
Saturn who is a more straw to all other men, to me is as a mountin.
1629. ellārum pākku ivaṉ oru tōppu.
All other men are areca-nuts, but this man is a grove of areca
trees.
1630. ellārum taṭukkiṉ kīḻ nuḻintāl avaṉ kōlattiṉ kīḻ nuḻivāṉ.
If all can hide themselves under a small mat, he can conceal himself
under the ornamental drawing on the floor.
The kōlam here mentioned is a floor ornament very commonly formed at the
entrance of a native house : the form is circular. These ornaments are
renewed every morning. They contin various devices. In some a temple
car as delineated, in others trees, birds, flowers. They are generally
striking becuse the combinations indicate considerable skill. It may be
that they are connected with the prevalent ideas regarding the evil eye.
1631. ellāt tāṭṭōṭṭum kullāvukkuḷḷē irukkiṟatu.
All the skill in steering centres in the out-rigger.
Out-rigger boats or canoes are common on the south western coast of
Ceylon. They are very swift and most safe. In 1848 when at Colombo duty
called me dily to a largo ship at the time out in tho offing anchored
in seven fathoms of water. On the occasion under notice I went on board
in an ordinary Cingalese out-rigger canoe. In the evening after having
completed the berths, for my two dughters who were going home for
education, I betook myself to the boat, and soon after we got clear of
the ship the boat was struck in the stern, filled, and went down. When I
emerged from the waves I found myself near one of the two brackets of
the out-rigger. The canoe was submerged but could not sink lower than
the ends of the brackets tied to her side. The out-rigger at the other
end of the brackets was floating on the surface of the sea, the canoe
being suspended by it like a pir of scales from a beam. Aided by one of
those brackets, which I pressed to my bosom, and a Chubb's Palanquin
box, I gined the rocks soon after sunset and escaped to land.
1632. ellā ōṭṭum kullāvilē.
All our progress depends on the outrigger.
1633. ellārum nallārā, kal ellām māṇikkamā?
Are all men good men, are all stones rubies ?
1634. ellā vēliyum ceyvāṉ, cettāl piḻikkamāṭṭāṉ.
He can do all things, but when dead he cannot resuscitate himself.
1635. ellārum kappal ēṟiyāyiṟṟu iṉi ammāṉār poṟpaṭṭam kaṭṭappōkiṟār.
All have embarked, my uncle is about to receive a golden mark of
distinction.
Spoken of aspirations beyond one's merits.
1636. ellārum ēṟi iḷitta kutiriyil cāstiriyār ēṟic caṟukki viḻuntār.
On mounting a jadod horse that all had ridden, the astrologer slipped
and fell down.
1637. ellām aṟintum, kaḻunīrppāṉiyil kiyiṭukiṟatā?
What, is one who knows all things, to put his hand into filthy water?
1638. elli pāḻpaṭṭālum kollikkaṭami viṭār.
Though the landmarks be destroyed, they will not remit the ground-rent.
1639. evaṉ ākilum tāṉ cāka maruntu uṇpāṉā?
Will any one takemedicine to poison, himself?
1640. eḻutāk kaṭaṉukku aḻutāl tīrumā?
Will crying avilfor money lent of which no account has been kept?
1641. eḻuti vaḻaṅkāṉ vāḻkki kaḻuti puraṇṭa kaḷam.
The condition of the man who keeps no accounts is like the place in
which an ass has rolled itself.
1642. eḻutiya viti aḻutāl tīrumā?
Will destiny be averted by weeping ?
1643. eḻutuvatu arumi eḻutiṉatip paḻutaṟa vācippatu arumi.
Writing is important, reading correctly what is written, is more so.
1644. eḻutukiṟatu peritu alla, iṉam aṟintu cērkkiṟatu peritu.
To be able to write is no great matter; to compose harmoniously is
great.
1645. eḻuttu aṟinta vaṇṇāṉ kuṟittu eṟintāṉ ōli.
The washerman who had learnt his letters threw away his ola.
1646. eḻuttu aṟinta maṉṉaṉ kiḻittu eṟintāṉ ōliyi.
The king who had learnt his letters tore and threw away his ola.
It would appear that the two preceding proverbs relate to personswho
suppose that ability to read supercedes all records.
1647. eḻuttukkup pāl māṟiṉa kaṇakkaṉum uṭukkikkup pālmāṟiṉa tāciyum varuttam
aṭivār.
The curmum who neglects his accounts, and the dancing girl who is
careless about her dress, will come to grief.
1648. eḻuntiruppāṉ kāl illi.
He would rise, but he has no feet.
1649. eḻupatu ceṉṟālum paṟi ēviṉāṟṟāṉ ceyyum.
Though seventy years old, a Pariah will do nothing unless he is
prompted.
1650. eḻupatu pērik koṉṟa paṭu nīli .
A cruel Nili-womam - who killed seventy persons.
1651. eḻikkum pēḻikkum kāṭukāḷ ammi.
The mother of Vairavi is the patroness of the poor and of boats.
1652. eḷiyavaḷ piḷḷi āṉālum ceyyum caṭaṅku ceyyavēṇṭum.
Though the child of a poor woman, the accustomed rites must beperformed.
1653. eḷiyavaṉi aṭittup puḷiyaṅkāy paṟittāṟpōla.
Like beating a poor man, and plucking his tamarind fruit.
1654. eḷiyavaṉukkup peṇṭāy irukkiṟatilum valiyavaṉukku aṭimi ākiṟatu nallatu.
It is better to be the slave of the wealthy, than the wife of the
indigent.
1655. eḷiyavaṉik kaṇṭu vāyāl ēykkiṟāṉ.
Seeing that the man is poor, he deceives him by fair words.
1656. eḷiyavaṉ peṇṭāṭṭi ellārukkum mittuṉicci.
The wife of a poor man is sister-in-law to all.
1657. eḷiyāri valiyār aṭittāl valiyārit teyvam aṭikkum.
If the powerful oppress the weak, God will punish them in return.
1658. eḷiyāri etir iṭṭukkoṇṭāl pirāṇhāṉi.
Inferiors if opposed become mortal enemies.
1659. eḷ attaṉiyi mali attaṉi ākkukiṟatu.
Magnifying a rape seed into a mountin.
1660. eḷḷukkāy piḷanta vivakāram.
A dispute as easy to decide as the splitting of a rape seed.
1661. eḷḷukkāy piḷantāṟpōlap pēcavēṇṭum.
One ought to speak as evenly as a rape-pod splits.
1662. eḷḷukkut takka eṇṇey, eḷḷukkut takka piṇṇākku.
Oil is proportioned to the sessamum, refuse proportioned to the pressed
seed.
1663. eḷḷukkup puḷḷi varum ecciṟku eṟumpu varum.
Birds are attracted by rape seed, ants by leavings.
1664. eḷḷukkuḷ eṇṇey pōla.
As oil in sessamum seed.
1665. eḷḷukkuḷ eṇṇey pōl eṅkum niṟintirukkum.
Everywhere present as oil throughout the sesamum seed.
1666. eḷḷukku ēḻu uḻavu koḷḷukku ōr uḻavu.
Ploughing seven times for sesamum seed, once for gram.
1667. eḷḷuttāṉ eṇṇeykku ularukiṟatu, elippiḻukki eṉṉattukku ularukiṟatu?
Sesamum seed is dried for oil; but why dry rat-dung ?
1668. eḷḷum pacci ariciyum pōla irukkavēṇṭum.
It must be as sesamum seed and raw rice.
1669. eḷḷum karumpum iṟukkiṉāl payaṉ tarum.
Sesamum seed and sugarcane yield a profit when pressed.
1670. eḷḷukku ēḻu uḻavu uḻukiṟavēḷi veḷḷāḷar koḷḷukku ōr uḻavu uḻutu
payircey.
O, Vellala who ploughest seven times to sow rape-seed, plough once for
horse-gram and thus cultivate the ground.
1671. eṟikiṟatu muyalukkup paṭukiṟatu paṟṟikku.
Throwing at a hare and hitting a bush.
1672. eṟivāṉēṉ coṟivāṉēṉ?
Why throw, why scratch?
Why provoke a nuisance?
1673. eṟumpu kaṭikkap poṟukkātā?
Canst thou not bear the bite of an ant?
1674. eṟumpiṉ kaṇ ataṉ aḷavukkup peritu; āṉiyiṉ kaṇataṉ aḷavukkuc ciṟitu.
The eyes of an ant are large in proportion to its size, the eyes of an
elephant are small for its size.
1675. eṟumpukkuk koṭṭāṅkaccit taṇṇīr camuttiram.
A cocoanut shell-full of water is an ocean to an ant.
1676. eṟumpup puṟṟil pāmpu kuṭi koḷvatu pōla.
As a snake occupies an ant-hole.
1677. eṟumpukkum taṉ kiyāl eṇcāṇ uṭampu.
Even an ant is eight spans long as measured by its own hand.
1678. eṟumpu ūrak kal tēyum.
By the continual creeping of ants a stone will wear away.
1679. eṟumpiṉ kaṇṇukku erumi mūttiram ēkapperuveḷḷam.
The urine of the buffalo is as a perfect deluge in the eye of an ant.
1680. eṟumpu ūrak kal kuḻiyum.
By the continual creeping of ants a stone will become hollow.
1681. eṟumpu ūra iṭaṅkoṭuttāl erutum potiyum uḷḷē celuttuvāṉ.
If room be given sufficient for ants to creep in, he will drivea loaded
bullock that way.
1682. eṟumpu eṭuttup pōvataṟkut taṭi eṭuttu niṟkiṟatu, periya pūcaṉikkāy
pōkiṟatu teriyātā?
You stand armed with a club to watch that which may be carried away by
ants, are you unable to see when a large pumpkin hasbeen taken away?
1683. eṟumpu mutal eṇṇāyiram kōṭikkum teriyum.
It is known to the eighty thousand millions of creatures from an ant
upwards.
1684. eṟumpu muṭṭi koṇṭu tiṭṭi ēṟiṉ maḻi peyyum.
If ants carry their eggs to a higher place, it will rin.
1685. eṉakku aṭṭamattuc caṉi.
With me Saturn is in the eighth sign.
1686. eṉakku ūṇum illi uṟakkamum illi.
I have neither food nor sleep.
1687. eṉatu nāṭkaḷ ellām ūmi kaṇṭa kaṉāppōl āyiṉa.
My days are like the dream of the dumb.
1688. eṉ īralik karukku arivāḷ koṇṭu aṟukkiṟatu.
It cuts my liver with a sharp sickle.
1689. eṉ kāriyam ellām nantaṉ paṭivīṭāyp pōyiṟṟu.
My affairs are like Nandan's camp.
The name of a shoemaker who is reputed to have reigned as a kingfor
three hours, and to have issued leather coin.
1690. eṉ kuṭi keṭṭatum uṉ kuṭi keṭṭatum poḻutu viṭintāl teriyum.
It will be knownat day-break whether my family or your's has
been ruined.
1691. eṉpit tiṉṟu catiyik koṭuttu vaḷarttāḷ.
Eating bones herself, she has brought up her children on flesh.
1692. eṉ makaḷ vārattōṭē vāram muḻukuvāḷ, eṉ marumakaṉtīpāvaḷikkut tīpāvaḷi
muḻukuvāṉ.
My dughter bathes once a week, my son-in-law bathes at depaveli.
1693. eṉ marumakaṉukku vēppeṇṇeyām tūkkeṇṇey.
Superior oil is sid tobe Margosa oil to my son-in-law.
1694. eṉ mukattiṟ karipūciṉāyē.
Thou hast smeared my face with charcoal.
1695. eṉ mutukiṉ tōl uṉakkuc ceruppāy irukkum.
The skin of my back will serve you for shoes.
1696. eṉ vīṭṭukku vantāl eṉṉa koṇṭu varukiṟāy, uṉ vīṭṭukku vantāl eṉṉa
koṭukkiṟāy?
When you come to my house, what do you bring, when I come to
yours, what do you give ?
1697. eṉṟum maṟintiruppatilum etirē pōtal uttamam.
It is better tocome to the front at once than to be always concealed.
1698. eṉṟu niṉṟum poṉṟuvar ōr nāḷ.
No matter how long one may live, the day of death will come.
1699. eṉṉa coṉṉālum eṉ putti pōkātu.
Say what you may, I will not change my mind.
1700. eṉṉaṭā tātā puraṭṭāci mātam muppatum oru kantāyam.
O! mendicant, the thirty days of September are all days of receipt.
1701. eṉṉaṭā kuccuk kaṭṭip pēcukiṟāy.
You fellow, you affect to speak in an elegant style.
1702. eṉṉamāyc colli itamāy urittālum kaḻutikku upatēcam kātil ēṟātu.
No instruction however explicit or agreeable will enter the ear of an
ass.
1703. eṉṉa māyam iṭicci māyam, mōrōṭu taṇṇīr kalanta māyam.
What is the deception practised by the diry mid ? It is mixingwater
with butter-milk.
1704. eṉṉaṭā keṭṭuppōkiṟāy eṉṟāl, iṉṉamum keṭṭuppōkiṟēṉ pantayam pōṭu
eṉkiṟāṉ.
If one say, fie on thee, thou art destroying thyself, he replies, I will
take a bet that I will exceed my former course.
1705. eṉṉa tiṉṟālum ataṟku mēlē nālu pērīccampaḻam tiṉṉa ellām aṭipaṭum.
No matter what may be eaten; if four dates be taken afterwards,the whole
will be digested.
1706. eṉṉuṭiya vīṭṭiṟkup pūvāy varap poṉṉum turumpāccu.
Since the woman came to my house, even gold has become a common thing.
1707. eṉṉippōlak kuralum eṉ akkāḷippōla oyilum illi eṉkiṟatām kaḻuti.
The ass boasted that there was no voice equal to his, and no git equal
to that of his elder sister.
1708. eṉṉik kaṇṭāl caṇalukkuḷḷē oḷikkiṟāy, eṉ peṇ cātiyik kaṇṭāl caṭṭikkuḷḷē
oḷikkiṟāy.
On seeing me, thou hidest among the hemp, and on seeing my wife, thou
hidest in a chatty.
1709. ēkātacikku mā iṭittāṟpōla.
Like pounding flour for Ekadasi.
Ekadasi, the eleventh of December which is observed by the Hindus as a
fast and therefore the preparation of food is not required. Said of
something irrelevant.
1710. ēkātacit tiruṭiyi ēṟṟaṭā ratattil
O fellow, put the pretended observer of Ekadasi on the car.
1711. ēkāli vākaṉam poti cumantāṟpōla.
As a Washerman's vehicle (a donkey) carries its load.
1712. ēccilum pēccilum vallavaṉē.
He is indeed clever in abuse and empty talk.
1713. ēṭā kūṭakkāraṉukku vaḻi eṅkē eṉṟāl, pōkiṟavaṉ tali mēlām.
If it be asked where is the way for the stubborn, it is replied, on the
head of the way faring man.
1714. ēṭu aṟiyātāṉ pīṭu peṟātāṉ.
The ignorant man is not held in estimation.
1715. ēṭṭuc curikkāy kaṟikku ākumā?
Will the word suriki a gourd serve as a curry?
1716. ēṇikkompukku kōṇakkompu pōṭalāmā?
Is a crooked pole fit for a ladder?
1717. ēṇikkaḻikkuk kōṇaṟkaḻi veṭṭukiṟatā?
Do you cut a crooked stick for a litter?
1718. ēṇṭā teṉṉamarattil ēṟiṉāy eṉṟāl, kaṉṟukkuṭṭikkup pul piṭuṅka eṉṟāṉ.
You fellow! why did you go up the Cocoanut tree! when thus addressed, he
replied, I went to get grass for the calf.
1719. ēṇṭā karuṭā cukamā? irukkiṟa iṭattil iruntāl cukam tāṉ.
O Garuda, are you well? I should be well enough if I were in the place
where I ought to be.
1720. ēṇṭā paṭṭappakalil tiruṭukiṟāy? eṉ avacaram uṉakkut teriyumā ?
What! do you steal in broad day light? He replies, do you know how
pressing my necessities are ?
1721. ēṇṭā puḷiyamarattil ēṟiṉāy? pūṉikkuṭṭikkup pul paṟikka.
Why, man, have you got up into the Tamarind tree? he replied, to pluck
grass for my kitten.
1722. ēṇṭā muṭiccu aviḻkkiṟāy? eṉpaci uṉakkut teriyumā?
Why, you fellow, do you untie the knot? Do you know how hungryI am ?
1723. ēṇṭi ciṟukki pullu āccā? oru noṭikku muṉ kaṭṭāccē.
Well, my girl, have you cut the grass? she replied, it was tied up
before one could snap his fingers.
1724. ēṇṭi peṇṇē cōrntirukkiṟāy? cōṟu pattiyam.
Why, my girl, do you faint? I have not had rice enough.
1725. ēṇṭi peṇṇē kuntiyirukkiṟāy? cōṟu paṟṟāmal.
Why my girl do you squat ? For want of sufficient rice.
1726. ētu piriyam ? illātatu piriyam.
What do you wish? That which I have not.
1727. ētum aṟṟavaṉukku ēṉ iraṇṭu peṇṭāṭṭi?
Why two wives to one who has nothing?
1728. ēteṉṟu kēṭpārum illi, eṭuttup piṭippārum illi.
None to enquire, none to help.
1729. ēri uṭikiṟataṟku muṉṉē aṇi pōṭavēṇṭum.
Before the bund bursts, it must be strengthened.
1730. ēri niṟintāl kari kaciyum.
If the tank be full, its bank will be moist.
1731. ēri perukil eṅkum perukku.
When the tank overflows, the flood will spread all around.
1732. ēri mitantāl iṭiyaṉi matiyātu.
If the hunch of the ox grow high, he will not fear his keeper.
1733. ēriyiṉ nīrik kaṭṭutal aritu uṭittal eḷitu.
It is difficult to confine the water of a tank, but easy to break the
bund.
1734. ēriyiṉ taṇṇīr itō cūriyatēvā ?
O divine sun, behold the water of the tank?
1735. ēriyōṭu paki koṇṭu cavucam ceyyātirukkiṟatā?
Do you abstin from ablution becuse you are dissatisfied with the tank?
1736. ēr uḻukiṟa piḷḷi iḷittu pōṉāl pōkiṟatu, pariyam pōṭṭa peṇṇip pārttu
vaḷar.
Never mind, should the youth at the plough become lean, take care of the
girl who has received the nuptial presents.
1737. ēr piṭittavaṉ eṉṉa ceyvāṉ pāṉi piṭittavaḷ pākkiyam.
Comfort depends on her who has charge of the cooking pots, not on the
ploughman.
1738. ēlavē tolintatu eṅkaḷit toṭṭa karmam.
The sin which affected us is already removed.
1739. ēlēlam! ēlēlam! erumiccāṇi kāykiṟatu.
Elelam, Elelam, the buffalo dung is drying.
The sound elelam is uttered by boatmen and others on the Coromandel
coast when palling together, as savas is, by boatmen on the Hoogly: Some
derive it from valikkiṟatu.
1740. ēlēlaciṅkaṉ poruḷ ēḻu kaṭaliṟ pōṉālum tirumpum.
The property of Elelasingam, though it pass over seven seas, will
return.
Elelasingam a wealthy merchant, the disciple of Tiruvallurar.
1741. ēvaṟ pēy kūriyip piṭuṅkum.
The excited demon will take off the thatch.
1742. ēvā makkaḷ mūvā maruntu.
Children who do their duty unprompted are as a life-preserving remedy.
1743. ēvukiṟavaṉukku vāyccol, ceykiṟavaṉukkut taliccumi.
To the taskmaster a word, to the servant a burden on the head.
1744. ēḻi aḻuta kaṇṇīr kūriyavāḷi okkum.
The tears of the poor are as sharp swords.
1745. ēḻi pākkut tiṉṉa eṭṭu vīṭu aṟiyavēṇṭumā?
Becuse the poor man uses betel-nut, is it to be made known at eight
houses?
1746. ēḻi pēccu araṇmaṉikku ēṟumā?
Will the speech of the poor go up to the palace ?
1747. ēḻip piḷḷikkut teyvamē tuṇi.
God is the helper of the helpless child.
1748. ēḻip piḷḷikku evarkaḷum tuṇi.
Every one is a helper to a helpless child.
1749. ēḻiyik kaṇṭāl mōḻiyum pāyum.
Even a beast without horns will attack the poor.
1750. ēṟac coṉṉāl erutukkuk kōpam, iṟaṅkac coṉṉāl noṇṭikkuk kōpam.
When told to mount, the bull is angry, when told to dismount, the lame
man is dissatisfied.
1751. ēṟappaṭāta marattilē eṇṇappaṭāta māṅkāy.
Innumerable mangoes on a tree no one can climb.
1752. ēṟa viṭṭu ēṇiyi vāṅkukiṟatā?
What ! remove the ladder after allowing one to mount ?
1753. ēṟiṉa kompāl iṟaṅka vēṇṭum.
One must come down on the branch by which he ascended.
1754. ēṟukiṟa kutiriyilum uḻavumāṭu atika uttamam.
A plough bull is superior to a saddle horse.
1755. ēṟu neṟṟi cūṟutali etirkka vantāl ākātu.
It is a bad omen to meet one with a high forehead or curly hir.
1756. ēṟumāṟāyp pēcukiṟatā?
What, is it to speak at random?
1757. ēṟum tēmal iṟaṅkum paṭartāmari kūṭum puruvam kuṭiyik keṭukkum. Ascending
cutaneous spots, descending ring-worm and eyebrows contiguous are
ominous.
1758. ēṟkavē māmi pēykkōlam atilum koñcam mākkōlam.
The mother-in-law is frightfully ugly already, and the flour on her face
makes her more so.
1759. ēṟkaṉavē varukiṟa vikkiṉaṅkaḷit taṭukkavēṇṭum.
Approaching evils should be provided aginst in due time.
1760. ēṟṟakkōlukkup piṭittāl arivāṭpiṭikku varum.
If you measure sufficient for a well-bucket pole, it may suffice for the
handle of a sickle.
1761. ēṟṟappāṭṭukku etirppāṭṭā?
Is the song of the picotta responsive?
1762. ēṉ koḻukkaṭṭi cavukkiṭṭāy? oru kācu vellam illāmaṟcavukkiṭṭēṉ !
Cake! why so insipid? Becuse I lack a cash-worth of sugar.
1763. aiṅkalak kappiyil naḻuviṉa kappi ivaṉ.
He is a broken grin fallen out of five kalams.
One of a large number of insignificant persons.
1764. aiṅkātam pōṉālum akappē arikkācu kiṭiyātē.
Though you may go fifty miles, you will not fetch half a cash.
1765. aiṅkātam pōṉālum, akappi arikkācu.
Although it may go fifty miles, an agappi will fetch but half a cash.
1766. aiṅkātam pōṉālum aṟimukam vēṇṭum.
Though one goes fifty miles off, an acquaintance is needed.
1767. aiṅkātam pōṉālum, taṉ pāvam taṉṉōṭē.
Though he may go fifty miles, his own sin will still cleave to him.
1768. aiṅkāyam iṭṭa kāram iṭṭālum taṉ nāṟṟam pōkātāmpēyccurikkāykku.
Though cooked with five ingredients, the wild gourd will not lose its
odour.
1769. aitatu nel, aṭarnta tu cuṟṟam.
In rice plants, distance is required, in kindred, closeness.
1770. aintu ciṭṭukku iraṇṭu kācu vili.
The price of five chits a small bird is two cash.
1771. aippaci mātam aḻukit tūṟal kārttiki mātam kaṉa maḻi.
In October drizzling, in November heavy rin.
1772. aippaci mātattu veyyalil aṉṟu uritta tōl aṉṟu kāyum.
In the sunshine of October a skin dries the day it is stript off.
1773. aippacikkum kārttikikkum maḻi illāviṭṭāl aṇṇaṉukkum cari tampikkum cari.
If the rins of October and November fail, the elder and younger brother
will be on a par.
1774. aippaci mātattu erumikkaṭāvum mārkaḻi mātattu nampiyāṉum cari.
A buffalo in the month of October and a Vaishnava brahman in December
are alike.
1775. aimpatilē aṟivu, aṟupatilē aṭakkam, aṟupatukku mēl oṉṟumilli.
At fifty discrimination, at sixty moderation, after sixty no
distinguishing characteristic.
1776. aimpatu vayatāṉavaṉukku aintu vayatup peṇṇā ?
Is a girl of five fit to be the wife to a man of fifty ?
1777. aiyaṅkārum tottuk koṭuppār.
Even the Aiyangar may communicate contagious disease.
1778. aiyappaṭṭāl piya naṭa.
If in doubt, advance slowly.
1779. aiyappaṉ kutiriyi viyāḷi viṭṭa kati.
Like the story of one who rode Aiyanar's horse.
1780. aiyamāṉa kāriyattic ceyyal ākātu.
To do a doubtful thing is bad.
1781. aiyam ēṟṟum aṟivē ōtu.
Though reduced to beggary, learn to be wise.
1782. aiyar eṉpavar tuyyar āvar.
T'hose who are entitled to be called brahmans are holy.
1783. aiyar varukiṟavariyil amāvāciyi niṟkumā ?
Will the new moon await the brahman's arrival ?
1784. aiyar katir pōla ammāḷ kutir pōla.
The husband is like an ear of corn, the wife is like a rice bin or grin
receptacle.
1785. aiyaṉār paṭiyiṟ kuyavaṉār paṭṭatu pōla.
As the potter perished in the army of Aiyanar.
1786 aiyaṉār kōvil ceṅkal attaṉiyum teyvam.
The bricks of Aiyanar's temple are so many gods.
1787. aiyaṉār kōvil maṇṇi mitittavar attaṉiyum piṭāri.
Those who have trodden the ground of Aiyanar's temple are so many
Pidaris.
1788. aiyaṉārē vārum kaṭāvik kikkoḷḷum.
Come, Aiyanar, and accept a goat, a sacrifice.
1789. aiyaṉ amippi ārālum taḷḷakkūṭātu.
No one may dispute the pre-ordination of god.
1790. aiyō eṉṟāl ivaṉukku āṟu mācattup pāvam cuṟṟum.
The man who expresses commiseration for him will incur six months sin.
1791. okkap piṟanta taṅki ōlamiṭṭu aḻaccē oppārit taṅkikkuc
ciṟṟāṭiyām?
When one's sister is weeping for a cloth, is it to be given to a woman
who resembles her?
1792. occiyam illāta ūrilē peṇ vāṅkiṉa kati.
The story of a man taking a girl to wife in a village inhabited by the
shameless.
1793. oṭinta kōl āṉālum ūṉṟukōl irukkavēṇṭum.
Though a broken one, a walking stick is necessary.
1794. oṭṭikkoṇṭu vantum taṭṭik kaḻikkiṟāṉ.
Though I cling to him, he repels me.
1795. oṭṭiṉāl toṭṭilum koḷḷum oṭṭāviṭṭāl kaṭṭilum koḷḷātu.
If compressed the crib will hold it, but if not even a bedstead will not
contin it.
1796. oṭṭiṉālum uḻakkuppīrc ceṉkiṟatā?
Is it to say draw one ulak of milk though the animal is lean ?
1797. oṭṭikkup paḷuvu ēṟṟukiṟatu pōla.
As a camel is loaded.
1798. oti peruttut tūṇāmā?
Though the odina tree grow large, will it do for a pillar ?
1799. oti peruttāl uralāmā?
Though the odina tree grow large, will it serve for a mortar ?
1800. oti peruttu eṉṉa, upakāram illātavaṉ vāḻntu eṉṉa ?
What avils the growth of an odina tree? Of what use is the prosperity
of the ungenerous ?
1801. otiyamaram tūṇāmō? ōṭṭāṅkiḷiñcil kācāmō?
Will an odina tree do for a pillar? will a broken oyster-shell pass as a
coin?
1802. otiyamaramum oru camayattukku utavum.
Even an odina tree may prove useful on an emergency.
1803. oyyārak koṇṭiyām tāḻampūvām uḷḷē mēyumām īrum pēṉum.
Her tresses are graceful and ornamented with the flowers of the screw
pine, but nits and lice breed therein.
1804. oru uṟiyilē iraṇṭu kattiyā?
Are two swords contined in one sheath?
1805. oru ūrukku oru vaḻiyā ?
Is there but one way to a village?
1806. oru kaṭṭu vikkōlit taṇṇīril pōṭṭu eṭṭu āḷ kūṭi iḻuttāṟpōla.
As a bundle of straw that had been dipped in water was dragged along by
eight persons.
1807. oru kampattil iraṇṭu āṉi kaṭṭukiṟatā?
What, to tie two elephants to one pole?
1808. oru kācu akappaṭukiṟatu kutirik kompu.
To earn one cash is as difficult as to find a horse with horns.
1809. oru kācu pēṇiṉ iru kācu tēṟum.
One cash saved is two cash got.
1810. orukāṟ ceytavaṉ irukāṟ ceyvāṉ.
He who has done a thing once, may do it agin.
1811. oru kācu koṭātavaṉ oru varākaṉ koṭuppāṉā?
Will he who refuses to give acash, give a pagoda?
A Pagoda is a gold coin worth about seven shillings.
1812. oru kācu eṉṟa iṭattil aḻukiṟāṉ.
He weeps when the word money is uttered.
1813 orukuṭam pālukku oru tuḷi puri.
Onedrop of butter-milk to a pot of milk.
1814. oru kuṇṭilē kōṭṭi piṭikkalāmā?
Cana fort be taken with one ball ?
1815. oru kuḷattiṟ piṟanta tāmariyum alliyum orē taṉmiyuṭiyatalla atu pōla oru
tāyiṉ vayiṟṟil piṟantacakōtarar iruppārkaḷ.
The lotus flowers of a tank are not all of the same species, so the
children of the same mother are not all alike.
1816.oru kūṭu muṭintavaṉ oṉpatu kūṭu muṭivāṉ.
He who has plaţted one basket may plat nine.
1817. oru kūṭi kallum teyvamāṉāl, kumpiṭukiṟatu entakkalli ?
If all the stones in a basket be gods, which shall I worship?
1818. oru ki taṭṭiṉāl ōci eḻumpumā?
Can clapping be effected by one hand?
1819. oru ki taṭṭiṉāl ōci eḻumpumā, iruki taṭṭiṉāl ōci eḻumpumā?
Can clapping be affected with one, or with both hands?
1820. oru ki muḻam pōṭumā ?
Can cubits be measured with only one arm ?
1821. oru kōpam vantu kiṇaṟṟil viḻuntāl āyiram cantōṣam vantālum eḻumpalāmā?
When one has fallen into a well, in a fit of anger, will a thousand
joyous considerations help him out ?
1822. oru caṭṭiyilē iraṇṭu tilam.
Two kinds of oil in one chatty.
1823. oru cantippāṉi nāy aṟiyātu.
A dog does not know the vessels used on fast-days.
Separate vessels are kept for special occasions, and also for different
purposes on ordinary days, as for boiling milk &c.
1824. oru cāṇ kāṭṭilē oru muḻattaṭi veṭṭalāmā?
Can a stick a cubit long be cut in a grove a span high?
1825. oru taram viḻuntāl teriyātā ?
Having fallen once are you not wiser ?
1826. orutali vaḻakku nūlilum cevvi .
An ex parte statement is strighter than a line.
1827. oru talikku iraṇṭu ākkiṉiyā?
Are there two punishments for one head?
1828. oru tāy aṟṟa piḷḷikku ūrellām tāy.
The whole village is mother to the motherless.
1829. oru naṉṟi ceytavari uḷḷa aḷavum niṉi.
Think of those who have done you even one favour as long as you live.
1830. oru nāṭ kūttukkut taliyic cirittatu pōl.
Like shaving the head for a single day's dance.
1831. oru nāḷikku ikaḻcci oru nāḷikkup pukaḻcci.
One day blame, another day prise.
1832. oru nāḷikku iṟakkiṟatu kōṭi piṟakkiṟatu kōṭi.
Ten millions are born, and ten millions die, dily.
1833. oru nāḷum cirikkātavaṉ tirunāḷilē cirittāṉ, tirunāḷum veṟunāḷāyiṟṟu.
A man who had never lughed before, lughed on a festival day,
consequently it became a common day.
1834. oru nāḷākilum tirunāḷ.
Although only one day, it is a festival day.
1835. oru paṉi iraṇṭu pāḷi oṉṟu nuṅku oṉṟu kaḷḷu.
One palmyra has two spathes, one yields fruit, the other toddy.
1836. oru pāṉic cōṟṟukku oṉṟē mātiri.
One grin suffices to test a whole pot of boiled rice.
1837. oru piḷḷi peṟṟavaḷukku uṟiyilē cōṟu, nālu piḷḷi
peṟṟavaḷukku naṭutteruvilē cōṟu.
The rice for a woman who has borne one child, is on the swinging tray,
that of the woman who has borne four, is in the middle of the street.
1838. oru piḷḷiyeṉṟu ūṭṭi vaḷarttāḷām, atu ceriyāk kuṇam piṭittuc cettatām.
It is sid that having only one child she fed it well, but that it died
of indigestion.
1839. oru puttiraṉ āṉālum kuru puttiraṉ āvāṉā?
Although he is an only son, will he become an obedient disciple?
1840. oru poruḷ ākilum eḻuti aṟi.
Though only one item, note it.
1841. oru marattup paḻamā orumikka?
What! did one tree yield all this fruit ?
1842. oru marattup paṭṭi oru marattilē oṭṭumā?
Will the bark of one tree stick to another ?
1843. oru marattuk kompu oru marattil oṭṭātu.
The branch of one tree will not stick to another.
1844. oru maṉappaṭu, ōtuvārkku utavu.
Besingle-minded, assist those that teach the Vedas.
1845. oru miḷakukku āṟṟik kaṭṭi iṟitta ceṭṭi.
A merchant who dammed up and drined a river to recover a grin of
pepper.
1846. oru miḷakum nālu uppum pōtum.
Onegrinof pepper and four grins of salt will suffice.
1847. oru muḻukkilē maṇ eṭukkiṟatā ?
Whạt ! is it to take up the soil by diving once?
1848.orumippāṭu illāta kuṭi orumikkak keṭum.
A family divided aginst itself will perish together.
1849. oruvaṉ aṟintāl ulakam aṟiyum.
If known to one, the world may know it.
1850. oruvaṉ aṟinta rakaciyam ulakattil paravum.
A secret known to one may spread through the world.
1851. oruvaṉukku iruvar tuṇi oruvaṉi aṟiya iruvar vēṉṭum.
Two men may help one, two are necessary that one may be known.
1852. oruvarum aṟiyāta uccitanāmaṉ.
One of illustrious name unknown to any.
1853.oruvaṉik koṉṟavaṉ uṭaṉē cāvāṉ. pala pērik koṉṟavaṉ paṭṭam āḷuvāṉ.
He who, kills one person immediately suffers death, hewho kills many is
rewarded with a crown.
1854. oruvaṉāyp piṟappatu oru piṟappāmā, oṉṟi maram tōppāmā?
Is it worth being to be an only offspring ? Is a single tree a tope a
grove ?
1855.oruvaṉ taliyil māṇikkam, irukkiṟateṉṟu veṭṭalāmā?
May you cut-off a man's head becuse there is a ruby in it?
1856. oruvaṉāyp piṟantāl taṉimi, iruvarāyp piṟantār pakimi.
When only one is born there is loneliness, when two enmity.
1857. oruvar aṟintāl irakaciyam iruvar aṟintāl parāciyam.
If known to one only it is a secret, if to two it is public.
1858. oruvaṉip paṟṟi ōrakattiru.
Attach yourself to one person, and dwell in one house.
1859. oru viral noṭi iṭātu.
A single finger cannot snap.
1860. ovvāk kūṭṭilum taṉimi aḻaku, ovvāp pēccu vaciyōṭu okkum. Solitude is
preferable to disagreeable society, an unpleasant expression may be felt
as a reproach.
1861. oḻukāta vīṭu uḷḷaṅki attaṉi pōtum.
If only as large as the palm of the hand a house that does not leak will
suffice.
1862. oḻukkam uyar kulattil naṉṟu.
Virtue is superior to rank.
1863. oḻukku vīṭṭilē veḷḷam vantatu pōl.
As a flood came into a leaky house.
1864. oḻukkukku vitta caṭṭipōla.
As a chatty placed under a leak.
1865. oḻuṅku oru paṇam caḷukku mukkāṟ paṇam.
Propriety is worth a fanam, pride, three fourths of a fanam.
1866. oḷikkum cēvakaṉukku mukattil ēṉ mīci?
Of what use is a mustache to a sneaking soldier?
1867. oḷikkap pōyum taliyāri vīṭṭilā?
When trying to conceal one's self, is it fit to do so in the house of
the chief ?
1868. oḷikkap pōyum iṭam iṭiñcalā?
Is the place too narrowfor one who has gone to hide himself?
1869. oḷḷiyar teḷḷiyar āyiṉum ūḻviṉi piya nuḻintu viṭum.
However enlightened and clear-headed one may be, his destiny will
quietly creep in.
1870. oṟṟiyum cītaṉamum paṟṟi āḷavēṇṭum.
Property received under mortgage, or as a dowry, must be taken into
immediate possession.
1871. oṉṟa vanta pēy ūrik keṭuttatu pōl.
As the demon that came for shelter destroyed the village.
1872.oṉṟa vanta kākam uram peṟṟāṟpōla.
As if a crow that came for shelter was strengthened in his position.
1873. oṉṟa vanta piṭāri ūrp piṭāriyi ōṭṭiṉatu pōl.
As a demoness that came for shelter drove away the demoness of the
village.
1874. oṉṟa vanta piṭāri ūrp piṭāri āṉatupōla.
As a demoness that came for shelter became the demoness of the village.
1875. oṉṟāṉa pirapu uṟaṅkik kiṭikkiyil piccikku vantavaṉtattiyōtaṉattiṟku
aḻukiṟāṉām.
It is sid that whilst a peerless nobleman was lying hungry and
exhusted, a beggar cries for rice and curds.
1876. oṉṟāl oṉṟu kuṟi illi, muṉṉālē kaṭṭat tuṇi illi.
Not a single want, yet no cloth to put on.
1877. oṉṟu niṉikka oṉṟāyiṟṟu.
One thing meditated, another effected.
1878. oṉṟum aṟiyāḷām kaṉṉi avaḷip piṭittatām caṉṉi.
It is sid that the virgin is innocent, and is seized with lock-jaw.
1879. oṉṟu terintavaṉukku ellām teriyātu.
He who knows one thing does not know all things.
1880. oṉṟum illiyeṉṟu ūtiṉāṉ atu tāṉum illiyeṉṟu koṭṭiṉāṉ.
He blew a conch to report that there was nothing; and beat a drum to
intimate that there was not even that.
1881. oṉṟum aṟṟavaṉukku oṉpatu peṇṭāṭṭi.
Nine wives to him who has nothing.
1882. oṉṟum aṟṟa taṅkāḷukku oṉpatu nāḷ caṭṭaṅkaḷ?
Is a ceremony of nine days to be performed for a destitute woman?
1883. oṉṟē kutiri oṉṟē rāvuttaṉ.
One horse, one horseman.
1884. oṉṟē rājā oṉṟē kutiri.
One king, one horse.
1885. oṉṟip peṟṟāl naṉṟē peṟavēṇṭum.
If one thing only be obtined, it ought to be good.
1886. oṉṟip piṭittāl cātikka vēṇṭum.
The thing asserted must be mintined.
1887. oṉṟip peṟṟālum kaṭukkap peṟu.
Though you get only one thing, get it quickly.
1888. oṉṟip peṟṟālum kaṉṟip peṟu.
If you get only one as your hire, take a calf.
1889. oṉṟit toṭiṉum naṉṟit toṭu.
Though you touch but one thing, touch what is good.
Most likely referring to the choice of a wife.
1890. ō koṭuppāṉukku vāḻkkip paṭṭu ōṭṭam oḻiya, naṭi illi.
