Having become the wife of the wretch, I am compelled to run instead of
walking.
1891. ōṅkil aṟiyum uyar kaṭaliṉ āḻam, pāṅki aṟivāḷ taṉ pattāviṉ valimi.
An onkil fish knows the depth of the ocean, a wife knows her husband's
strength.
1892. ōci kāṭṭip pūci cey.
Ring the bell, and perform puja.
1893. oci peṟum veṇkalam, ōcipeṟā maṭkalam.
Sounding brass, a soundless earthen vessel.
1894. ōṭam viṭṭu iṟaṅkiṉāl ōṭakkāraṉukku oru coṭṭu.
On landing, a cuff for the boatman.
1895. ōṭam viṭṭa āṟum aṭi cuṭum.
Even a navigable river may burn the feet when dry.
1896. ōṭam kaṭṭiṉa tūlam.
A block of wood to which a boat is moored.
1897. ōṭam kaṭantāl ōṭakkāraṉukku oru coṭṭu.
After crossing, a cuff for the boatman.
1898. ōṭam vaṇṭiyilum vaṇṭi ōṭattilum kāṇavumpaṭum.
A boat may be sesen in a cart, and a cart in a boat.
1899. ōṭam viṭṭa iṭam aṭi cuṭum aṭi cuṭṭa iṭam ōṭam viṭappaṭum.
The foot may be burnt in a navigable river, and a boat may float where
the foot was burnt.
1900. ōṭavum māṭṭāṉ piṭikkavum māṭṭāṉ, ōyāmal pēcuvāṉ.
He can neither run nor overtake, but he can talk continually.
1901. ōṭavum māṭṭēṉ piṭikkavum māṭṭēṉ.
I can neither run nor catch.
1902. ōṭi oru kōṭi tēṭuvatilum iruntu oru kācu tēṭuvatu nalam.
It is better to acquire a cash in quiet than a lak by running about.
1903. ōṭi oṉpatu paṇam campātippatilum uṭkāntiruntu oru paṇam campātippatu naṉṟu.
Better earn one fanam where you are, thannine fanams by running hither
and thither.
1904. ōṭi ōṭi uḷḷaṅkālum veḷuttatu.
The sole of the foot has become white by constant running.
1905. ōṭippōṉa puruṣaṉ vantu kūṭikkoṇṭāṉ, uṭimimēl uṭimi pōṭṭu miṉukkik
koṇṭāḷ.
The husband that ran away has returned and is reconciled, therefore she
has adorned herself with jewels to excess.
1906. ōṭippōṉa ūril ātarittavaṉ kavuṇṭaṉ.
He who entertined the man that fled from his own village was a
Koundan-a man of that tribe.
1907. ōṭippōkiṟavaṉ pāṭip pōkiṟāṉ.
He who is running away, does so singing.
1908. ōṭiya muyal periya muyal allavō?
The hare that ran away was a large one, was it not ?
1909. ōṭiyam ākilum ūṭuruvak kēḷ.
Though it is obscene, hear it out.
1910. ōṭiyum kiḻavikkup piṟakēyā?
Having set out to run will it do for him to be behind an old Woman?
1911. ōṭukiṟa pāmpip piṭikkiṟa paruvam.
Old enough to seize a running snake.
1912. ōṭukiṟavaṉik kaṇṭāl turattukiṟavaṉukku ilēcu.
Seeing him who retreats, makes the efforts of his pursuer easy.
1913. ōṭukiṟa veḷḷam aṇiyil niṟkumā?
Will a rolling flood stay at the anicut?
1914. ōṭṭic caṭṭi āṉālum koḻukkaṭṭi ventāṟ cari.
The chatty may be cracked, what matters that if it bakes the cakes.
1915. ōṭṭi nāḻikkup pūṇ kaṭṭiṉatu pōla.
Like adorning a cracked measure with a metal rim.
1916. ōṭṭip pāṉiyilē carkkari irukkum.
A cracked pot will hold sugar.
1917. ōṭṭip pāṉic carkkari kacakkumā?
Will sugar, becuse put in a cracked pot, taste bitter?
1918. ōṭṭi maṇi āṉālum ōci nīṅkumā ?
Though the bell may be cracked will it be void of sound ?
1919. ōṇāṉ viḻuṅkiya kati pōla.
Like the story relating to the swallowing of a bloodsucker.
1920. ōtātārkku illi uṇarvoṭu oḻukkam.
The unlettered have neither good sense nor virtue.
1921. ōtuvaṉ ellām uḻuvāṉ talikkaṭiyil.
All poets may be found at the entrance of the ploughman's house.
1922. ōtuvāṉukku ūrum uḻuvāṉukku nilamum illiyā?
Has the poet no country, has the ploughman no land?
1923. ōtuvārkku utavu.
Assist instructors-the brahmans.
1924. ōnti vēlikku iḻukkiṉṟatu, tavaḷi taṇṇīrukku iḻukkiṉṟatu.
The blood-sucker draws its prey to a hedge, the frog to water.
1925. ōmapiṇṭatti nāy iccittāṟpōlē.
As if a dog longed for consecrated food.
Spoken by Sita to Ravana.
1926. ōyā maḻiyum oḻiyāk kāṟṟum.
Continual rin, and ceaseless wind.
1927. ōyvilā nēcamē yōlamē caraṇam.
Thou, the object my unceasing love, take me under thy protection.
1928. ōram coṉṉavaṉ ārukku āvāṉ?
Will any employ a person who is given to one-sided statements ?
1929. ōram coṉṉavaṉ kuṭittaṉam pōlē.
Like the family of him who makes partial statements.
1930. ōr āṇṭi pacittirukka ulakam ellām kiṟukiṟeṉṟu cuṟṟukiṟatā?
Is the whole world so giddy through famine as to allow a religious
mendicant to suffer from hunger?
1931. ōr āṟu tāṇṭa māṭṭātavaṉ oṉpatu āṟu tāṇṭuvāṉā?
Will he who cannot cross one river, cross nine?
1932. ōr ūrukkup pēccu maṟṟōr ūrukku ēccu.
That which is polite in one country may be abusive in another.
1933. ōr ūrukku oru pēriṭṭuk koḷḷalāmā?
May one assume a different name in every village?
1934. oḷavi collukku accam illi.
No fear about Avvi's sayings.
1935. oḷaviyārmēlum kuṟṟam uṇṭu aṇṇāviyārmēlum paḻu tu uṇṭu. Even Avvi may
be charged with blame, a religious teacher also may have his defects.
1936. kaṅkiyilē muḷittālum pēyccuri nalla curi āmā ?
Will a wild gourd become a good gourd by growing on the Ganges ?
1937. kaṅkiyil mūḻkiṉālum kākki aṉṉam ākumā?
Will a crow become a swan by bathing in the Ganges?
1938. kaṅkiyil āṭiṉālum, kaṇamum viṭāmaṟ ceyta pāvam tīrātu.
Though you wash in the Ganges, sin repeated agin and agin will not be
expiated.
1939. kaṅkiyil āṭiṉālum pāvam tīrumā?
Will sin be expiated by bathing in the Ganges ?
1940. kaṅkiyilē paṭintālum pēyccurikkāy nalla curikkāy ākātu.
Though a wild gourd be dipped in the Ganges, its inferiority will
remin.
1941. kaṅkiyilē piṟanta natti cālakkirāmam ākātu.
A snil of the Ganges is not a Shalgram.
The Shalgram is a flinty stone, contining the impression of one or more
ammonite, supposed by the Hindus to represent Vishnu.
1942. kacakki mōralāmā?
Can the fragrance of a flower be appreciated after bruising it?
1943. kacaṭarukku illik kaṟṟōr uṟavu.
The base do not enjoy the friendship of the learned.
1944. kacaṭaṟak kallārkku ici uṟal illi.
Persons of imperfect learning have no reputation.
1945. kacaṭāṉa kalviyiṉum kalviyīṉam nalam.
Ignorance is better than imperfect knowledge.
1946. kacintu vantavaṉ kaṇṇit tuṭi.
Wipe off the tears of him who comes weeping.
1947. kaccaṟ karuvāṭu mōṭcattiṟkup pōṉālum, piccikkārar mōṭcattiṟkup
pōkamāṭṭārkaḷ.
Though dried fish may attin final emancipation-heaven-beggars cannot.
1948. kañci varatappā eṉṟāl, eṅkē varatappā eṉkiṟāṉ.
If I say, O Varathappa give me some kanji, he replies, O Varathappa
where.
1949. kaṭalilē ēṟṟam pōṭṭa kati.
The story relating to a picotta on the sea-side.
1950. kaṭalil iṭṭa peruṅkāyam pōla.
Like assafoetida cast into the ocean.
1951. kaṭalilē turumpu kiṭantālum maṉatilē oru col kiṭavātu.
A rush may remin in the sea, but a secret will not remin in the mind.
1952. kaṭalil karitta puḷipōla.
Like tamarind acid dissolved in the sea.
1953. kaṭalit tūrttum kāriyam muṭikka vēṇṭum.
You must accomplish your undertaking, though you my have to fill up the
ocean.
1954. kaṭalit tūrttālum kāriyam muṭiyātu.
Though the sea be filled up, the thing cannot be effected.
1955, kaṭal kotittāl viḷāva nīr eṅkē ?
Should the sea boil, whence water to cool it?
1956. kaṭal nīr niṟintu āvatu eṉṉa, kāñciri paḻuttu āvatu eṉṉa ?
What benefit arises from the water of the ocean, what good comes
from the ripening of the kanjiri, strychnos nux vomica, fruit ?
1957. kaṭal tāṇṭa āci uṇṭu, kālvāy tāṇṭak kāl illi.
He wishes to cross the ocean, but has no feet to cross a small drin.
1958. kaṭal mīṉukku nuḷiyaṉ iṭṭatē caṭṭam.
As regards the fish of the sea, whatever name the fisherman givesis
final.
1959. kaṭal vaṟṟik karuvāṭu tiṉṉalāmeṉṟu uṭal vaṟṟic cettatām kokku.
It is sid that the stork died while waiting for the ocean to dry, in
the hope of getting a supply of dried fish.
1960. kaṭal perukiṉāl kariyum perukumā?
Will the shore extend when the sea flows?
1961. kaṭal perukiṉāl kari ētu?
What avils the shore when the sea flows ?
1962. kaṭaṟ karit tāḻaṅkāy kīḻ toṅki eṉṉa mēl toṅki eṉṉa ?
What matters it whether the wild pine fruit on the sea-shore hangs high
or low?
1963. kaṭaṉōṭu kaṭaṉ kantap poṭi kāṟpaṇam.
Though my debt be increased, let me have a quarter of a fanam worth more
of fragrant powder.
1964. kaṭaṉ illāta kañci kāl vayiṟu.
One fourth supply of kanji for the stomach is better than debt.
1965. kaṭaṉkāraṉukkuk kaṭaṉum, uṭaṉ piṟantāṉukkup paṅkum
koṭukka vēṇṭum.
One must pay one's creditor and give to a relation the portion due to
him.
1966. kaṭaṉ vāṅkik kaṭaṉ koṭuttavaṉum keṭṭāṉ ; maram ēṟik ki viṭṭavaṉum keṭṭāṉ .
He who borrowed to lend was ruined; and he who let go his hold of the
tree he had climbed also perished.
1967. kaṭaṉ vāṅkiyum paṭṭiṉi kalyāṇam paṇṇiyum canniyāci .
Famished though he has borrowed, an ascetic though he is wedded.
1968. kaṭaṉ vāṅkiyum paṭṭiṉiyā!
What to famish after having borrowed money!
1969. kaṭaṉkāraṉukkuk kaṭaṉum paḻikāraṉukkup paḻiyum koṭukka vēṇṭum.
Debt to the creditor and retaliation to the wrong-doer are due.
1970. kaṭaṉkāraṉi vikkak kaḻu uṇṭā ?
Is there a stake for impaling debtors ?
1971. kaṭaṉ paṭṭār neñcam pōṟ kalaṅkiṉāṉ.
He was as disquieted as the mind of a debtor.
1972. kaṭā kaṭā eṉṟālum maruntukku oru pīr eṉkiṟāṉ.
Though informed that it is a he-goat, he persists in asking a drop of
milk for a medicinal purpose.
1973. kaṭā piṉvāṅkukiṟatu pāyccalukku aṭiyāḷam.
The drawing back of the he-goat shows that he is about to butt.
1974. kaṭā mēykkiṟavaṉ aṟivāṉō koḻup pōṉaviṭam.
Does the cowherd know where the ploughshare has passed?
1975. kaṭāviṉ cantiṟ pullit tiṉkiṟatu pōla.
Like eating grass screened behind a bull.
1976. kaṭāvum kaṭāvum caṇṭi pōṭukiṟapōtu uṇṇi nacuṅkiṉāṟpōla.
As the tick was crushed to death when the goats fought.
1977. kaṭikōlilē kaṭṭiṉa nāy.
A dog tied to a stick.
1978. kaṭikka oru elumpum illi, kātil miṉukka ōliyum illi .
She has not even a single bone to pick, nor has she an ear-ornament to
polish.
1979. kaṭikkiṟa nākam kalantu uṟavākumā?
Will the cobra be affected by friendly intercourse?
1980. kaṭikkiṟa nāykkuk kaḻuttil kuṟuṅ kayiṟu.
A short rope round the neck of a bitingdog.
1981. kaṭitāṉa piḷḷi peṟṟārukku utavumā ?
Will an obdurate child prove helpful to its parents ?
1982. kaṭitāṉa col aṭiyilum valitu.
A harsh word is more pinful than a blow.
1983. kaṭitta pākkum koṭāta ciṟṟappaṉ kaṭitteru variyil
vaḻiviṭṭāṉām.
It is sid that his uncle who would not give even a bite of an
areca-nut, bore him company to the bazaar.
1984. kaṭitta vāy tuṭittāṟpōla.
Like wiping the mouth after biting.
1985. kaṭitta pāmpukku pāl vārttāl viṣattit tarum atu pōlappollātavarkkuc
ceyyum upakāram irukkum.
When milk is placed before a biting. snake, in return it gives poison,
and such is the return for the favours done to the wicked.
1986. kaṭitta mūṭṭiyum cari, kaṭiyāta mūṭṭiyum cari.
Bugs are all the same whether they bite or not.
1987. kaṭitta nāykkuk kāṭiyik koṭu.
Give vinegar to the dog that bit you.
1988. kaṭittālum kaṭikkaṭṭum. nī kollātiru.
No matter if it bite you, do not kill it.
1989. kaṭitta nāyip payittiyam koṇṭatu eṉṟu colvārkaḷ.
They will call that a mad dog which has bitten some one.
1990. kaṭinta collilum kaṉinta collē naṉmi.
A kind word is better than a harsh one.
1991. kaṭiyāta mūṭṭiyeṉṟu viṭṭu viṭuvārkaḷā?
Will they let a bug escape becuse it did not bite ?
1992. kaṭiyum cuṟukkuttāṉ aṭiyum cuṟukkuttāṉ.
Both the bite and the stroke were quickly affected.
1993. kaṭukiccu muṭukiccu vaṭukacci kaliyāṇam.
The Vaduga woman's wedding is come nigh.
1994. kaṭuku pōkiṟa iṭattil taṭi eṭuttukkoṇṭu tirivāṉ, pūcaṉikkāy pōkiṟa iṭam
teriyātu.
He walks about with a staff in a place that will admit a mustard seed,
and yet he is ignorant of the place where a pumpkin might easily pass.
1995. kaṭuku ciṟuttālum kāram pōkātu.
Though small, the mustard seed is not without pungency.
1996. kaṭuku pōṉa iṭam ārāyvār pūcaṉikkāy pōṉa iṭam teriyātu.
They search for a grin of mustard seed, but not for a missing pumpkin.
1997. kaṭuku pōṉa iṭam ārāyvār miḷaku pōṉa iṭam ārāyār.
They search for a grin of mustard seed. but not for a grin of pepper.
1998. kaṭukuk kaḷavum kaḷavutāṉ karppūrak kaḷavum kaḷavutāṉ.
Theft is theft whether as regards a mustard seed or camphor.
1999.kaṭukkāykku ataṇi nañcu, cukkiṟkup puṟaṇi nañcu.
The kernel of the gall-nut and the skin of ginger are poisonous.
2000. kaṭuṅkāṟṟu maḻi kāṭṭum, kaṭuñciṉēkam paki kāṭṭum.
Strong wind foretells rin, excessive friendship foreshows hatred.
2001.kaṭuñ ciṉēkam kaṇṇukkup paki.
Excessive intimacy will prove unfriendly to one's eyes.
2002.kaṭuñceṭṭuk kāriyakkēṭām.
It is sid that hard dealing fails in its object.
2003. kaṭuñceṭṭuk kaṇṇik keṭukkum.
Unfair dealing destroys the eyes.
2004. kaṭuñcoṟ kēṭṭāl kātukku aruvaruppu.
It is shocking to the ears to hear harsh words.
2005. kaṭumuṭukkaṭā cēvakā kamparici aṭā campaḷam.
Peon, my fellow, you are too strict, you have kamboo rice only as wages.
2006. kaṭumpaci kal matil uṭittum kaḷavu ceyyac collum.
Extreme hunger will induce a man to break through a stone wall and
steal.
2007. kaṭi keṭṭa vāḻvu tali kaṭṭa nēram illi.
Labouring in menial offices she has not leisure to put up her tresses.
2008. kaṭikkuk kaṭi āḷ iruppārkaḷ.
Each bazaar will have its attendant.
2009. kaṭicic cōṟṟukku mōrum, kāl māṭṭiṟkup pāyum vēṇṭum.
Buttermilk for the last distribution of rice, and a mat for the foot of
the bed are indispensable.
2010. kaṭit tēṅkāy eṭuttu vaḻip piḷḷiyārukku uṭittāṟpōl.
Like taking up a cocoanut in the bazaar and breaking it to Ganesa.
2011. kaṭiyilē koṇṭu maṉiyilē vikkiṟāṉ.
He buys in the bazaar, and bestows at home.
2012. kaṭiyil arici kañcikku utavumā, apicāri puruṣaṉ āpat tukku utavuvāṉā?
Will the rice in the bazaar serve for kanji, will the paramour of an
adulteress be of service to her in distress?
2013. kaṭiyil vantatum ariciyō naṭiyil ventatum cātamō?
Is that which comes to the bazaar rice? Is boiled rice cooked by the way
desirable ?
2014. kaṭiyilē kaṭṭit tūkkiṉālum aḻukaṟ pūcaṇikkāy aḻukalē.
Although rotten pumpkins are tied up in the bazaar, they are still
rotten.
2015. kaṭṭappāriyi viḻuṅkic cukkuk kaṣāyam kuṭittatupōla.
Like drinking a decoction of dry ginger after swallowing a crow-bar.
2016. kaṭṭāntari aṭṭipōla.
Like a wood-louse on dry ground.
2017. kaṭṭikkoṇṭu varaccoṉṉāl veṭṭikkoṇṭu varuvāṉ.
When told to tie and bring, he cuts and brings.
2018. kaṭṭi aḻukiṟapōtu kiyum tuḻāvukiṟatē.
When she embraces and weeps, her hand is groping to steal.
2019. kaṭṭil uḷḷa iṭattil piḷḷi peṟṟuc cukkuk kaṇṭa iṭattil kāyamtiṉpāḷ.
She brings forth her child where she sees a bedstead, and takes the
prescribed tonic where she sees dry ginger.
2020. kaṭṭi vaḻi viṭṭāl veṭṭi aracu āḷalām.
If he organize an army and lead it, he may cut down the enemy and reign
as a king.

There is honey in sugarcane there is sap in milk-hedge.
2174. karumpukkaṭṭikku eṟumputāṉē varum.
Ants will of course be attracted by a lump of sugar-candy.
2175. karumpu muṟittuk kaḻutiyi aṭitta kati.
The story of one striking an ass with a sugar-cane.
2176.karumpik kaḻuti muṉ pōṭṭāl kaḻutikkut teriyumō karumpu ruci?
Does the ass enjoy the flavor of the sugar-cane that is placed before
it?
2177. karumpu tiṉṉak kūliyā?
What, a reward for eating sugar-cane?
2178. karumpum vēmpu āyiṟṟē.
Even sugar cane has become a margosa tree.
2179. karumpukku uḻuta, puḻuti kāycciṉa pālukkuc carukkari āmā ?
Will earth pulverised by the plough for sugar-cane, answer for sugar to
be mixed with boiled milk?
2180. karumpu kacakkiṟatu vāykkuṟṟam.
If sugar-cane provebitter to the palate, it is the fault of the mouth.
2181. karumpu ruciyeṉṟu vērōṭu piṭuṅkalāmā?
May sugar-cane be plucked up by the root becuse it is of fine flavor?
2182. karumpi virumpa, atu vēmpu āyiṟṟu.
If taken to excess sugar-cane becomes bitter as margosa.
2183. karumpu kaṭṭōṭē irukka eṟumpu tāṉē varum.
Where bundles of sugar-cane are, there ants will come of themselves.
2184. karuvu illāta muṭṭiyum kuru illāta vittiyum.
An egg without yolk, an art without a teacher.
2185. karuvēlamarattiṟku niḻal illi, kaṉṉāṉukku muṟi illi.
The karuvel Acasia casts no shade, smiths observe no relationship in
marrying.
2186. kari kāṇāta tōṇipōla.
As a doney out of sight of land.
The common name of a country vessel, some of which are of considerable
size. They have no deck and are thatched with palm leaves
2187. kari taṭṭiṉ kappal pōla.
Like a stranded vessel.
2188. karippakkam pāti irukkak kappal ēṟiṉavaṉum, collāta ti maṉiyāḷukkuc coṉṉavaṉum
paṭṭa pāṭupōla.
Like the misery endured by him who embarked when there was a way by
land, and by the husband who revealed to his wife that which he ought
not.
2189. karippār karittāṟ kallum kariyum.
Under the management of the skilful, even a stone may be dissolved.
2190. kalakattilē puḷukātavaṉ narakattilē pōvāṉām.
It is sid that he who does not exaggerate when a tumult occurs will go
to hell.
2191. kalakattilē pōyum kālmāṭu talimāṭā?
In regard to a bed is the distinction of head and foot observed in a
battle-field?
2192. kalakam kalantāl ulakam kalaṅkum.
In a general insurrection universal consternation previls.
2193. kalakam piṟantāl niyāyam piṟakkum.
Popular agitation leads to justice.
2194. kalakamē meyyāṉāṟ puḷukātavaṉ pāvam.
If an insurrection exists, it is wrong not to make it known.
2195. kalakkanti kaṭṭik kāṇap pōṉāl irukalak kanti kaṭṭi etirē vantāḷ.
When I went in rags to see her, she appeared enveloped in double the
quantity.
2196. kalak kampu tiṉṟālum kāṭi kāṭṭilē.
Though it may consume a kalam of grin, the quail lives in the jungle.
2197. kalakkiṉum kaṭal cēṟākātu.
Though never so troubled, the sea does not, become muddy.
2198. kalattilē cōṟṟi iṭṭuk kiyip piṭittāṟpōla.
Like holding the hand after putting rice on the plate.
2199. kala nellukkuk kala umi irukkumā?
Will a kalam of paddy yield a kalam of husks?
2200. kalap paṇattip pārkkilum oru kiḻap piṇam nallatu.
The corpse of an old person is better than immense wealth.
2201. kalap pāl kaṟakkalām tuḷip pāl mulikku ēṟṟalāmā ?
A kalam of milk may be drawn; can a drop beinfused into the teat?
2202. kalap pālukkut tuḷip puri.
A drop of butter-milk or curds to a kalam of milk.
2203. kalappāṉālum pūcapūcap poṉṉiṟam.
Though not pure, repeated gilding will give it the colour of gold.
2204. kalap pālil oru tuḷi viṣam kalantāṟpōlē.
Like putting a drop of poisonin a kalam of milk.
2205.kalap pāl kuṭittapūṉi āḻākkup pāl kuṭiyātā?
Will not a cat that has drunk a kalam of milk drink alak?
2206.kala mā iṭittavaḷ pāviyum, kappi iṭittavaḷ puṇṇiyavatiyumā?
Is she who pounded a kalam of flour vicious, and she who pounded
prolong(?) virtuous?
2207. kalam kalantāl kulam kalakkum.
When plates are interchanged, tribal marriages interchange.
2208. kalam kuttiṉālum patar arici ākātu.
Though a kalam of chaff bepounded, it will not become rice.
2209. kala umi tiṉṟāl oru arici taṭṭātā?
If a kalam of chaff be eaten may not a grin of rice turn up?
2210. kaliyāṇac cantaṭiyil tāli kaṭṭa maṟantāṉām.
It is sid that he forgot to tie the tali owing to the bustle at the
wedding.
2211. kaliyāṇattilum pañcam illi, kaḷattilum pañcam illi.
No scarcity at a wedding, nor in the threshing floor.
2212. kaliyāṇa vīṭṭil pantaṟkālik kaṭṭi aḻukiṟavaḷ cetta vīṭṭil cummā iruppāḷā?
Will the woman who wept as sheclung to the post of a marriage pandal at
be silent when she attends a funeral?
2213. kaliyāṇa vīṭṭiṟkup pōy aṟiyāṉ, mēḷac cattamum kēṭṭu aṟiyāṉ.
He has never been at a wedding, nor has he heard the sound of a tom-tom.
2214. kaliyāṇam ceytum caṉṉiyāciyā?
What, though married yet a Sanyasi-ascetic?
2215. kal ākilum kaṇavaṉ, pul ākilum puruṣaṉ.
Though in heart hard as a stone, and worthless as a blade of grass, he
is your husband.
2216. kal eṟikkut tappiṉālum kaṇ eṟikkut tappakkūṭātu.
Though one may escape the cast of a stone, he cannot escape theglance of
an evil eye.
The evil eye kaṇṇūṟu and the evil tongue nāvūṟu are fearedvery generally.
To avert their influence as regardsfruits &c, in a garden, it is common
to put up some object of attraction as a water-pot whitewashed, inverted
on a stump, and dotted with black spots &c. In some enclosures grotesque
images are seen.
2217. kallaṭic cittaṉ pōkiṟa vaḻi, kāṭu mēṭu ellām taviṭu poṭi.
All the rough and rugged ground before the stone-cutter will be reduced
to powder.
2218. kallāṭam paṭittavaṉōṭu mal āṭātē
Do not contend with him who has studied Kalladam.
Kalladam a work on the third part of grammar is sid to be extremely
dry.
2219.kallātār celvattilum kaṟṟār vaṟumi nalam.
The poverty of the learned is better than the wealth of theunlearned.
2220. kallāta oruvaṉ kulanalam pēcutal nelluṭaṉ piṟanta patarākummē.
The illiterate who boast of high rank may be compared to the dust mixed
with paddy-unhusked rice.
2221. kallār uṟavilum kaṟṟōr paki nalam.
The enmity of the learned is better than the friendship of the
illiterate.
2222. kallār uṟavakal kāmak kaṭal kaṭa.
Keep at a distance from the unlettered; cross the sea of passion.
2223. kallilum vaṉmi kaṉa mūṭar neñcam.
The heart of a great fool is harder than a stone.
2224. kallilē nār urikkiṟatu pōla.
Like stripping fibre from a stone.
2225. kallum tēṅkāyum pōlap pēcukiṟāṉ.
He speaks as effectively as the hitting of a stone on a cocoanut.
2226. kallum kāvēriyum uḷḷa maṭṭum vāḻka.
May you live as long as the rocks and the Cauveri exist.
The river Cauveri rises in the western ghuts and flows into the bay of
Bengal. It is the source of the great fertility of Tanjore and the
adjacent districts. According to the legend it originated in the
following manner. In ancient times when the people of southern India
suffered from drought, Ganesa became incarnate in the form of a crow. As
such he visited the abode of an ascetic on one of the western hills. He
there alighted up the waterpot of a hermit and upset it. The water thus
spilt, by reason of the hermit's merit, he came a river : the current
spelling would make the name the lake of the crow: the legend requires
the word to be spelt kālēṟi crow
mounting, whereas kāvēri lake of the crow is common.
2227. kallu varukiṟa viciyik kaṇṭāl pallic cikkeṉa mūṭa vēṇṭum.
On seeing the rapid approach of a stone press your teeth and shut your
mouth.
2228. kalli ākilum karikkalām, mūrkkaṉ maṉatik karikkal ākātu.
Even stones may be dissolved, but the mind of a fool cannot.
2229. kallik kaṇṭāl nāyik kāṇōm, nāyik kaṇṭāl kallik kāṇōm.
On finding a stone we see no dog, on seeing a dog we find no stone.
2230. kallik kuttuvāṉēṉ ki nōkiṟatu eṉṟu aḻuvāṉēṉ?
Why beat a stone and then weep, excliming it has pined my hand?
2231. kallip pōlak kaṇavaṉ irukkak kañcikku aḻuvāṉēṉ?
Why weep for kanji while you have a husband as hard as a stone ?
2232. kallik kiḷḷik ki iḻantatu pōlām.
Like losing the hand by pinching a stone.
2233. kalvi illāṉ celvam kaṟpillāḷ aḻaku.
Wealth without learning is like beauty without chastity.
2234. kalvi eṉṟa payirukkuk kaṇṇīr eṉṟa maḻi vēṇṭum.
Plants of learning flourish in the rin of tears.
2235. kalviyum kulamum velvatu viṉavil.
When inquiring what will previl, one's learning and rank naturally
occur.
2236. kalvi uḷḷa iḷiñaṉ kaṉa kiḻavaṉ.
A well instructed youth is an old man.
2237. kalviyip paṅkupōṭa ēlātu.
Learning cannot be allotted in portions-to persons.
2238. kavaṇeṟi neṟiyil nillātē, kaṇṭavaṉ taliyi uṭikkum.
Do not stand in the way of a stone from a sling, it may break the head
of the beholder.
2239. kavi koṇṭārkkuk kīrtti, atic cevikoḷḷārkku avakīrtti.
Prise to those who approve of a poem, and blame to those who will not
listen to it.
2240. kavikkoṇṭārkkum kīrtti kalippārkkum kīrttiyā?
Are those who accept a poem and those who reject it alike prise worthy?
2241. kavintirāṇam kajēntirāṇam rājāvē niṟpayē vaki.
Oking, manage a poet lureate and a superior elephant without fear.
2242. kaviyippaṟṟik kaḻutiyiṉ kālip piṭi.
To effect your object, if necessary, cling to the legs of an ass.
2243. kaḻuti kāṇavēṇṭiṉāl kuṭṭiccuvarilē kāṇalām.
If an ass be wanted, it may be found feeding near a ruinous wall.
2244. kaḻutik keṭṭāl kuṭṭiccuvar.
If an ass be out of condition, it will be as useless as a ruinous wall.
2245. kaḻutikku ēṉ kaṭivāḷam?
Why a bridle for an ass?
2246. kaḻutip puṇṇukkup puḻuti maruntu.
Dust is medicine for the gores of an ass.
2247. kaḻutiyum kutiriyum cari āmā ?
Are an ass and a horse equal ?
2248. kaḻutiyum kutiriyum piṇittāṟpōla.
Like yoking an ass and a horse together.
2249. kaḻuti aṟiyumā kantappoṭi vācaṉi?
Can an ass appreciate fragrant powder?
2250. kaḻutikkuc cēṇam kaṭṭiṉālum kutiri āmā ?
Does an ass become a horse by being saddled?
2251. kaḻuti tēyntu kaṭṭeṟumpu āyiṟṟu.
The ass by wearing away has become an ant.
2252. kaḻutikku ēṉ kaliṉam, allatu kaṭivāḷam?
Why a bit or a bridle for an ass?
2253. kaḻutiyik kaṭṭi ōmam vaḷarttatu pōla.
Like tying an ass and feeding a sacrificial fire.
This proverb relates to the following story. A certin Maharaja had a
barber who had evinced great skill in shaving his royal master when he
was asleep. To reward him the King resolved to elevate him in the
socialscale. The brahmans were called and ordered to make a brahman of
the barber. They set to work, kindled a sacred
fire and the barber was led round it, whilst the priests uttered the
required incantations for accomplishing their purpose. The prime
minister being of a somewhat facetious temperament determined to
metamorphose an ass. As the proverb intimates he kindled a fire and led
the animal round it. When the King
saw him thus engaged he asked him what he was doing. The prime minister
replied by stating that if the brahmans could make a brahmanof a barber
he thought an ass might be made into a horse, and he was therefore
engaged in the ceremony.
2254. kaḻutikku upatēcam kātilē coṉṉālum apayak kural aṉṟi aṅku oṉṟum illi.
Though religious instruction be whispered in the ears of an ass nothing
will come of it but the accustomed braying.
2255. kaḻutikku vāḻkkippaṭṭu utikku añcalāmā?
Having married an ass, do you fear his kicking ?
2256. kaḻutimēl ēṟi eṉṉa, iṟaṅki eṉṉa ?
What matters it whether you mount an ass, or alight from one ?
2257. kaḻutimēl ēṟiyum perumi illi, iṟaṅkiyum ciṟumi illi.
Though you may ride on an ass, you gin no honour, nor are you disgraced
by dismounting.
2258. kaḻuti uḻutu vaṇṇāṉ kuṭi āṉāṉā?
Did the washerman become a farmer by ploughing with an ass ?
2259. kaḻuti uḻutu kuṟavaṉ kuṭi āṉāṉā?
Has the forester become a farmer by ploughing with an ass?
2260. kaḻuti mayir piṭuṅkiṉāl ilāpam eṉṉa naṣṭam eṉṉa ?
What profit or loss will arise from plucking off the hir of an ass ?
2261. kaḻuti mayir piṭuṅkit tēcam kaṭṭi āḷvāṉā?
Can one reign as a king by selling ass-hir?
2262. kaḻutikkut teriyumā kastūri vācaṉi?
Does an ass appreciate the odour of musk?
2263.kaḻutikkup paratēcam kuṭṭiccuvar.
The foreign country of an ass is a ruinous wall.
2264. kaḻuti tappiṉāl kuṭṭiccuvariṭattil irukkum.
If an ass goes astray, it may be found near a ruinous wall.
2265. kaḻutip potiyil aiṅkala māṟāṭṭamā?
Are there five kalams of frud in the pack on an ass?
2266. kaḻuti viṭṭi āṉālum ki nirampa vēṇṭum eṉkiṟāy.
You say get a handful, although it be but ass dung.
2267. kaḻuttilē karimaṇi illi peyar muttumāli.
Her name is pearl necklace, yet on her neck she has not a black bead.
2268. kaḻuvik kaḻuvip piṉṉum cēṟṟi mitikkiṟatā?
What ! is it to tread in the mud every time you wash your feet?
2269. kaḻuvukku ēṟṟa kōmuṭṭi.
A komutti exactly fitted to the stake.
2270. kaḷam cāṟukiṟavaḷi miraṭṭuvāṉām pōr piṭuṅkukiṟavaṉ.
It is sid that he who steals grin from the stack, will frighten away
the women who
may sweep the threshing floor.
2271. kaḷiyak kūṭātatik kaṇṭāl aṭi peyarntu appuṟam pō.
If you cannot pluck up, pass by on the other side.
2272. kaḷiyak kūṭātatik kaṇṭiyāmal cakittukkoḷ.
Do not fret yourself about that which cannot be avoided.
2273. kaḷ viṟṟuk kalappaṇam campātippati viṭak kaṟpūram viṟṟuk kāṟpaṇam campātippatu
mēl.
It is better to gin a quarter of a fanam by selling camphor than a
kalam by selling toddy.
2274. kaḷḷaṉum āki viḷakkum piṭikkiṟāṉ.
He plays the thief and also holds the lamp.
2275. kaḷḷa vicuvācam kaḻuttellām cepamāli.
The neck of the hypocrite is covered with rosaries.
2276. kaḷḷaṉiyum veḷḷa aṇiyum kaṭṭiviṭu.
Secure a thief by tying, and a flood by damming it up.
2277. kaḷḷap piḷḷiyilum cellappiḷḷi uṇṭā?
Are there any indulged ones among the children of kallars?
2278. kaḷḷaṉi āru naḷḷār eṉṟum.
No one will ever approve of a thief.
2279. kaḷḷaṉukku ūrellām viḷḷāp paki.
The whole country owes implacable hatred to a thief.
2280.kaḷḷa māṭu canti ēṟātu.
A vicious bullock will not be brought to market.
2281. kaḷḷaṉi nampiṉālum kuḷḷaṉi nampalākātu.
A rogue may be trusted, but not a dwarf.
2282. kaḷḷaṉ piṉ pōṉālum kuḷḷaṉ piṉ pōkappaṭātu.
One may follow a rogue without risk, but not a dwarf.
2283. kaḷḷaṉ accam kāṭu koḷḷātu.
The jungle is not large enough to contin the fear of robbers.
2284. kaḷḷa māṭu tuḷḷum.
An unwilling bullock is restless.
2285. kaḷḷa maṉam tuḷḷum.
A guilty mind is agitated.
2286. kaḷḷaṉ uṟavu uṟavum alla, kācā viṟaku viṟakum alla.
The friendship of a rogue is not friendship, nor is kasawood fuel.
2287. kaḷḷaṉ putti tiruṭṭu mēlē.
The mind of a rogue is set on thieving.
2288.kaḷḷaṉ piḷḷikkum kaḷḷap putti.
Even the child of a thief is characterized by thievish propensites.
2289. kaḷḷaṉ maṉivi kimpeṇ eṉṟum.
The wife of a thief is at any moment liable to become a widow.
2290. kaḷḷaṉ maṉiyāḷik kaḷavup poruḷik kāṇum kuṟi kēṭkalāmā?
Can the wife of a thief be consulted as an augur for the recovery of
stolen property ?
2291. kaḷḷaṉum veḷḷamum oṉṟu.
A thief and a flood are alike.
2292. kaḷḷaṉukkup pāti veḷḷaṉukkup pāti.
One half to the rogue, and one half to the honest.
2293. kaḷḷaṉum tōṭṭakkāraṉum oṉṟu.
A gardener is another term for a thief.
2294. kaḷḷaṉum tōṭṭakkāraṉum oṉṟu kūṭiṉāl viṭiyumaṭṭum tiruṭalām.
If the thief and a gardener are united, one may steal till day-break.
2295. kaḷḷaṉ koṇṭa māṭu ettuṟi pōyeṉṉa?
What matters it to what ferry the bull may go that has been bought by a
thief?
2296. kaḷḷaṉukkuk kāṇpittavaṉ paki.
He who points out a thief is hated by him.
2297. kaḷḷi peruttu eṉṉa? kāy uṇṭā niḻal uṇṭā?
What if the kalli grow large ? Has it any fruit or any shade ?
2298. kaḷḷikku nāṭu ellām kāṭu.
The whole country is a wild fit for the growth of the kalli.
2299. kaḷḷik kompukku veḷḷippūṇ kaṭṭiṉatupōla.
Like attaching a silver ferrule to a kalli stick.
2300. kaḷḷi eṉṉaṭi kal iḻippatu? kātil iruppatu pittaḷi.
Why you hypocrite, inlay with gems ? that which is in the ear is made of
brass.
2301. kaḷḷikkum veṭṭik kaṟṟāḻikkum veṭṭukiṟatu.
After cutting the kalli to cut the kattali.
2302. kaḷḷikkut taṇṇīr kaṇṇilē, nīlikkut taṇṇīr nimiyilē.
The tears of a thievish woman are in her eyes, those of an abandoned
woman are in her eyebrows.
2303. kaḷḷikku kala nīr kaṇṇilē.
A thievish woman has a kalam of tears in her eyes.
2304. kaḷḷikku vēli ēṉ, cuḷḷikkuk kōṭāli ēṉ?
What avils a fence aginst a thievish woman, or an axe for cutting
twigs?
2305. kaḷḷum cūtum irukkiṟa iṭattil kūttik kaḷḷaṉum iruppāṉ.
Where there is theft and gambling, there will also be a whoremonger.
2306. kaḷḷik kuṭittāl uḷḷatic colvāṉ.
Should he drink toddy, he will tell the truth.
2307. kaṟakkiṟatu uḻakkup pāl, utikkiṟatu pallup paṟakka.
Yielding an ulak of milk, and kicking out the teeth.
2308. kaṟanta pālum eccil piṟanta piḷḷiyum eccil.
First drawn milk is unclean, and so is a first-born child.
2309. kaṟanta pāl mulikku ēṟumā?
Will the milk that has been drawn, agin enter the udder?
2310. kaṟanta pālik kāyccāmaṟ kuṭittāl kāca viyāti tāṉē eṭupaṭum.
If fresh drawn milk unboiled be drunk, consumption may be cured.
2311. kaṟuttatu ellām taṇṇīr veḷuttatu ellām pāl eṉkiṟāṉ.
Whatever is black he calls water, and whatever is white he calls milk.
2312. kaṟuppu veḷuppu ākātu, kacappu iṉippu ākātu.
Black will not become white, nor bitter sweet.
2313. kaṟuppu nāy veḷḷi nāy ākumā?
Will a black dog become white?
2314. kaṟumuṟukāntap paṭalam vācikkiṟār kavirāyar.
The poet is reading the section on grumbling.
2315. kaṟiyāṉ puṟṟil aravam kuṭikoṇṭatu pōla.
Like a snake that occupied an ant-hill.
2316. kaṟiyāṉ puṟṟup pāmpukku utavukiṟatu.
An ant-hill is useful to a snake.
2317. kaṟkaṇṭāṟ ceyta eṭṭikkaṉi kacakkumā ?
Will etti fruit made of sugar-candy taste bitter ?
2318. kaṟkiyil kalvi kacappu, kaṟṟapiṉpu atuvē iṉippu.
Learning when being acquired is bitter, but when possessed it is sweet.
2319. kaṟpakataruvic cārnta kākamum amutam uṇṇum.
Even a crow that lives in the kalpaka tree feeds on ambrosia.
2320. kaṟpittavaṉ kaṇṇik koṭuttavaṉ.
He who gave the eye is he who tught.
2321. kaṟpittavaṉ kāppāṟṟuvāṉ.
He who preordined will protect or guide.
2322. kaṟpillā aḻaku vācaṉi illāp pū.
Beauty without chastity, a flower without fragrance.
2323. kaṟṟa iṭattilā vittiyik kāṭṭukiṟatu?
To practise a trick on him who tught it ?
2324. kaṟṟavaratu kōpam nīriṉ piḷavupōl māṟum.
The anger of the learned passes away as water recovers its surface after
being
divided.
2325. kaṟṟatu kiyaḷavu kallātu ulakaḷavu.
What he has learnt is a handful, and what he has still to acquire is
wide as the world.
2326. kaṟṟatu collāṉ maṟṟu eṉṉa ceyvāṉ?
What other service will he do for you who would not tell you what he has
learnt ?
2327. kaṟṟavaṉukku vitti kāl nāḻi.
To the artist his art is but a quarter of a nali.
2328. kaṟṟa vittiyik kāyccik kuṭikkiṟavaṉ pōlē.
Like one who boils and drinks his learning.
2329. kaṟṟaṟi mōḻiyāy irātē.
Be not a learned fool.
2330. kaṟṟavaṉum uṇpāṉ, peṟṟavaḷum uṇpāḷ.
A learned man and a fruitful woman will not suffer from want of food.
2331. kaṟṟāḻi nāṟṟamum pittaḷi vīccamum pōkātu.
The ill odour of the karrali and the bad smell emitted from brass will
not leave them.
2332. kaṟṟāḻi ciṟuttālum āṉi aṭi vikkātu.
Though the karrali be small, the elephant will not tread upon it.
2333. kaṟṟuk koṭutta pēccum kaṭṭik koṭutta cātamum ettaṉi nāḷ niṟkum ?
How long will words put into one's mouth, and rice prepared for a
journey, last?
2334. kaṟṟuk kaṟṟup pēcātē.
Do not speak in a studied way.
2335. kaṟṟum kaṟṟaṟimōḻi.
Though he has learnt, he continues a learned fool.
2336. kaṟṟum kaṟṟaṟimōḻiyā?
What, to be a learned fool?
2337. kaṟṟōr arumi kaṟṟōr aṟivar.
The learned know the worth of the learned.
2338. kaṉa āci kaṉa naṣṭam.
Excessive desire entils great loss.
2339. kaṉa eli vaḷi eṭātu.
When rata are numerous, they do not burrow.
2340. kaṉaka māri poḻintatu pōlē.
As it rined gold.
2341. kaṉañ ceytāl iṣṭam, kaṉavīṉattāl naṣṭam.
Dignity gins approval, meanness entils loss.
2342. kaṉatta uṭamikku aṉarttam illi.
Solid jewels are invaluable.
2343. kaṉattāl iṉamākum paṇattāl caṉamākum.
Dignity or worth increases relatives, wealth secures society.
2344. kaṉattiṟku naṟkuṇam cumitāṅki.
Virtue is the support of dignity.
2345. kaṉattik kaṉam aṟiyum karuvāṭṭup pāṉiyi nāy aṟiyum.
Dignity is appreciated by dignity, the pot of dried fish is known to the
dog.
2346. kaṉa mūṭaṉ kipporuḷ iḻappāṉ.
A great fool loses his substance.
2347. kaṉavilum kākkikku malam tiṉkiṟatē niṉivu.
Even in its dreams, the crow's thoughts turn on eating filth.
2348. kaṉavil kaṇṭa paṇam celaviṟku utavumā?
Will the money seen in a dream meet one's expenses?
2349. kaṉavil kaṇṭa poruḷ kāṉalil kaṇṭa puṉal.
The wealth seen in a dream, the water observed in a mirage.
2350. kaṉavil uṇṭa cōṟu paci tīrkkumā?
Will the rice consumed in a dream appease hunger ?
2351. kaṉavilē kaṇṭavaṉukkup peṇ koṭutta kati.
The story of a woman given in marriage to a man seen in a dream.
2352. kaṉinta paḻam tāṉē viḻum.
Ripe fruit falls of itself.
2353.kaṉinta paḻam nīr tiṉṟīr kāyi ulukkiviṭṭīr.
You have eaten the ripe fruit, and you have shaken off the unripe.
2354. kaṉiyi viṭṭuk kāyit tiṉkiṟatā?
What, to leave ripe fruit and to eat unripe ?
2355. kaṉmattiṉāṟ cātiyaṉṟic ceṉmattiṉāl illi.
Caste arises from action, it is not from birth.
2356. kaṉamaḻi peytālum karuṅkal kariyumā ?
Will granite be dissolved by any amount of hil?
2357. kaṉmattiṉāl vantatu taṉ mantiṉāl pōkavēṇṭum.
That which has come of action sin must go by alms giving religiousmerit.
2358. kaṉṟu irukkak kācattaṉi pāl kaṟavāp pacu kaṉṟu cetta piṟaku kalappāl kaṟakkumā?
Will a cow that gave milk worth a small coin while its calf lived, give
a kalam after her calf is dead ?
2359. kaṉṟukkuṭṭiyi aviḻkkac coṉṉārkaḷā kaṭṭuttaṟiyip piṭuṅkac coṉṉārkaḷā?
Did they tell you to loose the calf or to pull up the peg?
2360. kaṉṟum pacuvum kāṭēṟi mēyntāl kaṉṟu kaṉṟu vaḻiyē pacu pacu vaḻiyē.
When the calf and cow go out to graze, each takes its own way.
2361. kaṉṟukaḷāyk kūṭik kaḷi paṟikkap pōṉāl vikkōl ākumā cetti ākumā?
If a herd of calves weed the corn, will there be any straw, or ever dry
stubble?
2362. kaṉṟik kaṇṭu ōṭivarum pacuvippōlē.
Like a cow that comes running on seeing her calf.
2363. kaṉṉāṉ naṭamāṭak kuyavaṉ kuṭipōvāṉ.
When the brazier begins to move, the potter will abandon his
dwelling-place.
2364. kaṉṉāṉukku muṉṉē kaḻuti paratēcam pōṉāṟpōlē.
As an ass went on a pilgrimage before a copper-smith.
2365. kaṉṉi irukkak kāḷi maṇam pōkalāmā?
When a virgin is yet unmarried, may the youth-her brother marry?
2366. kākattilē veḷḷi uṇṭā?
Are there any white crows ?
2367. kākattiṉ kaṇṇukkup pīrkkampū poṉ niṟam.
To the eye of a crow the flower of the gourd is tinged with gold.
2368. kākam kar eṉṟāl kaṇavaṉi appā eṉṟu kaṭṭikkoḷḷuvāḷām.
It is sid that when a crow caws she will exclim, alas, and cling to
her husband.
2369. kākam ēṟip paṉaṅkāy utirumā ?
Will palmyra fruit fall becuse a crow alights on the tree ?
2370. kākam illāta ūr pāvi illāta ūr.
Vicious persons are not found in a village which has no crows.
2371. kākam irukkap paḻam viḻuntatupōla.
Like the dropping of the fruit when a crow alighted on the tree.
2372. kākam illāta ūr cōṉakaṉ illāta ūr.
The country in which crows do not exist is without mussalmans.
2373. kākam kālamē kā kā eṉṟu kattum.
Crows will caw at day-break.
2374. kākkaṉukkum pūkkaṉukkum pūttāyō puṉṉi kaṇṇāḷar varuntaṉiyum poṟukkaliyō
puṉṉi?
O punni tree, Alexandrian lurel, hast thou blossomed for Kakan and
Pookan, couldst not thou have waited till the arrival of my husband ?
2375. kākkāyk kuñcu āṉālum taṉ kuñcu poṉ kuñcu.
Though but a young crow, it is a golden one to its mother.
2376. kākkiyiṟ karitu kaḷāmpaḻam.
Kala fruit, earissa, is blacker than a crow.
2377. kākkiyum kuyiṟ kuñcit taṉ kuñcu pōl vaḷarkkum.
Even a crow will bring up a young kuyil-India cuckoo-as her own.
2378. kākkikku añcu kuṇam.
The crow has five characteristics.
2379. kākki ēṟiṉatum paṉampaḻam viḻuntatum.
The alighting of the crow, and the falling of the palmyra fruit.
2380. kākkiyum kattik kattip pōkiṟatu karuvāṭum ularntu pōkiṟatu.
The crowcontinues to caw whilst the karuvadu,(salted fish) dries.
2381. kākkiyum kāṟṟum pōkku uṇṭāṉāl varum.
Crows and windsgo but to return.
2382. kākkiyik kaṇṭu añcuvāḷ karaṭiyip piṭittuk kaṭṭuvāḷ.
She is afrid on seeing a crow, and yet she will capture a bear.
2383.kākki ēṟiṉ kompu aciyātā?
Will not the branch shake on which a crow alights?
2384. kāci iraṇṭu eḻuttuttāṉ kāṇa ettaṉi nāṭcellum?
Kasi is formed of two letters only; how many days will it take to reach
it?
2385. kācikkup pōyum karumam toliyavilli.
Though one has gone to Benares his sins are not removed.
2386. kācikkup pōṉālum akappi arikkācu.
Though taken to Benares, awooden ladle will fetch but half acash.
2387. kācikkup pōṉālum kati peṟa vaḻi illi.
Although he may go to Kasi, he will not find the way to heaven.
2388. kācikkuppōyum muṭattavaci kālil viḻukiṟatā?
What ! having gone to Kasi is it to fall at the feet of a lame ascetic?
2389. kāciyil irukkiṟavaṉ kaṇṇik kuttak kāñcipurattiliruntu kiyi nīṭṭikkoṇṭu
pōkiṟatā?
If you wish to strike the eyes of one at Kasi, do you go, for that
purpose, with outstretched band from Conjevaram?
2390. kācu illātavaṉukku varākaṉ pēccu eṉṉa?
Why should a penniless man talk about pagodas?
2391. kācu koṭuttāl vēci varuvāḷ, kala nellik koṭuttāl avaḷ akkāḷum āttāḷum
kūṭa varuvārkaḷ.
If you are free with your money a harlot willcome, if you give also a
kalam of rice, har sister and her mother will come.
2392. kācukku oru kuñcu viṟṟālum kaṇakkak kuñcu ākātu.
Though young birds are sold a cash each, an accountant's young bird is
not good.
2393. kācukku oru kuñcu viṟṟālum kammāḷak kuñcu ākātu.
Though sold for a single cash, never buya smith's bird.
2394. kācukku oru puṭivi viṟṟālum nāyiṉ cūttu ammaṇam.
Though cloth may be cheap; dogs go naked.
2395. kācukkūṭu karikkūṭāyp pōyiṟṟu.
The money basket has become a charcoal basket.
2396. kācukkup pōṉa māṉam kōṭi koṭuttālum varātu.
Chastity lost for a cash will not be recovered by a crore.
2397. kācik koṭuttuk kuttu māṭu tēṭukiṟatā?
Will you buy a vicious cow at a high price?
2398.kāñcipurattiṟkup pōṉāl kāl āṭṭi uṇṇalām.
If you go to Conjeverarm, you may eat without labour.
2399. kāṭikkup pōyt tayir koṇṭuvantatu pōla.
Like going for vinegar and bringing curds.
2400. kāṭu kāttavaṉum kaṟcēri kāttavaṉum palaṉ aṭivāṉ.
Both will be benefited, he who watches the jungle and he who waits at
the cutchery.
2401. kāṭu vā eṉkiṟatu vīṭu pō eṉkiṟatu.
Home bids me go, and the place of incremation sayscome.
2402. kāṭu viḷintālum oru mēṭu viḷintālum kaṭaṉ kaḻintupōm.
The debt will be discharged in either case, whether the jungle or
highland yield a
good crop.
2403. kāṭu viḷiyāviṭṭālum kaṭami pōkumā?
Though the waste land has yielded nothing, will the tax be remitted?
2404. kāṭu ventāṟ cantaṉa maramum vēkātō?
Should the jungle be consumed, would the sandal wood tree escape?
2405. kāṭi iṭam āṉāl nāṭṭi āḷalām.
If a quail crosses one's path to the left, he will govern a province.
2406. kāṭṭik koṭuttuk kaṭakkap pōy niṟkalāmā?
Is it proper to betray one, and then stand aloof?
2407. kāṭṭil āṉiyik kāṭṭi vīṭṭil peṇṇik koṭukkiṟatā?
Is it to give a girl in marriage when an elephant in the jungle is shown
as dower?
2408. kāṭṭilē cettālum vīṭṭilētāṉ tīṭṭu.
Though a man dies in the jungle, ceremonial uncleanness will attach to
his house.
2409. kāṭṭup pūṉikkuc civarāttiri viratamā?
Does the wild cat observe the fast of Sivaratri?
2410. kāṭṭukku eṟitta nilāvum kāṉalukkup peyta maḻiyum.
Moonlight in the jungle and rin in the plins.
2411. kāṭṭu vāḻi vantāl vīṭṭu vāḻvu pōkum.
When wild plantins come, domestic prosperity will vanish.
2412. kāṭṭup pēyccurikkāy kaṟikku ākumā?
Is a wild gourd fit for curry?
2413. kāṭṭuk kōḻikku uraṟ kuḻiyē kilācam.
The hole in a mortar is paradise to a jungle fowl.
2414. kāṭṭup pūccikaḷ etirtta kati āccu.
It has become the story of wild insects offering resistance.
2415. kāṇak kiṭittatu kārttikip piṟipōla.
That which was seen was like the new moon of November.
2416. kāṇappaṭṭaṉa ellām aḻiyappaṭṭaṉa.
All that is seen is temporary.
2417. kāṇāmal kaṇṭēṉō kampaṅkatirē cintāmal kuttaṭi cilli mūkkē ?
Have I seen kambu-millet for the first time, pound it without scattering
thou nose
bleeding woman?
2418. kāṇi āḷaṉ vīṭu vēkaccē kālip piṭittu iḻutta kati.
The story of one pulling by the feet the landlord whose house was on
fire.
2419. kāṇi ēṟak kōṭi aḻiyum.
While 1/80 is added a crore is destroyed.
2420. kāṇi kaviḻntu pōkiṟatā ?
Does land turn upside down?
2421. kāṇi kāṇiyāyc campātittuk kōṭi kōṭiyāyc celavaḻikka vēṇṭum.
We must acquire by eightieths and spend by crores.
2422. kāṇikku ottatu kōṭikku.
The profit or loss on an eightieth part of a unit, will determine that
on a crore.
2423. kāṇi cōmpal kōṭi varuttam.
A little indolence creates great trouble.
2424. kāṇi mantam kōṭi tukkam.
A little indolence will bring great sorrow
2425. kātam viṭṭu irukātam cuṟṟukiṟatu pōla.
Like ranging over the distance of two kathams instead of one.
2426. kātam koṭuttu irukkātam vāṅkukiṟatu pōla.
Like giving a katham and getting two.
2427. kātavaḻi pōy aṟiyātāṉ mātam ellām naṭantāṉām.
He who never walked a katham is sid to have walked a whole month.
2428. kātavaḻi pōy aṟiyāṉ kaḻutip piṟappu.
He who never walked a katham is an ass.
2429. kātavaḻi peyar illāṉ kaḻutiyōṭu okkum.
He whose reputation does not reach a katham is like an ass.
2430. kātilē nārācam ēṟṟiṉāṟ pōla.
Like an iron wire passed into the ear.
2431. kātil cilanti ōtaṭi āṉanti.
O Ananti, I have a boil in my ear, utter an incantation.
2432. kātu aṟutta kūli kimēlē.
The hire for tearing the ear is readily pid.
2433. kātu aṟuttālum aṟukkum pēṉip pārttālum pārkkum kuraṅku.
A monkey may tear the ears or hunt lice if he choose.
2434. kātu kātu eṉṟāl nāti nāti eṉkiṟāṉ.
If one says, my ear, my ear, he replies it is mine, it is mine.
2435. kātu kātu eṉṟāl ceviṭu ceviṭu eṉkiṟāṉ.
If one cry an ear, an ear, he cries deafness deafness.
2436. kātu kuttap poṟukkātā?
Can you not bear the boring of your ears?
2437. kātukku iṭṭāl mukattukku aḻaku.
If worn in the ears, they will add beauty to the face.
2438. kātukkuk kaṭukkaṉ iṭṭu āṭṭikkoṇṭu tirikiṟāṉ.
Having put on ear-rings he walks about shaking them.
2439. kātum kātum vittāṟpōl irukkavēṇṭum.
It should be as if an ear, and an ear, were brought in contact.
2440. kāti aṟuttavaṉ kaṇṇiyum kuttuvāṉā?
Will he who cut off the ear strike the eye also ?
2441. kāttiruntavaṉ peṇṭāṭṭiyi vēṟṟu maṉitaṉ piṭittukkoṇṭāṉ.
A stranger took possession of the wife of him who was watching her.
2442. kāntamum ūciyum pōl.
Like a magnet and needle.
2443. kāppāṉukkuk kaḷḷaṉ illi.
He who takes care of his property will not be robbed.
2444. kāyam paṭṭa kuraṅkupōl.
Like a wounded monkey.
2445. kāyum puḻuviṟkuc cāyum niḻal pōla.
Like a falling shadow on a sun-striken worm.
2446. kāytta marattil kal eṟipaṭum kāyāta marattil kal eṟi paṭumā?
Stones are thrown at a fruit bearing tree; are they thrown at that which
does not
bear?
2447. kāytta marattilē kal eṟiyum cil eṟiyum.
Stones and other missiles are thrown at a fruit-bearing tree.
2448. kāytta maram vaḷintu niṟkum naṟkuṇam uṭiyavar taṇintu niṟpār.
A fruit-bearing tree bends; the virtuous are lowly.
2449. kāynta maram taḷirkkumā ?
Will a dry tree bud?
2450. kāynta māṭu kampilē viḻuntāṟpōla.
Like a starving cow falling in the kambu-millet.
2451. kāynta puli āṭṭu mantiyilē viḻukiṟatupōla .
Like a hungry tiger falling on a flock of sheep.
2452. kāynta puli āvilē viḻukiṟatu.
A lean tiger falls on a cow.
2453. kāyntālum vennīr avam pōmō?
Is water useless becuse it is boiled?
2454. kāynta vāṉam pēyntāl viṭātu ?
Rain after drought will not soon cease ?
2455. kāyntum keṭuttatu pēyntum keṭuttatu.
It was destroyed by the sun and by the rin.
2456. kārāmpacuvukkup pullāṉāl nantavaṉattukkuk kaḷiyumām.
Grass serves as food for kine, and as an ornament to a flower garden.
2457. kāriki kaṟṟuk kavi colluvatiṟ pēriki koṭṭip piḻippatu naṉṟu.
It is better to live by beating a drum than by composing verses
according to the rules
of prosody.
2458. kāriyakkāraṉ kolliyilē kaḻuti mēykutu.
The ass grazes in the grounds of the industrious.
2459. kāriyattilē kaṇ allāmal vīriyattilē illi.
His eye is on the object, not on the attendant appluse.
2460. kāriyattukkuk kaḻutiyiṉ kālip piṭi.
If necessary, secure your purpose by clinging to the feet of an ass.
2461. kāriyam peritō vīriyam peritō?
Which is the greater, success or boasting?
2462. kār aṟukkaṭṭum kattari pūkkaṭṭum.
Wait till the kar paddy is reaped, and the brinjal blossoms.
2463. kārttikikku mēl maḻiyum illi, karṇaṉukku mēl koṭiyum illi.
No rin like that of November, no liberality superior to that of Karnan.
Karnan, one of the seven princes distinguished for great generosity.
2464. kārttikip piṟipōlak kaṇṭēṉ.
I saw it like the third-day moon of November.
2465.kālañ ceykiṟatu kōlam ceyyātē.
Time will effect that which mere parade cannot.
2466. kālañ ceykiṟatu ñālam ceyyātu.
The world cannot accomplish that which time effects.
2467. kālattiṉāl ceyta naṉṟi.
A benefit conferred opportunely.
2468. kālattiṟ peyta maḻipōl.
Like seasonable rin.
2469. kālattukku ēṟṟa kōlam.
Equipage suited to the occasion.
2470. kālamē eḻuntiruntu kākki viḻikkal ākātu.
The sight of a crow on rising of a morning is ominous of evil.
The crow being regarded as the vehicle of Saturn the sight of a single
crow is inuspicious; two are not so regarded. The cawing of a crow may
excite apprehension. When this is the case the following formula may be
used.
Measure the length of your shadow by the foot and add 12 to the number
of feet ascertined. Then divide by 7 and the reminder will determine
the import of the bird's note (1) Good luck, (2) Gain , (3) Rain, (4)
Conflict, (5) Feasting, (6) Victory. If on dividing by 7 there is no
reminder the cawing of the crow is ominous of death.
2471. kālam aṟintu ñālam oḻuku.
Regulate your affairs with due reference to the times in which you live.
2472. kālam keṭṭuk kippicci eṭuttāṟpōla.
Like receiving alms when in distress.
2473. kālam aṟintu piḻiyātavaṉ vāl aṟunta kuraṅku āvāṉ.
He who does not live according to the times will become an ape.
2474. kālam pōm vārtti niṟkum, kappal pōl tuṟi niṟkum.
Time goes, words remin, a ship goes, the shore remins.
2475. kālam pōṉa kālattil mūlam vantu kuṟukkiṭṭatu pōla.
As one is subject to piles in his riper years.
2476. kālam allāta kālattilē kappal ōṭṭi.
A mariner in anfavourable weather.
2477. kālālē naṭantāl kātavaḻi, taliyālē naṭantāl evvaḷavu tūram?
If on foot it is a katham; how much more distant if one walk on the
head?
2478. kālāl muṭintatik kiyāl aviḻkkakkūṭātu.
What he tied with his feet others cannot untie with the hands.
2479. kālilē paṭṭapiṟakā kirakacāram pōtātu ?
After having hurt the foot is it to be attributed to planetary
influence?
2480. kālil viḻukiṟatu nallatu mēlil viḻukiṟatu keṭṭatu.
To fall at the feet is good, to fall on another is bad.
2481. kālil paṭṭatu kaṇṇil paṭṭatu pōla.
That which struck the feet was felt as if it had struck the eye.
2482. kālukkup pōṭṭāl talikkup pōṭukiṟāṉ.
If he puts ornaments on his legs, he will put ornaments on his head.
2482. kālukkuk kaṭuppē tavira, kaṇṭa palaṉ oṉṟum illi.
No benefit accrued but the trouble of walking.
2484. kālukkuk ki utavi kikkuk kāl utavi.
The hand helps the legs and the legs help the hands.
2485. kālukkut takka ceruppum kūlikkut takka uḻippum.
A slipper suited to the foot, and labour suited to the hire.
2486. kālukku ākiṟa ceruppu talikku ākumā?
Will a slipper that fits the foot do for the head?
2487. kālic cuṟṟiṉa pāmpu kaṭittāl oḻiya viṭātu.
The snake that has coiled round the leg will not leave without biting.
2488. kālic cel pūttāl aṭutta maḻi aṭaṅkum.
Should winged white-ants come out in the morning the heavy rin will
cease.
2489. kāli tuyilvāṉum māli iruppāṉum patar.
He who sleeps after day-break, and he who keeps awake in the fore part
of the night are worthless.
2490. kālip piṭitta caṉiyaṉ ūric cuṟṟi aṭikkum.
If Saturn seize the feet, he will drive one round the village.
2491. kālip paṉikkum kaṇ viḻikkum ottatu celvam.
Wealth is comparable to the morning dew, and the twinkling of an eye.
2492. kāli mōṭcamum vāli ñāṉamum nilikkātu.
Dreams of bliss and premature wisdom are not lasting.
2493. kāl aḷavē ākumām kappaliṉ ōṭṭam nūl aḷavē ākumām nuṇcīli.
The speed of a vessel is proportioned to the force of the wind, the
quality of cloth will
be as the yarn used in its manufacture.
2494. kāl āṭak kōl āṭum kōl āṭak kuraṅku āṭum.
The foot puts the stick in motion, as that moves, the monkey dances.
2495. kāl ciṟitākil kaṉal ūrum kaṉṉiyarmēl māl ciṟitākil maṉam ūrum.
Where there is a little wind fire will spread, where there is lust the
affections will be fastened on women.
2496. kāl naṭikku iraṇṭu kācu ki vīccukku aintu kācu.
Two cash for walking on foot, five cash for the swing of the arms.
2497. kāl māṟik kaṭṭiṉāl kaṉam kuṟiyumā?
If one be tied cross-legged, will his weight diminish?
2498. kālvāyit tāṇṭātavaṉ kaṭalit tāṇṭuvāṉā?
Can he who cannot leap over a channel leap over the sea?
2499. kāvaṭip pāram cumakkiṟavaṉukkut teriyum.
The weight of a burden is felt by the bearer.
2500. kāval tāṉē pāviyarkku aḻaku.
Chastity is a feminine grace.
2501. kāvēri āṟṟi maṟippāy kārttiki mātattuk kaṟkaṭaka cantiraṉiyum maṟippāyā?
You may stay the Cauvery, but can you hinder the full moon of November
if he be in Cancer ?
2502. kāvēri kañciyāyp pōṉālum nāy nakkittāṉ kuṭikka vēṇṭum.
Should the Cauvery become kanji, the dog would partake thereof by
lapping.
2503. kāvōli viḻuntateṉṟu kuruttōli cirittatām.
The green leaves of the palm lughed becuse the dry ones fell off.
2504. kāḷitōṭṭattuk kaṟpaka virukṣam ārukkum utavātu.
Even the katpaka tree in the garden of Durga is of no use to man kind.
The katpaka tree is sid to yield whatever a suppliant pay require.
2505. kāḷippaṭṭam pōṉālum mūḷippaṭṭam pōkātu.
The name Kali may become obsolete, but not the (nickname) Mooli, the
earless.
2506. kāṟ kācup pūṉi mukkāṟ kācut tayirik kuṭittatu.
A cat worth a quarter of a cash consumed curds worth three quarters of a
cash.
2507. kāṟṟil akappaṭṭa kappal pōl alikiṉṟatu maṉam.
The mind is agitated like a ship in a storm.
2508. kāṟṟukkā maḻikkā pōrttukkoḷḷa tuṇikkā?
Is it as a security aginst the wind and rin that you are thus clad?
2509. kāṟṟukku etirilē tuppiṉāl mukattilē viḻum.
If one spit aginst the wind-the spittle-will strike his own face.
2510. kāṟṟukkut tōṇi etirttu ōṭātu.
A dhony cannot sil aginst the wind.
2511. kāṟṟum cilari nīkki vīcumō?
Will the wind blow so as to avoid certin persons?
2512. kāṟṟu uḷḷapōtu tūṟṟikkoḷ karumpu uḷḷapōtu āṭṭikkoḷ.
Winnow when the wind blows, work the sugar-mill when the cane is ripe.
2513. kāṟṟip piṭittuk karakattil aṭikkalāmā ?
Can one seize the wind and confine it in a small vessel?
2514. kāṟṟip pārttuk kappal nāṭṭu.
Anchor a ship with reference to the wind.
2515. kāṉali nīreṉṟu eṇṇi māṉ ōṭi iḷittatu pōla.
Like the deer that wearied itself in pursuit of a mirage imagining it to
be water.
2516. kiṭakkiṟatu kiṭakkaṭṭum kiḻavaṉi eṭuttu maṇiyilē vi.
Leave the things as they are, and take the old man and put him on the
stool to bathe.
The first thing done before a corpse is removed from the homestead is to
bathe it.
2517. kiṭakkiṟatu kuṭṭiccuvar kaṉāk kāṇkiṟatu maccuvīṭu.
Dwelling in a ruinous hut and dreaming of a palace.
2518. kiṭṭa vā nāyē eṉṟāl mūñciyi nakkukiṟatu.
When called, the dog licks the face.
2519. kiṭṭāta oṉṟi veṭṭeṉa maṟa.
Forget at once what cannot be obtined.
2520. kiṇaṟu veṭṭap pūtam puṟappaṭṭāṟpōl āyiṟṟē.
It has happened as if a demon had sprung out of a well just dug.
2521. kiṇaṟum veṭṭit tavaḷiyum piṭittu viṭukiṟatā?
What! is it to dig a well and supply it with frogs?
2522. kiṇaṟu kiṭakka mali kallātē.
Whilst there is a well do not excavate amountin for water.
2523. kiṇaṟu veṭṭit tākam tīrkkalāmā?
Must one dig a well to quench his thirst?
2524. kiṇaṟu mettiṉāṟ kīḻvari pociyum.
If there be water the undermost ring of the well will be wet.
2525. kiṇaṟu iṟikka iṟikkac curakkum.
The more a well is drawn, the better the spring.
2526.kiṇaṟu tappit turavil viḻalāmā?
Having escaped falling into the well, shall we fall into a tank?
2527. kiṇaṟṟit tūrttāl vayiṟṟit tūrkkum.
If the well in to be filled up, the belly must be filled up.
2528. kiṇaṟṟut tavaḷi taṇṇīr kuṭittatik kaṇṭatu ār, kuṭiyātatik kaṇṭatu ār ?
Who knows whether the frog in the well did or did not drink water?
2529. kiṇaṟṟut tavaḷikku ēṉ nāṭṭu vaḷappam?
What has the frog in the wall to do with the news of the country?
2530. kiṇaṟṟukkut tappit tīyilē pāyntāṉ.
Having escaped falling into the well he jumped into the fire.
2531. kiṇaṟṟik kaṇṭu kaṭal otuṅkippōkumā?
Will the sea shrink at the sight of a well?
2532. kiṇaṟṟut taṇṇīri veḷḷam koṇṭupōkumā ?
Will the flood carry away the water of the well ?
2533.kiṇaṟṟilē taṇṇīr utittatu.
Water sprang up in the well.
2534. kiṇaṟṟu āḻamum kayiṟṟu nīḷamum pārkkavēṇṭum.
The depth of the well, and the length of the cord must be ascertined.
2535. kiṇaṟṟil viḻuntavaṉ maṟupaṭiyum viḻuvāṉā?
Will he who has fallen into a well once fallinagin?
2536. kiṇaṟṟil kal pōṭṭatu pōla.
Like dropping a stone into awell.
2537. kiṇṭak kiṇṭa ampaṭṭaṉ kuppi mayirē puṟappaṭum.
The more you dig in the refuse heapof the barber, the more will hir
turn up.
2538. kiṇṭak kiṇṭak kīriyum mayirum.
The more youdig, the more will greens and hir appear.
2539. kiraka cāntikkuc cavaram paṇṇik koḷḷukiṟatā?
Will shaving counteract the evil influence ofaplanet?
2540. kiriyi aṟṟōṉ maṟi cāṟṟuvatu ēṉ?
What! is it for an evil-doer to teach religious precepts?
2541. kirupā natiyē caruvā niti.
The stream of grace is the source of all treasures.
2542. kiḻak kuṭalukkuc cōṟum iṭic cuvarukku maṇṇum iṭu.
Give rice to the aged and add mud to a ruinous wall.
2543. kiḻap pēccu capikku ēṟumā?
Will the words of an old man go up to the assembly?
2544. kiḻavaṉ āṉālum keṭṭi āṉālum kaṭṭikkoṇṭavaḷ piḻippāḷ.
She who marries will do well whether her husband be old or poor.
2545. kiḻavaṉukku vāḻkkippaṭukiṟatilum kiṇaṟṟil viḻalām.
It is better to fall into a well than to marry an old man.
2546. kiḻavi pēccik kiṉṉarakkāraṉ kēṭpāṉō?
Will a musician listen to the speech of an old woman?
2547. kiḻaviyum kātam kutiriyum kātam.
The old woman is a katham from the horse, and the horse a katham from
the old woman.
2548. kiḻavi irunta vīṭum kiḷi irunta kāṭum īṭēṟa māṭṭātu.
Neither the house of an old woman, nor the grove in which there are
parrots, will be saved from ruin.
2549. kiḻinta cīli kācukku iraṇṭu,
Two rags for a cash.
2550. kiḻinta pamparam kācukku iraṇṭu.
Two split tops for a cash.
2551. kiḷiyi vaḷarttup pūṉi kiyil koṭukkalāmā?
Is it proper to trin a parrot and give it into the paw of acat?
2552. kiḷiyippōlap pēccum mayilippōla naṭiyum.
Speech like that of a parrot, git like that of a peacock.
2553. kiḷḷap paḻukkumām kiḷi iruntu koñcumām.
It is sid that it ripens by being pinched, and that parrots will nibble
it leisurely.
2554. kiḷḷuvārkīḻ iruppatilum aḷḷuvārkīḻ irukkalām.
One may endure those who rob, but not those who pilfer.
2555. kīri kaṭitta pāmpupōlē.
Like a snake bit by a mungoose.
2556. kīrikkum pāmpukkum tīrāp paki.
Inveterate enmity exists between the mungoose and snakes.
2557. kīriyum pāmpum pōla.
Like a mungoose and a snake.
2558. kīrik kaṭikkum etirkaṭi vēṇṭum.
An opposition shop is good even among green grocers.
2559. kīrikkup pulluruvi kīḻē muḷittāṟpōlē.
As if a parasite should spring from the lower part of a vegetable.
2560. kīrik kaṭṭi veṭṭac coṉṉāl tōraṇam kaṭṭukiṟatā?
When I order the greens to be pruned, is a garland put up?
2561. kīrit taṇṭu piṭuṅka ēlēlappāṭṭu ēṉ?
Why sing elela when plucking up greens?
2562. kīriyi iraṇṭu kaṟi paṇṇātē.
Do not make two curries of a vegetable.
2563. kīrttiyāl paci tīrumā?
Will hunger be appeased by fame?
2564. kīrtti peṟṟum kilēcam eṉṉa?
Whence sorrow to himwho has obtined prise?
2565. kīḻē pāmpu eṉṟāl mēlē pārkkiṟāṉ.
If it is sid that a snake is below, he looks up.
2566. kīḻkulattāṉ āṉālum kaṟṟavaṉ mēlkulattāṉ.
Though of low origin, the learned ranks with the highest class.
2567. kīḻkkātu mēṟkātu mūḷi, caṇṭikku raṇapattirakāḷi.
Her ears are rent at both ends; in an affray she is equal to Durga.
2568. kīṟi āṟṟiṉāl puṇ āṟum.
If lanced, the sore will heal.
2569. kuṅkiliyat tūpam kāṭṭic caṉṉatam kulintāl kumpiṭu eṅkē ?
If indeed the incense has expelled the demon, where is the sign?
-uplifted hands.
2570. kuṅkumam cumanta kaḻuti parimaḷam aṟiyumā?
Can the ass which carried the kungumam-a fragrant resin-appreciate
scents?
2571.kuṅkumak kōtikku añcu paṇam, kuruṭṭuk kaṇṇikkum, añcu paṇamā?
The donation to the perfumed dame being five fanams, is five fanams to
be assigned
to the blind woman, also?
2572. kuñcirippukku maruntu cāppiṭa uḷḷa cirippum pōṉāṟ pōla.
As if one lost his natural smile by taking medicine to induce a
şimpering expression.
2573. kuṭattil ēṟṟiya viḷakku.
A lamp lit in an earthen pot.
2574. kuṭattil poṉ kūttu āṭumā?
Will gold in a pot dance?
2575. kuṭappāl kaṟantālum kutiri ōṭṭam ōṭamāṭṭātu.
Though the cow gives a large pot of milk, it is not equal to the horse
in speed.
2576. kuṭappāl kaṟantālum kūri piṭuṅkukiṟa māṭu ākātu.
Though it may yield a large pot of milk, a cow that destroys the roof is
not desirable.
2577. kuṭam taṇṇīriṟ koḷḷi vittāṟpōla.
As if a firebrand were put into a pot of water.
2578. kuṭalum kūntalum koṇṭatu koḷki.
Habit is the rule of the stomach and the hir.
2579. kuṭal kūḻukku aḻukiṟatām koṇṭi pūvukku aḻukiṟatām.
It is sid that the stomach cries for gruel, and the tresses for
flowers.
2580. kuṭal aṟunta nari entamaṭṭum ōṭum ?
How far can a jackal run after its bowels are dislodged ?
2581. kuṭal aṟunta kōḻi eṅkē pōkum?
Whither will a fowl go after its entrils have been exposed?
2582. kuṭi iruntu aṟi vaḻi naṭantu aṟi.
Learn the character of a person by dwelling with him, and the condition
of a road by
walking in it.
2583. kuṭi iruntu pār kūṭṭuppayir iṭṭup pār.
Test his social qualities by living with him, and his honesty byjoint
tillage.
2584. kuṭi iruppatu kuccuvīṭu, kaṉāk kaṇṭatu maccumāḷiki.
He who dwells ina humble cottage dreams ofceiled apartments.
2585. kuṭi illā vīṭṭiṟ kuṇṭup peruccāḷi ulāvum.
Bandycootes will run about in an uninhabited house.
2586. kuṭi illā ūrilē aṭi iṭa ākātu.
It is a risk to set a foot in an uninhabited village.
2587. kuṭi illā ūrukku nari irācaṉ.
A jackal is king in a deserted village.
2588. kuṭi uṭiyāṉē muṭi uṭiyāṉ.
He is fit for kingship who is approved by the people.
2589. kuṭikkiṟatu kūḻāk koppaḷikkiṟatu paṉṉīrām.
He has gruel to drink, and washes his mouth with rose water.
2590. kuṭikkiṟa pāli veṭippilē vārkkiṟatā?
What, is it to pour down the drinkable milk into a crevice?
2591. kuṭikkiṟatu kāṭi nīr, ataṟkut taṅkavaṭṭilā ?
What you drink is sour gruels, do you require a cup of fine gold for it?
2592. kuṭikkiṟatu kūḻām irukkiṟatu ciṅkācaṉamām.
Living on gruel and sitting on a throne.
2593. kuṭikkiṟa vīṭu viṭiyumā?
Will the family of a drunkard prosper?
2594. kuṭittaṉamō turittaṉamō etu peritu?
Whether is greater, the government of a family or of a state ?
2595. kuṭittaṉam mēliṭa vēṇṭi piṭāriyip peṇṭuvittuk koṇṭāṉ.
Wishing to elevate his family he married a Pidari-village goddess.
2596. kuṭittaṉameṉṟu paṇṇiṉāl naṉmiyum varum tīmiyum varum.
If one becomes a householder, he must expect both good and evil.
2597. kuṭipōṉa vīṭṭilē vaṟaṭṭu nāy kāttatu pōla.
As a lean dog watched a deserted house.
2598. kuṭimatam aṭipaṭat tīrum.
The madness of a drunkard may be cured by beating.
2599. kuṭiyaṉum veṟiyaṉum cari.
A drunkard and a mad man are alike.
2600. kuṭiyāta vīṭu viṭiyātu.
The family of a drunkard is always benighted.
2601. kuṭiyiṟ peṇ vayiṟu erintāl koṭiyiṟ cēli niṉṟu eriyum.
If the bowels of the house-wife burn, her cloth hanging on the line will
burn also.
2602. kuṭiyillā ūrilē oṟṟip paṇakkāraṉ.
He is the only wealthy man in a deserted village.
2603. kuṭiyiṟ piṟantu kuraṅku āṭṭam āṭutal.
Born in a good family, and playing the monkey.
2604. kuṭiyiṟ piṟantu ceṭiyil viḻuntāṉ.
Born in a good family and falling into a bush.
2605. kuṭi vittuk koṇṭāyō koḷḷi vittuk koṇ ṭāyō?
Have you taken a family to dwell with you ? Or have you taken
firebrands?
2606. kuṭi vitta vīṭṭiṟ koḷḷi vikkalāmā?
Is it proper to burn down a house after admitting a family?
2607. kuṭṭik kiṭiyilē ōṉāy pukuntatupōl.
As a wolf entered a fold of lambs.
2608. kuṭṭik kalakam paṇṇukiṟavaṉ kuṭṭuppaṭṭuc cāvāṉ.
He who breeds divisions in a family shall be cuffed to death.
2609. kuṭṭik karaṇam pōṭṭālum lōpaṉ koṭāṉ .
The miser will give nothing though you tumble heels overhead.
2610. kuṭṭic cuvarilē tēḷ koṭṭa, kaṭṭut taṟiyilē neṟi iṭumā?
If a scorpion sting in a ruinous dwarf wall, will the glandular swelling
appear in the
stall?
2611. kuṭṭic cuvarilē tēḷ koṭṭat taṇṇīr miṭāvilē neṟi kaṭṭumā?
Does the sting of a scorpion in a ruinouswall, produce a glandular
swelling in a water-pot?
2612. kuṭṭic cuvari muṭṭa veḷḷeḻuttā?
Is he so dim-sighted as to stumble aginst a ruinous wall ?
2613. kuṭṭi pōṭṭa pūṉi pōl alikiṟāṉ.
He wanders about like a cat that has kittened.
2614. kuṭṭiṉa kuṭṭum kuṇṭiṟ pāynta taṇṇīrum varumā?
Can a cuff already inflicted and water that has run into a pit be
recalled?
2615. kuṭṭi āṭu koḻuttālum vaḻuvaḻuppup pōkātu.
Though it become fat, the flesh of a kid is always clammy.
2616. kuṭṭi kulittu nāypēril vikkiṟatu pōla.
Like pups barking and creating a quarrel among dogs.
2617. kuṭṭi nāykoṇṭu vēṭṭi āṭiṉatupōla.
Like hunting with pups.
2618. kuṭṭu māṉam tappik kucavaṉōṭē pēciṉāl, caṭṭiyum paṇṇāṉ, caṭṭiyum koṭāṉ.
Ifyou speak familiarly with a potter, he will not respect you, nor will
he supply you with chatties.
2619. kuṇatti māṟṟak kuru illi.
No guroo- religious teacher-can change a man's temperament.
A Guru among the Hindus is regarded as a spiritual parent from whom the
youth receives the initiatory mantra or prayer, and whoconducts the
ceremonies necessaryat various seasons of infancy andyouth, up to the
period of in vestiture with the sacred thread or string:this person may
be the natural parent or the religions preceptor. The word Guru is also
used for a teacher or preceptor generally.
2620. kuṇattiṟku aḻukiṟatā, piṇattiṟku aḻukiṟatā ?
Is the weeping on account of the virtue of the deceased or does it
relate to his corpse?
2621. kuṇam uṟṟavaṉ maṇam uṟṟavaṉ, kuṇam aṟṟavaṉ maṇam aṟṟavaṉ.
A good-natured man has an engaging exterior, an ill-natured man has a
repulsive aspect.
2622. kuṇam pāti koṇṭa nōvu pāti.
His suffering is due partly to his faint-heartedness, and partly to a
disease.
2623. kuṇṭāk karaṇam pōṭṭālum piṇṭāc cōṟṟukku vaḻi illi.
Though he makes somersults to all he sees, he has no food to eat.
2624. kuṇṭi kāyntāl kutiriyum vikkōl tiṉṉum.
If starved even a horse will eat straw.
2625. kuṇṭi ettaṉi kōṇaṟ kōṇiṉālum cumi vīṭṭiṟ pōyc cērntālē cari.
No matter how the body may be bent if the burden be carried home.
2626. kuṇṭi aṟunta paruntu pōla.
Like a kite witha broken til.
2627. kuṇṭuc caṭṭiyilē kutiri ōṭṭukiṟāṉ.
He displays his horsemanship in an earthen pot.
2628. kuṇṭu paṭṭuc cākātavaṉ vaṇṭu kaṭittuc cettāṉām.
It is sid that he who survived a gunshot wound, died of the bite of an
insect.
2629. kuṇṭuc caṭṭiyiṟ karaṇam pōṭalāmā?
Can one turn a somersult in an earthen pot ?
2630. kuṇṭup peruccāḷiyum vaṇṭu pōla.
The bandycoote is also like a beetle.
2631. kuṇṭu collukiṟavaṉukku iru nākku kaṭṭuviriyaṉukkum irunākku.
A slanderer and a snake of deadly poison have each two tongues.
2632. kuṇṭi palattāl kuṭi palakkum.
As kine increase, a household increases.
2633. kuti kuti eṉpārkaḷ ellārum, kūṭak kutippār illi.
They will all say leap, leap into the pyre, but there is none willing to
leap with me.
2634. kutittuk kutittu mā iṭittālum puḻukkikku oru koḻukkaṭṭiyē.
Though she may leap joyously and pound the grin, the slave woman gets
but one
cake.
2635. kutiri kuruṭāṉālum koḷḷut tiṉkiṟatiṟ kuṟiccal illi.
Though blind, the horse does not eat the less grin.
2636. kutiri koṇṭāl kaliṉattukku vaḻakkā?
When you have bought the horse, is there any occasion for disputing
about its bit?
2637. kutiriyiṉ kuṇam aṟintallavō tampirāṉ kompu koṭukkavilli?
Was it not becuse the Creator knew the nature of the horse that he did
not provide
him with horns?
2638. kutiri cettatallāmaṟ cēṇam cumakka vēli āyiṟṟu.
Besides the death of the horse there is the saddle also to be carried.
2639. kutirikkum nāykkum kuṭipōkac cantōṣam.
The horse and the dog like a change of place.
2640. kutiri ēṟa atiṣṭam iruntāl kuṇṭiyiṉ kīḻ vantu nuḻiyātā ?
If it be your good fortune to ride, will not your horse come and place
itself under your
seat?
2641. kutiri nallatutāṉ cuḻi keṭṭatu.
It is certinly a good horse, but its circular marks are bad.
The circlets of hir on a horse are generally observed by dealers and
owners very carefully.
2642. kutiri ēṟi eṉṉa , kōṇak kompu ūti eṉṉa, vīṇarkkum kīrttikkum veku tūram.
What if you do ride a horse, and have a hora blown before you?
There is a great distance between the worthless and reputation.
2643. kutiri pākaṉit taḷḷuvatum allāmal kuḻiyum tōṇṭukiṟatām.
It is sid that the horse has not only thrown its rider, but is digging
his grave.
2644. kutiri cettatu allāmal kuḻi tōṇṭa mūṉṟu paṇamā ?
Besides losing the horse, have I to pay three fanams to bury him ?
2645. kutiriyum kātam kiḻaviyum kātam.
The horse is ten miles before you, and the old woman is ten miles behind
you.
2646. kutiri piṭikkac cammaṭṭi aṭikka kūppiṭṭak kuralukku ēṉeṉṉa.
You will attend to my horse, ply the sledge hammer and answer my calls.
2647. kutiri iruppu aṟiyum koṇṭa peṇṭāṭṭi kuṇam aṟivāḷ.
The horse knows his rider, and a wife her husband.
2648. kutti vaṭittālum campā kuppiyilē pōṭṭālum taṅkam.
Though pounded and boiled it is samba-superior rice-though cast on the
rubbish
heap it is gold.
2649. kuttuppaṭṭup poṟuttālum kuṟivayiṟu poṟukkumā?
A cuff may be borne, can hunger?
2650. kunti iruntu tiṉṟāl kuṉṟu māḷum.
If you eat properly seated you may consume a heap of rice.
2651. kupēraṉ paṭṭaṇattilum viṟaku cumakkiṟavaṉ uṇṭu.
Even in the city of Kuberan there were firewood carriers.
2652. kupēraṉ paṭṭaṇam koḷḷi pōṉālum atiṣṭavīṉaṉukku akappik kāmpum akappaṭumā?
Though the city of Kuberan be despoiled, will the unfortunate obtin
even the handle of a wooden spoon?
2653. kuppuṟa viḻuntu tavam ceytālum kurukkaḷukku mōṭcam illi.
Though they may prostrate themselves and perform penance, religious
teachers do not attin heaven.
2654. kuppuṟa viḻuntum mīciyilē maṇ paṭavilli eṉkiṟāṉ.
Though he fell flat on his face, he says that his mustache did not touch
the ground.
2655. kuppiyil muḷitta kīri kappalukkuk kālākumā?
Will a plant grown on a dunghill answer for the mast of a ship?
2656. kuppiyil muḷitta koṭi kūriyil ēṟiṉatupōla.
As a creeper springing on a dunghill climbed on a roof.
2657. kuppiyiṉṟip payir viḷiyātu.
Without manure the growing grin will not yield a good crop.
2658. kuppiyiṟ kiṭantālum kuṉṟimaṇi kuṉṟimaṇitāṉ.
Though it lies on the dunghill the kunrimani-abrus precatoriusremins
unchanged.
2659. kuppiyiṟ putittālum kuṉṟimaṇi niṟam pōkātu.
Though the kunrimani be buried in the dunghill its colour will not
change.
2660. kuppi uyarntatu kōpuram tāḻntatu.
The rubbish heap has risen, the tower has sunk.
2661. kuppi uyarntateṉṉa kōpuram tāḻntateṉṉa?
How is it that the rubbish heap has risen, and the tower sunk?
2662. kuppiyum kōḻiyum pōlak kuruvum cīṣaṉum.
The teacher and his disciple are like the dunghill and the fowl.
2663. kumari oru piḷḷi kōṭi oru veḷḷi .
A woman is young till she bears a child, and cloth is new till it is
washed.
2664. kumari taṉivaḻiyē pōṉālum koṭṭāvi taṉivaḻiyē pōkātu.
Though a young woman may go on her way alone, yawning is never alone.
2665. kumariyāy irukkiyiṟ koṇṭāṭṭam kiḻaviyāy irukkiṟa pōtu tiṇṭāṭṭam.
When young, a woman is full of hilarity, when old her portion is
vexation.
2666. kumpiṭṭuk kaṭaṉ koṭātē kumpiṭṭuk kaṭaṉ vāṅkātē.
Neither lend nor borrow beseechingly.
2667. kumpiṭap pōṉa iṭattil kōyil iṭintu viḻuntatu.
When I went to worship the temple fell.
2668. kumpiṭap pōṉa teyvam kuṟukkē vantatupōl.
As the deity one went to worship came to meet one cross wise.
2669. kumpiṭṭa teyvam kulateyvam.
The deity I worship is my hereditary deity.
2670. kumpiṭum kaḷḷar kuḻintiṭum kaḷḷar.
Obsequious rogues, deceitful friends.
2671. kuyavaṉukkup pala nāḷi vēli, taṭi aṭikāraṉukku oru nimiṣa vēli.
That which cost the potter many days labour, is but the labour of a
moment to the
breaker.
2672. kuyavā kalacam koṇṭuvā , iṭiyā pāl koṭu eṉṟāl pōla.
Like saying to the potter bring a vessel, and to the shepherd fill it
with milk.
2673. kuyil kūviṉāṟpōla.
Like the warbling of the Indian cuckoo.
2674. kuyiṟ kuralum mayil aḻakum pōla.
Like the voice of the Indian cuckoo, and the beauty of the peacock.
2675. kuraṅkiṉ kip pūmāli.
A garland of flowers in the hand of a monkey.
2676. kuraṅkukkup putti collit tūkkaṇaṅkuruvi kūṇṭu iḻantatu.
The advice which the pendulous bird Loxia, gave to the monkey ended in
the destruction of its own nest.
2677. kuraṅkukkum taṉ kuṭṭi poṉ kuṭṭi.
Even to the monkey its own young is precious.
2678. kuraṅkup puṇ āṟātu.
A sore on a monkey never heals.
2679. kuraṅkup puṇ piramāṇṭam.
A sore of the monkey is enormously large.
2680. kuraṅkup piṇamum kuṟap piṇamum kaṇṭavar illi.
No one ever saw the dead body of a monkey, or the corpse of a kuravan-a
mountineer.
2681. kuraṅku taṉ kuṭṭiyiṉ kiyikkoṇṭu patam pārkkiṟatu pōlap pārkkiṟāṉ.
He tests it by another as a monkey tests an object by the hand of its
young.
2682. kuraṅkup piṭipōl piṭikkavēṇṭum.
Lay hold of a thing as firmly as a monkey.
2683. kuraṅkiṉ pīyi maruntukkuk kēṭṭāl koppu kiḷi ellāmtattippāyum.
If a monkey be asked for his droppings for medicinal purposes, he will
leap from branch to branch.
2684. kuraṅku ellām oru mukam.
All monkeys have faces alike.
2685. kuraṅku āṉālum kulattil koḷḷavēṇṭum.
Though she be a mere monkey, one should take a wife in one's own tribe.
2686. kuraṅku ēṟāta kompu uṇṭā ?
Is there a branch the monkey has not climbed?
2687. kuraṅku ellām kūṭṭam kūṭṭamāka irukkum.
All monkeys go about in troops.
2688. kuraṅkiṉ kiyil koḷḷi akappaṭṭa kati.
The story of a firebrand in the hand of a monkey.
2689. kuru illāta cīṣaṉ uṇṭā ?
Is there a disciple without a Guru-teacher?
2690. kuru illārkku vittiyum illi mutal illārkku lāpamum illi.
There is no art without a teacher, and no profit without capital.
2691. kuru eṉa vantāṉ tiru uri tantāṉ.
He came as a Guru; he gave sacred instruction.
2692. kurukkaḷ piḻittatu maṟupiṟappu.
The escape of the religious teacher was as it were a second birth.
2693. kuruṭaṉ kiyil vilāṅku akappaṭṭatu pōla.
As an eel was cught in the hand of a blind man.
2694. kuruṭaṉukku vēṇṭiyatu kōl.
The need of a blind man is a staff.
2695. kuruṭaṉukku vēṇṭiyatu kaṇ.
What the blind want is eye-sight.
2696. kuruṭaṉukkuk kuruṭaṉ kōl piṭikkalāmā?
Can the blind lead the blind with a staff ?
2697. kuruṭaṉ āṭu mēykka eṭṭāḷukku vēliyā?
Is it a work for eight men to help a blind man to feed sheep?
2698. kuruṭaṉ kūttup pārkkap pōṉāl payaṉ eṉṉa?
What advantage will the blind man gin by attending a comedy?
2699. kuruṭaṉukkup pāl kokkup pōṉṟatu.
To the blind milk was like a heron.
2700. kuruṭaṉ peṇṭāṭṭi kūṉaṉōṭu uṟavāṭiṉāḷ.
The wife of a blind man became intimate with a hunch-backed man.
2701. kuruṭaṉ peṇṭiri aṭittāṟpōla.
As if a blind man bent his wife.
2702. kuruṭaṉip pārttu irāca viḻi viḻikkac coṉṉāl viḻippāṉā?
If requested to assume the looks of, a king, can a blind man do so?
2703. kuruṭaṉi nōṭṭam pārkkac coṉṉāṟpōla.
As if a blind man was asked to assay metals.
2704. kuruṭaṉ kik kōlip piṭuṅkiṉatupōla.
Like snatching away the staff of the blind.
2705. kuruṭaṉukku kuruṭaṉ vaḻi kāṭṭiṉāl iruvarum kuḻiyilē viḻuvār.
If the blind lead the blind, both willfall into the pit.
2706. kuruṭi taṇṇīrkkup pōka eṭṭāḷ miṉa keṭṭatu pōl.
As the work of eight persons was stopped by reason of a blindwoman going
to fetch
water.
2707. kuruṭum ceviṭum kūttup pārttatu pōla.
As the blind and the deaf attending comedy.
2708. kuruṭṭuk kutirikkuc caṟukkiṉatu cākku.
The excuse of a blind horse was his stumbling.
2709. kuruṭṭup pūṉi viṭṭattiṟ pāyntatu pōla.
As a blind cat leaped on the cross beam.
2710. kuruṭṭukkokkiṟku ūrkkuḷamē cāṭci.
The village tank is the witness of the blind crane.
2711. kuruṭṭuk kōḻi taviṭṭukku vīṅkiyatu pōla.
As a blind fowl wasover-anxious to feed on bran.
2712. kuruṭṭuk kaṇṇukkuk kuṟuṇi mi iṭṭu āvatu eṉṉa?
Why adorn blind eyes with collyrium?
2713. kuru moḻi kēḷātavaṉum tāy vārttikku aṭaṅkātavaṉum caṇṭi.
He who will not hear the words of his Guru-religious teacher-and he who
disobeys his mother, are worthless.
2714. kuru moḻi maṟantōṉ tiruvaḻintu aḻivāṉ.
He who forgets the words of his Guru will forfeit the divine favor and
perish.
2715. kuruviyiṉ kaḻuttilē paṉaṅkāyik kaṭṭit tūkkalāmā?
Cana palmyra fruit be suspended from the neck of a small bird ?
2716. kuruvikkut takka irāma attiram allavō?
Is not the weapon of Rama suited to the bird?
2717. kuruvikkut takka rāmēcuram.
Rameswaram suited to the bird.
This is I think a perversion of the foregoing proverb. An aged old
feeble person may say, my Rameswaram, pilgrimage, efforts, must be
moderate.
2718. kuruvikkut takka pāram.
A burden suited to the bird.
2719. kuruvik kūṭṭik kōlāṟ kalikkātē.
Never disturb the nest of a bird with a stick.
2720. kuruvukku miñciṉa cīṣaṉā?
Is there a disciple who is above his Guru ?
2721. kuruvukku nāmam kuḻittuppōṭuvāṉ.
He will deceive his own Guru.
2722. kuruvukkut turōkam ceytālum kuṭalukkut turōka ceyyakkūṭātu.
Though a Guru may be deceived, the stomach cannot.
2723. kuru vēṣam koṇṭavaṉ ellām kuru āvāṉā?
Are all religious teachers who have assumed the garb?
2724. kulattik keṭutta kōṭālikkāmpu.
The handle of an axe that destroyed its own species.
The tree from which its handle was taken, was felled by the axe.
2725. kulattik keṭukkumām kuraṅku.
It is sid that a monkey will destroy its own species.
2726. kulamakaṭku aḻaku taṉ koḻunaṉip pēṇutal.
It becomes a lawful wife to honour her husband.
2727. kulamakaṉ kulattukku aḻuvāṉ, mūkkaṟiyaṉ mūkkukkuaḻuvāṉ.
The high-born mourns the loss of caste, and the man who has lost his
nose, his nose.
2728. kulamum oṉṟu kuṟiyum oṉṟu.
Birth and external sign are at variance.
2729. kulam pukuntum kuṟi tīravilli.
Though he has entered another tribe he is still in want.
2730. kulam kulattōṭē veḷḷam āṟṟōṭē.
Tribe goes with tribe, the flood with the river.
2731. kulam kulattōṭē veḷḷāṭu taṉṉōṭē.
A tribe associates with its own tribe, and goats follow their own kind.
2732. kulam kuppiyilē paṇam pantiyilē.
High birth lies on the dunghill, while wealth is at the festive board.
2733. kulavitti kaṟṟup pāti kallāmaṟ pāti.
Arts appropriate to a family are partly intuitive and partly acquired.
2734. kulikkiṟa nāy kaṭikka aṟiyātu.
A barking dog does not bite.
2735. kulikkiṟa nāykku elumpip pōṭṭāṟpōla.
Like throwing a bone to a barking dog.
2736. kulikkiṟa nāy vēṭṭi piṭikkumā?
Will a barking dog avil in hunting?
2737. kulikkiṟa nāyiṉ vāyilē kōlik koṭuttāl ūr eṅkum koṇṭōṭik kulikkum.
If a staff be put into the mouth of a noisy dog, it will carry it
through the village barking.
2738. kuliyāta nāy kutiṅkālik kaṭikkum.
A silent dog will bite the heels.
2739. kuḻanti paṭṭiṉiyum kōyil paṭṭiṉiyum illi.
Infants and temple servants do not suffer from hunger.
2740. kuḻantikkum nāykkum kuṭipōkac cantōṣam.
Children and dogs are pleased with a change of place.
2741. kuḻantiyum teyvamum koṇṭāṭiṉa iṭattilē.
Children and gods best agree with their admirers.
2742. kuḻantik kāyccalum kuṇṭaṉi kāyccalum pollātu.
The fever of children and the spite of a slanderer are bad.
2743. kuḻanti tūṅkukiṟatu ellām ammiyārukku ilāpam.
The sleep of a child is advantageous to the mother.
2744. kuḻantiyiṉ tēkampōl.
Tender as the body of aninfant.
2745. kuḻantāy kuḻiyil amiḻntātē.
My child, do not sink into the pit.
2746.kuḻappaṭi kaṇṭu tirikaṭal ēṅkumā ?
Has the stormy weather set the whole sea in motion?
2747. kuḻampup pāl kuṭikkavum kumarā kaṇṭavaliyā?
O my youthful son, is your throat pined by drinking thick milk?
2748. kuḻiyiṟ payiri eṭuttuk kūrimēl iṭalāmā?
Is it right to trin a parasite to the roof?
2749.kuḻiyiṟ piḷḷiyi nari cuṟṟukiṟāppōlē.
Likea jackal going round the grave of a child.
2750. kuḷattik kalakkip paruntukku iri iṭalāmā?
Is it proper to agitate the tank to supply prey for kites?
2751. kuḷattukku maḻi kuntāṇiyā?
Are rin drops the weight of a mortar to a tank?
2752. kuḷattiṟ pōṭṭuk kiṇaṟṟil tēṭalāmā ?
Having put it into the tank, do you seek it in the well?
2753. kuḷattōṭu kōpittuk kāl kaḻuvātavaṉ pōl.
Like the man who would not wash his feet in the tank becuse he was
angry with it.
2754. kuḷap paṭi nīr iṟittālum kaṭaṟ paḷḷam nirampumā?
Will the hollowof the sea be filled by drining a tank down to the
lowest step?
2755. kuḷam uṭintu pōkumpōtu muṟi vītamā?
When a tank bursts, do they dispute about turns?
2756. kuḷam kākkiṟavaṉ taṇṇīrik kuṭiyāṉō ?
Will not he who watches the tank drink?
2757. kuḷam vaṟṟiyum muṟi vītam uṇṭā ?
Why dispute about your turn for drawing water seeing that the tank is
dried up?
2758. kuḷam veṭṭu muṉṉē mutali kuṭi irukkumā?
Before the tank is dug will the alligator go to dwell therein ?
2759. kuḷavik kūṭṭik kōlāl kulittāṟpōla.
Like poking a wasp's nest with a stick.
2760. kuḷavik kūṭṭilē kallu viṭṭu eṟikiṟatā?
What! throw a stone at a wasp's nest?
2761. kuḷavikkup paccip puḻu piḷḷi.
A green caterpillar is the offspring of a wasp.
2762. kuḷavi puḻuvit taṉ niṟam ākkuvatu pōla.
Like a wasp changing worms to its own colour.
2763. kuḷikkap pōyc cēṟṟip pūcikkoṇṭatu pōla.
As one smeared himself with mud after bathing.
2764. kuḷirnta koḷḷiyāy iruntu kuṭiyik keṭukkalāmā?
Pretending to be an extinguished firebrand, is it proper to destroy the
house ?
2765. kuḷḷap pārppāṉ paḷḷattil viḻuntāṉ taṇṭu eṭu taṭi eṭu.
